\mathrm{div}_{g_{o}}h^{4}_{\zeta}&=O(|\zeta|^{2}r_{o}^{-3-}),\\
{h}^{4}_{\zeta}&=H^{4}_{\zeta}+O(|\zeta|^{2}r_{o}^{-2-})\text{ at the singular point }.\end{aligned}\right.

Note 2.32
.

Here and in the rest of the article the notation
\mathrm{div}_{g_{o}}h^{4}_{\zeta}=O(|\zeta|^{2}r_{o}^{-3-})
means that for any
c>0
, there exists
C>0
such that
|\mathrm{div}_{g_{o}}h^{4}_{\zeta}|\leq C|\zeta|^{2}r_{o}^{-3-c}
, see also the Appendix
E
.

Proof.

The analysis of
[
Biq13
, Section 10]
provides the existence of a tensor
{h}^{4}_{\zeta}
with
(\Delta_{L,g_{o}}+2\Lambda){h}^{4}_{\zeta}=0
as well as
{h}^{4}_{\zeta}=H^{4}_{\zeta}+O(|\zeta|^{2}r_{o}^{-2-})
. It may be obtained more concretely as follows.

Consider
\chi
to be a cut-off function supported close to the singular point of
M_{o}
, and
(\chi H^{4}_{\zeta})^{0}_{g_{o}}
, the traceless part of
H^{4}_{\zeta}
for the metric
g_{o}
. It satisfies
(\chi H^{4}_{\zeta})^{0}_{g_{o}}=H^{4}_{\zeta}+O(|\zeta|^{2}r_{o}^{-2})
at the singular point as well as

(\Delta_{L,g_{o}}+2\Lambda)(\chi H^{4}_{\zeta})^{0}_{g_{o}}=O(|\zeta|^{2}r_{o}^{-4}),

where the right-hand side is
g_{o}
-traceless since
(\Delta_{L,g_{o}}+2\Lambda)
preserves traceless symmetric
2
-tensors.

We may now use the analysis of
[
Biq13
, Section 10]
to find a traceless symmetric
2
-tensor
h^{\prime}=O(|\zeta|^{2}r_{o}^{-2-})
such that
(\Delta_{L,g_{o}}+2\Lambda)((\chi H^{4}_{\zeta})^{0}_{g_{o}}+h^{\prime})=0
.
∎

3.
First approximations

We now present a curve of metrics that will be an approximate solution to the normalized Ricci flow which we will perturb into an actual Ricci flow in Section
8.4
. Note that all of the gluing constructions and estimates are performed in the case of the Eguchi-Hanson metric, but easily extends to the case of general Ricci-flat ALE metrics.

3.1.
Definition of the approximate metric

Let
(M_{o}^{4},g_{o})
be a
4
-dimensional Einstein orbifold. The Einstein constant is denoted by
\Lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, i.e.
\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g_{o})=\Lambda g_{o}
on the regular part of
M_{o}
. We assume from now on that
M_{o}
has one single orbifold singularity modeled on
\mathbb{R}^{4}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
at a point
p_{o}
. A finite number of singularities can be handled analogously.

In the coordinates of Remark
2.2
, at the point
p_{o}
, for
r_{o}<\delta_{0}
,

(3.1)

g_{o}=dr_{o}^{2}+r_{o}^{2}g_{\mathbb{S}^{3}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}}+H_{2}(r_{o})+O(r_{o}^{4}).

Remark 3.1
.

Notice that the previous estimate (
3.1
) is
O(r_{o}^{3})
if the metric is
\Gamma
-invariant for
\Gamma
a finite subgroup of
\operatorname{SU}(2)
. In order to reach the same degree of precision with arbitrary hyperkähler ALE metrics, we will need to extend the cubic terms of the expansion in Section
11
.

From now on,
r
will denote any positive smooth extension of the radial coordinate on
(T^{*}\mathbb{S}^{2}=:N,\mathbf{eh}_{\zeta}=:g_{\zeta})
as in (
2.12
) (which is independent of
\zeta
). In such coordinates, thanks to Proposition
2.7
for
r>1000\sqrt{|\zeta|}
we have

(3.2)

\mathbf{eh}_{\zeta}=g_{\zeta}=dr^{2}+r^{2}g_{\mathbb{S}^{3}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}}+|\zeta|^{2}H^{4}(r_{o})+O(|\zeta|^{4}r^{-8}),

Again, the above estimate (
3.2
) is
O(|\zeta|^{\frac{5}{2}}r^{-5})
if the metric is
\Gamma
-invariant for
\Gamma
a finite subgroup of
\operatorname{SU}(2)
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
and
\delta>0
be small parameters such that to be determined later. We define the following subsets:

\begin{split}N^{\delta}&:=\{r<2\delta^{-1}\}\subset N,\\
M_{o}^{\delta}&:=\{r_{o}>\delta\}\subset M_{o}.\end{split}

Let
M^{\varepsilon,\delta}=M
be the manifold obtained as the union of
N^{\varepsilon/\delta}
and
M_{o}^{\delta/2}
along the identification of the annuli of the asymptotic cones through
1000\sqrt{|\zeta|}<r=r_{o}<\delta_{0}
in (
3.1
) and (
3.2
), and the identity on
{\mathbb{S}^{3}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
.

Let
\chi:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,1]
be an even smooth cut-off function satisfying
\chi\equiv 1
on
(-\infty,1/2]
and
\chi\equiv 0
on
[2,+\infty)
. Let
\chi_{\delta(t)}:=\chi(r_{o}/\delta)
. The first approximation background metric is defined as:

(3.3)

\tilde{g}:=\chi_{\delta}(g_{\zeta}+h_{2,\zeta})+(1-\chi_{\delta})\left(g_{o}+h_{\zeta}^{4}\right),

where
g_{\zeta}:=\mathbf{eh}_{\zeta}
is defined in (
2.14
), where
h_{2,\zeta}
is given by Proposition
2.13
and where
h^{4}_{\zeta}
is ensured by Proposition
2.31
.

Remark 3.2
.

In the sequel, when dealing with ancient flows,
\varepsilon
and
\delta
are smooth functions of time
t\in(-\infty,t_{0})
for
t_{0}
negative. In that case, we denote
(\tilde{g}(t))_{t<t_{0}}
to be the curve of metrics defined for each time
t<t_{0}
by
\tilde{g}
with parameters
\varepsilon(t)
and
\delta(t)
. Similarly, when dealing with immortal flows, we will define
\tilde{g}(t)
in the same way for
t\in(t_{0},+\infty)
for
t_{0}>0
. Many of our estimates will be proved for both ancient and immortal flows at the same time, since up to flipping the sign of
t
, the proofs are exactly the same: ODE’s are time-reversible. We will deal with ancient and immortal flows separately when the proofs are substantially different: parabolic flows are not time-reversible.

From now on, define

\varepsilon_{0}(t):=\exp\left\{\frac{\Lambda_{0}}{2}t\right\},\quad t\leq T,\quad\text{respectively $\varepsilon_{0}(t):=\exp\left\{-\frac{\Lambda_{0}}{2}t\right\},\quad t\geq T,$}

for some
\Lambda_{0}>0
to be specified in Section
8
and we will assume the following conditions:

(
\mathcal{H}_{0}
)

\begin{split}&C^{-1}\varepsilon_{0}(t)\leq|\zeta(t)|\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t),\quad|\dot{\zeta}(t)|+[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon_{0}(t)}\leq C|\zeta(t)|,\\
&|\delta^{\prime}(t)|\leq C\delta(t)\quad\text{and}\quad C^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\delta}\leq\delta(t)\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{\delta},\quad\delta\in\left(\frac{5}{9}\,,\,\frac{3-\sigma}{5-\sigma}\right),\end{split}

for some positive constant
C
and all
t\leq T
in the ancient case, and for all
t\geq T
for immortal flows and some fixed
\sigma\in(0,1/2)
. Here the semi-norm
[\,\cdot\,]_{\alpha,t,r}
is defined in Definition
D.1
.
In particular, since
\varepsilon(t)^{2}=|\zeta(t)|
, one gets from (

H
0

) that

|\varepsilon^{\prime}(t)|+[\varepsilon^{\prime}]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon_{0}(t)}\leq C\varepsilon(t).

Finally, define the parabolic weight:

\rho(x,t):=\varepsilon(t)+r_{o}(x),\quad(x,t)\in M\times(-\infty,T],\quad\text{respectively $(x,t)\in M\times[T,+\infty)$.}

Note 3.3
.

The intervals
t\leqslant T
and
t\geqslant T
respectively refer to the ancient and immortal cases.

From now on, we will omit the spatial dependency of the weight function defined above but we will most of the time keep the time dependency and will write
\rho(t)
to denote the function
\rho(\cdot,t)
.

The curve
(\tilde{g}(t))_{t\,\leq\,T}
satisfies the following estimates:

Lemma 3.4
.

For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
C_{k}>0
such that on
\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}
,

\left|\nabla^{g_{o},\,k}\left(\tilde{g}(t)-\left(g_{\zeta(t)}+h_{2,\zeta(t)}\right)\right)\right|_{g_{o}}\leq C_{k}\,\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-5-k}.

In particular, for all
k\geq 0
, on
\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}
,

\begin{split}\left|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),\,k}\left(\left(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta(t)}\right)-\left(g_{\zeta(t)}+h_{2,\zeta(t)}\right)\right)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq C_{k}\,\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-5-k},\\
|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),\,k}h_{2,\zeta(t)}|_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq C_{k}\,\delta(t)^{2-k}.\end{split}

Finally, for each
k\geq 0
, there exists
C_{k}>0
such that on
M\times(-\infty,T]
,

\begin{split}\rho(t)^{2+k}|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),\,k}\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\leq C_{k}.\end{split}

Remark 3.5
.

In the first two estimates of Lemma
3.4
, one gets an error
O(r_{o}^{3-k})
if
g_{o}=\mathbf{e}+H_{2}+O(r_{o}^{3})
if the group of the singularity is not even. This requires extending cubic terms of the orbifold metric to the ALE metric for general Ricci-flat ALE metrics in Section
11
.

Proof.

Since by [(
2.13
), Proposition
2.7
] and Proposition
2.31
,

\begin{split}g_{\zeta(t)}&=\mathbf{e}+H^{4}_{\zeta(t)}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\rho(t)^{-5}),\\
h_{\zeta(t)}^{4}&=H^{4}_{\zeta(t)}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-2^{-}}),\end{split}

one has,

\begin{split}g_{\zeta(t)}&=\mathbf{e}+h_{\zeta(t)}^{4}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\rho(t)^{-5}+\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-2^{-}}).\end{split}

Therefore, by definition of
\tilde{g}(t)
given in (
3.3
) together with Proposition
2.13
, the following holds on
\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}
,

\begin{split}\left|\tilde{g}(t)-\left(g_{\zeta(t)}+h_{2,\zeta(t)}\right)\right|_{\mathbf{e}}&=(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\left|\left(g_{0}+h^{4}_{\zeta(t)}-\left(g_{\zeta(t)}+h_{2,\zeta(t)}\right)\right)\right|_{\mathbf{e}}\\
&\leq(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\Bigg|\mathbf{e}+H_{2}+O(\rho(t)^{4})+H^{4}_{\zeta(t)}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-2^{-}})\\
&\quad-\left(\mathbf{e}+H^{4}_{\zeta(t)}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\rho(t)^{-5})+H_{2}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\rho(t)^{-2^{-}})\right)\Bigg|_{\mathbf{e}}\\
&=O(\delta(t)^{4})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2^{-}})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-5})\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-5}),\end{split}

where we have used
\delta>5/9
in the last line. One imposes
\delta<(3-\sigma)/(5-\sigma)<5/7
to get a better error compared to
|h_{2,\zeta(t)}|_{g_{\zeta(t)}}=O(\rho(t)^{2})
.

Higher covariant derivatives with respect to
g_{o}
(and then with respect to
\tilde{g}(t)
) can be obtained similarly.

The last estimate on the covariant derivatives of
h_{2,\zeta(t)}
follows from Proposition
2.13
, the previous estimate and the schematic formula for two Riemannian metrics
g_{i}
,
i=1,2
and a tensor
T
:

(\nabla^{g_{2}}-\nabla^{g_{1}})T=g_{2}^{-1}\ast T\ast\nabla^{g_{1}}(g_{2}-g_{1})
.

As for the curvature estimates, observe that on the set
\{r_{o}>2\delta(t)\}
,
\tilde{g}(t)=g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta(t)}
. Since
g_{o}
is time independent, its curvature tensor stays bounded as
t
tends to
-\infty
. Lemma
C.1
together with Proposition
2.31
give for
t\leq T
:

\begin{split}|\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{o}+h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})|_{g_{o}}&\leq|\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{o})|_{g_{o}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-6},\end{split}

which is bounded since
\delta<(3-\sigma)/(5-\sigma)<2/3.

On the set
\{r_{o}<\delta(t)/2\}
,
\tilde{g}(t)=g_{\zeta(t)}+h_{2,\zeta(t)}
so that on the one hand,

\begin{split}|\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{\zeta(t)})|_{g_{\zeta(t)}}&=|\zeta(t)|^{-1}(s_{\zeta(t)})_{*}|\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g)|_{g}\leq|\zeta(t)|^{-1}(s_{\zeta(t)})_{*}\left(\frac{C}{(1+r_{b})^{4}}\right)\\
&\leq\frac{C\varepsilon(t)^{2}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{4}}\leq\frac{C}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{2}},\end{split}

and on the other hand, Lemma
C.1
gives for
t\leq T
:

\begin{split}|\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{\zeta(t)}+h_{2,\zeta(t)})-\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{\zeta(t)})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq C\left(|\nabla^{g_{\zeta(t)},2}h_{2,\zeta(t)}|_{\tilde{g}(t)}+|\nabla^{g_{\zeta(t)}}h_{2,\zeta(t)}|_{\tilde{g}(t)}^{2}\right)\leq C.\end{split}

Here we have used Proposition
2.13
in the last line and
C
is a positive constant that may vary from line to line. By invoking Lemma
C.1
twice on the regions
{\{r_{o}<3\delta(t)/2\}}
and
{\{r_{o}>\delta(t)\}}
together with the convergence rates obtained previously on
\tilde{g}(t)-(g_{\zeta(t)}+h_{2,\zeta(t)})
, one gets the expected estimate on
|\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))|_{\tilde{g}(t)}.
The corresponding estimates on the covariant derivatives of
\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))
can be obtained similarly.

∎

The next lemma measures up to first order how far the approximation
\tilde{g}(t)
is from being Einstein.

Lemma 3.6
.

For all
k\geq 0
, there exists
C_{k}>0
such that for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}&\left|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),\,k}\left(-\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))+\Lambda\tilde{g}(t)-\chi_{\delta(t)}d_{\zeta(t)}g(\mathbf{R}^{+}_{p}(\zeta(t)))+(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o})\right)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\leq\\
&C_{k}\,\rho(t)^{2-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+C_{k}\,\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-7-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+C_{k}\,\varepsilon(t)^{8}\rho(t)^{-10-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{2\delta(t)\,\leq\,r_{o}\}}.\end{split}

Remark 3.7
.

This estimate is reminiscent of
[
Biq13
, Lemma
4.2
]
. However, one has to refine the contribution of the obstruction tensor
-\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))+\Lambda\tilde{g}(t)
to be an exact Einstein metric on the orbifold here. The
L^{2}
-estimates in Section
4
explains the need for this refinement. This is particularly true in the proofs of Propositions
4.1
and
4.5
.

Proof.

Recall the following property shared by the linearization of
-\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits
at a metric
g
along a variation
h
such that
|h|_{g}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, as recalled in Lemma
C.2
:

(3.4)

\left|\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g+h)-\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g)-d_{g}\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(h)\right|_{g}\leq C\left(|h|_{g}|\nabla^{g,2}h|_{g}+|\nabla^{g}h|^{2}_{g}+|\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g)|_{g}|h|^{2}_{g}\right),

for some positive constant
C
depending on the dimension only. In particular, on the region where
\chi_{\delta(t)}\equiv 1
, one gets with the help of (
3.4
) and Lemma
3.4
:

\begin{split}&\left|-\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))+\Lambda\tilde{g}(t)-\chi_{\delta(t)}d_{\zeta(t)}g(\mathbf{R}^{+}_{p}(\zeta(t)))\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&=\left|-\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g_{\zeta(t)}+h_{2,\zeta(t)})+\Lambda(g_{\zeta(t)}+h_{2,\zeta(t)})-d_{\zeta(t)}g(\mathbf{R}^{+}_{p}(\zeta(t)))\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&\leq\left|-\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g_{\zeta(t)})-d_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(h_{2,\zeta(t)})+\Lambda(g_{\zeta(t)}+h_{2,\zeta(t)})-d_{\zeta(t)}g(\mathbf{R}^{+}_{p}(\zeta(t)))\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}+Cr_{o}^{2}\\
&\leq Cr_{o}^{2}.\end{split}

Here we have used the Ricci flatness of
g_{\zeta(t)}
in the last line together with the equation satisfied by
h_{2,\zeta(t)}
imposed in Proposition
2.13
and the fact that
\nabla^{g_{\zeta(t)},\,k}h_{2,\zeta(t)}=O((\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{2-k})
.

Now, estimates on higher derivatives are obtained by linearizing as above together with Lemma
3.4
.

In the region
\{\delta(t)/4\,<\,r_{o}\,<\,\delta(t)\}
, we linearize the Ricci tensor around the metric
g_{\zeta(t)}
with the help of Lemma
3.4
: if
\tilde{h}(t):=\tilde{g}(t)-g_{\zeta(t)}
then (
3.4
) and Proposition
2.31
imply,

\begin{split}-\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))+\Lambda\tilde{g}(t)&=-\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g_{\zeta(t)})+\Lambda g_{\zeta(t)}+\Lambda\tilde{h}(t)-d_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{h}(t))+O(\delta(t)^{2})\\
&=\Lambda g_{\zeta(t)}-d_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(h_{2,\zeta(t)})-d_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{h}(t)-h_{2,\zeta(t)})+O(\delta(t)^{2})\\
&=-d_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{h}(t)-h_{2,\zeta(t)})+O(\delta(t)^{2})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4})\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-7})+O(\delta(t)^{2})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4})=O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-7}).\end{split}

Here we have invoked
2/3>\delta>5/9
thanks to (

H
0

) and we have used the fact that
h_{2,\zeta(t)}
is in Bianchi gauge together with the fact that
|\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))|_{g_{\zeta(t)}}=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-4})
in the third equality.

Now, on the region
\{r_{o}>\delta(t)\}
, we linearize the Ricci tensor around the metric
g_{o}
with the help of Lemma
3.4
: if
\tilde{h}(t):=\tilde{g}(t)-g_{o}
, since

d_{g_{o}}(2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits-2\Lambda)(h^{4}_{\zeta(t)})=\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o})=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-4}),

thanks to Proposition
2.31
, then (
3.4
) implies,

\begin{split}-\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))+\Lambda\tilde{g}(t)&=-\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g_{o})+\Lambda g_{o}-d_{g_{o}}(\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits-\Lambda)(\tilde{h}(t))+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\rho(t)^{-10})\\
&=-d_{g_{o}}(\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits-\Lambda)(\tilde{h}(t)-h^{4}_{\zeta(t)})-\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\rho(t)^{-10})\\
&=-\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-7})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}\\
&\quad+\varepsilon(t)^{8}\rho(t)^{-10}\mathbbm{1}_{\{2\delta(t)\,\leq\,r_{o}\}}.\end{split}

Here we have invoked
1/3<5/9<\delta<2/3
thanks to (

H
0

).

The equivalent estimate is obtained thanks to the combination of the previously established estimate together with Remark
2.14
and (

H
0

).
∎

The last lemma of this section reveals the obstruction of the approximate solution
(\tilde{g}(t))_{t\,\leq\,T}
to be an exact solution to the normalized Ricci flow:

Lemma 3.8
.

There exists
C>0
such that the first time variation of
\tilde{g}(t)
satisfies:

\begin{split}&\left|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)-\chi_{\delta(t)}d_{\zeta(t)}g(\dot{\zeta}(t))-(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})d_{\zeta(t)}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\leq\\
&C\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\rho(t)^{-2^{-}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-5}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}},\end{split}

and for each
k\geq 1
, there exists
C_{k}>0
such that for
t\leq T
,

\left|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),\,k}\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\leq C_{k}\,\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-4-k}.

Moreover,

\begin{split}\left|\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t))\right|&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\rho(t)^{-2^{-}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-5}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\rho(t)^{-8}\mathbbm{1}_{\{2\delta(t)\,\leq\,r_{o}\}}.\end{split}

The obstruction to be a solution to the renormalized Ricci flow can be estimated as follows for all
k\geq 0
:

\begin{split}\Bigg|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)\,,k}&\Bigg(-\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)-2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))+2\Lambda\tilde{g}(t)+\chi_{\delta(t)}d_{\zeta(t)}g\left(\dot{\zeta}(t)-2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}(\zeta(t))\right)\\
&+(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\left(d_{\zeta(t)}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})+\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o})\right)\Bigg)\Bigg|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&\leq C\left(\rho(t)^{2-k}+\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\rho(t)^{-2^{-}-k}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-7-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}\\
&\quad+C_{k}\,\varepsilon(t)^{8}\rho(t)^{-10-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{2\delta(t)\,\leq\,r_{o}\}}.\end{split}

Proof.

By a straightforward differentiation in time, we see that:

(3.5)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)&=\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}\frac{r_{o}\delta^{\prime}(t)}{\delta(t)^{2}}\left(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta(t)}-g_{\zeta(t)}-h_{2,\zeta(t)}\right)+\chi_{\delta(t)}\partial_{t}\left(g_{\zeta(t)}+h_{2,\zeta(t)}\right)+(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\partial_{t}h^{4}_{\zeta(t)}\\
&=\underbrace{\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-5})}_{\text{supported in $\{\delta(t)/2\leq r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}+\chi_{\delta(t)}\partial_{t}\left(g_{\zeta(t)}+h_{2,\zeta(t)}\right)+(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\partial_{t}h^{4}_{\zeta(t)},\end{split}

where we have used Lemma
3.4
in the last line and the constraint
|\delta^{\prime}(t)|\leq C\delta(t)
for all
t
in the interval of interest thanks to (

H
0

).

Now, by definition of the differential,
\partial_{t}g_{\zeta(t)}=d_{\zeta(t)}g(\dot{\zeta}(t))
and thanks to Proposition
A.16
,
|\partial_{t}h_{2,\zeta(t)}|_{g_{\zeta(t)}}=O(|\dot{\zeta}(t)|\varepsilon(t)^{2^{-}}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2^{-}})=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2^{-}}),
thanks to (

H
0

). This gives the first estimate on
\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)
.

The higher covariant derivatives of
\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)
can be obtained by differentiating the above expression and by invoking Proposition
A.16
. Notice here that the terms supported on the annulus are either of the form
O(\delta(t)^{4-k})
or
O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-k})
since
|\partial_{t}h^{4}_{\zeta(t)}|_{g_{\zeta(t)}}=O(|\dot{\zeta}(t)|\varepsilon(t)^{2}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-4})=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4})
,
h^{4}_{\zeta(t)}
depending quadratically on
|\zeta(t)|
. Condition (

H
0

) ensures that it is
O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-k})
.

As for the trace of
\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)
, it suffices to estimate
\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\left(\chi_{\delta(t)}d_{\zeta(t)}g(\dot{\zeta}(t))\right).
Now, since
d_{\zeta(t)}g(\dot{\zeta}(t))
lies in the kernel of the Lichnerowicz operator associated to the Ricci flat metric
g_{\zeta(t)}
,
\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\left(d_{\zeta(t)}g(\dot{\zeta}(t))\right)=0
thanks to Subsection
2.2.2
. Therefore, Lemma
3.4
ensures that

\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\left(\chi_{\delta(t)}d_{\zeta(t)}g(\dot{\zeta}(t))\right)=\chi_{\delta(t)}d_{\zeta(t)}g(\dot{\zeta}(t))\ast\left(\tilde{g}(t)-g_{\zeta(t)}\right)=O\left(|\dot{\zeta}(t)|\varepsilon(t)^{2}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2}\right),

by invoking (

H
0

) once more. Proposition
2.31
ensures that
\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}\left(d_{\zeta(t)}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})\right)=0
so that
\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}((1-\chi_{\delta(t)})d_{\zeta(t)}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta}))=O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\rho(t)^{-8})
on
\{r_{o}\,\geq\,\delta(t)\}
. This gives the expected estimate on
\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)).

As for the third estimate, it is a combination of Lemma
3.6
and the previous estimate (
3.5
).
∎

3.2.
Definition of the approximate kernel: rescalings and rotations

We define
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
as the localized part of the tensor
\mathbf{o}_{i}
appropriately rescaled:

(3.6)

\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t):=\chi_{\delta(t)}\cdot\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t)).

This definition follows the work of Biquard
[
Biq13
]
and that of Morteza-Viaclovsky
[
MV20
]
.

Remark 3.9
.

Alternatively, as in
[
BK17
]
, we could have defined the tensor
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{1}(t)
, for
t\leq T
, to be the trace-free part of the tensor
\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\tilde{g}
at
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(t)
, i.e. the tensor:

\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\tilde{g}-\frac{1}{4}\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\left(\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\tilde{g}\right)\tilde{g}(t).

The advantage of the approximate kernel as defined in
[
BK17
]
is that it is not only defined on a curve but also on the whole moduli space of possible gluings (accounting for the deformations of the ALE spaces and the orbifold) where the curve
(\tilde{g}(t))_{t}
lives. Our choice in this paper seems to be handier for direct estimates whereas theirs seems to be better suited for a global understanding of the dynamics of the Ricci flow in the moduli space of glued metrics.

We make precise the fact that the tensors
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
can be interpreted as being in the approximate kernel of the Lichnerowicz Laplacian associated to
\tilde{g}(t)
for
t\leq T
in the next lemma:

Lemma 3.10
.

For each
k\geq 0
, there exists
C_{k}>0
such that for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}|\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)|&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}},\\
\left|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)\,k}\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\right|&\leq C_{k}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-4-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+C_{k}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-6-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}},\\
\left|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),\,k}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq C_{k}\,\chi_{\delta(t)}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-4-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+C_{k}\,\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}},\\
\left|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),\,k}\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq C_{k}\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\rho(t)^{-4^{-}-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+C_{k}\,\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}},\\
\left|\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\right)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-3}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-5}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}},\\
\left|\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}(\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t))\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{8^{-}}\rho(t)^{-7^{-}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-5}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}.\end{split}

Proof.

Observe that since
\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))=0
(see Section
2.2.2
),

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)&=\chi_{\delta(t)}\left(\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}-\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\right)\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))=\chi_{\delta(t)}O(r_{o}^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-4})\\
&=\chi_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2}),\end{split}

where we have used Lemma
3.4
in the penultimate estimate.

On
\{r_{o}\leq 3\delta(t)/2\},
since
\Delta_{L,g_{\zeta(t)}}\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))=0
,

\begin{split}\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\mathbf{o}_{i}(t)&=\Delta_{L,g_{\zeta(t)}}\mathbf{o}_{i}(t)+\sum_{k=0}^{2}\nabla^{g_{\zeta(t)},\,k}(\tilde{g}(t)-g_{\zeta(t)})\ast\nabla^{g_{\zeta(t)},2-k}\mathbf{o}_{i}(t)\\
&=\sum_{k=0}^{2}O\Big(\Big((\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{2-k}+\underbrace{O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-k})}_{\text{with support in $\{\delta(t)/2\leq r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}\Big)\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-4-(2-k)}\Big)\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-4})+\underbrace{O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-6})}_{\text{with support in $\{\delta(t)/2\leq r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}.\end{split}

Here we have invoked Lemma
3.4
once more. Note that the estimate
O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-4})
is optimal: this is exactly the scale given by the “small eigenvalues” of
\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}
, see
[
NO24
]
.

By taking account for the cut-off function
\chi_{\delta(t)}
,

\begin{split}\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)&=\Delta_{\tilde{g}(t)}\chi_{\delta(t)}\cdot\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))+2\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\chi_{\delta(t)}}\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))+\chi_{\delta(t)}\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\\
&=\underbrace{O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-6})}_{\text{with support in $\{\delta(t)/2\leq r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}+\chi_{\delta(t)}\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t)).\end{split}

This last estimate combined with the penultimate one leads to the first desired estimate on
\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
on
\{r_{o}\leq 3\delta(t)/2\}
. As for the corresponding estimate on
\{r_{o}\geq\delta(t)\},
a similar reasoning by linearizing with respect to
g_{o}
or the Euclidean metric
\mathbf{e}
leads to the desired decay.

The estimate holding on the covariant derivatives of
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
and those of
\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
follows from the same reasoning.

Regarding the time derivative of
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
: thanks to Proposition
A.16
, we have

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)&=\chi_{\delta(t)}\partial_{t}\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))-\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}r_{o}\delta^{\prime}(t)\delta(t)^{-2}\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\\
&=\chi_{\delta(t)}O(|\dot{\zeta}(t)|\varepsilon(t)^{2^{-}}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-4^{-}})+\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4}).\end{split}

Here we have used the assumption (

H
0

) on
|\dot{\zeta}(t)|
and
\delta^{\prime}(t)
in the last line. The estimate holding on the covariant derivatives of
\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
follows from the same reasoning together with Proposition
A.16
.

Finally, observe that since
\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))=0
by Section
2.2.2
, we have the following additional improvement:

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\right)&=\chi_{\delta(t)}\left(\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}-\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\right)\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))+\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))(\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\chi_{\delta(t)},\cdot)\\
&=\chi_{\delta(t)}\tilde{g}(t)^{-1}\ast g_{\zeta(t)}^{-1}\ast\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}(\tilde{g}(t)-g_{\zeta(t)})\ast\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\\
&\quad+\chi_{\delta(t)}\tilde{g}(t)^{-1}\ast g_{\zeta(t)}^{-1}\ast(\tilde{g}(t)-g_{\zeta(t)})\ast\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))+\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-5})\\
&=\chi_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-3})+\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-5}).\end{split}

Here we have used Lemma
3.4
in the last line. A similar (but tedious) reasoning leads to the corresponding estimate on the divergence of the time derivative
\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
.
∎

3.3.
Definition of the approximate kernel: conformal vector fields

In this section, we define tensors that will only be used when constructing ancient Ricci flows coming out of spherical orbifolds.

Let
v_{o}
be an eigenfunction on the orbifold
M_{o}
associated to the eigenvalue
-4=-\Lambda-1
, i.e.
\Delta_{g_{o}}v_{o}=-(-\Lambda+1)v_{o}
. In particular,
v_{o}
is the restriction of the
x_{5}
coordinate to the orbifold in the description of Example
2.18
. In coordinates where
g=d\theta^{2}+\sin(\theta)^{2}g_{\mathbb{S}^{3}/\Gamma}
, one has
v_{o}(\theta)=\cos(\theta)
.

In particular, the Lie derivative satisfies
\mathcal{L}_{\nabla^{g_{o}}v_{o}}g_{o}=\frac{1}{4}\Delta_{g_{o}}v_{o}g_{o}=-v_{o}g_{o}.
The eigenfunction
v_{0}
is normalized so that the following asymptotic expansion close to the singular point
o\in M_{o}
holds:

(3.7)

\nabla^{g_{o},\,k}v_{0}=\nabla^{g_{o},\,k}\left(1-\frac{1}{2}r_{o}^{2}\right)+O_{g_{o}}(r_{o}^{4-k}),\quad k\geq 0.

Let us define the approximate eigenfunction
\tilde{v}(t)
for
t<0
as follows:

\tilde{v}(t):=(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\cdot v_{o}+\chi_{\delta(t)}\cdot v_{g_{\zeta(t)}}(t),\quad\text{on $M$}.

Here the function
v_{g_{\zeta(t)}}
is defined to match and extend
v_{o}
on
N
by:

v_{g_{\zeta(t)}}(t):=1-\varepsilon^{2}(t)(s_{\zeta(t)})_{\ast}u,

where
u
satisfies
\Delta_{g}u=4
,
u=\frac{1}{2}r^{2}+o((1+r)^{-2})
together with derivatives as explained in Section
2.3
. In order to lighten the notations, we introduce the function
v(t):=v_{g_{\zeta(t)}}(t)
so that:

\tilde{v}(t)=(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\cdot v_{o}+\chi_{\delta(t)}\cdot v(t),\quad\text{on $M$}.

The associated Lie derivative will be denoted by
\tilde{c}(t)
(where the letter
c
stands for conformal) for
t\leq 0
and defined as:

\tilde{c}(t):=\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)}\tilde{g}(t)=\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),\,2}\tilde{v}(t),\quad\text{on $M$}.

Let us summarize the properties of
\tilde{c}(t)
in the following lemma:

Lemma 3.11
.

For each
k\geq 0
, there exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
, the function
\tilde{v}(t)
and the tensor
\tilde{c}(t)
satisfy:

(1)

On
M
,

\begin{split}\left|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),\,k}\left(\tilde{v}(t)-1\right)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq C_{k}\rho(t)^{2-k},\\
\left|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),\,k}\left(\Delta_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)+(\Lambda+1)\tilde{v}(t)\right)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq C_{k}\rho(t)^{2-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+C_{k}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-4-k}),\\
|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),\,k}\left(\partial_{t}\tilde{v}(t)\right)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq C_{k}\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\rho(t)^{-2^{-}-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+C_{k}\delta(t)^{4-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}.\end{split}

(2)

On
M
,

\begin{split}\left|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),\,k}\left(\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{c}(t)+(\Lambda+1)\,\tilde{c}(t)\right)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq C_{k}\rho(t)^{-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+C_{k}\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-7-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}\\
&\quad+C_{k}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-4-k}.\end{split}

(3)

On
M
, for each
k\geq 0
,

\begin{split}|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),\,k}\partial_{t}\tilde{c}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\leq C_{k}\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\rho(t)^{-4^{-}-k}+C_{k}\delta(t)^{2-k}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}.\end{split}

Proof.

The first estimate is straightforward since by definition of
v(t)
,

(3.8)

\begin{split}\tilde{v}(t)-v_{o}&=\chi_{\delta(t)}\,\left(v(t)-v_{o}\right)\\
&=\chi_{\delta(t)}\,O(r_{o}^{4}+\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2}).\end{split}

Covariant derivatives of the difference follows by differentiating the previous estimate on the basis of (
3.7
) and Section
2.3
.

Since
\Delta_{g_{o}}v_{o}+(\Lambda+1)v_{o}=0
and thanks to Lemma
3.4
and (
3.8
),

\begin{split}\Delta_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)+(\Lambda+1)\tilde{v}(t)&=\left(\Delta_{\tilde{g}(t)}-\Delta_{g_{o}}\right)\tilde{v}(t)+\Delta_{g_{o}}(\tilde{v}(t)-v_{o})+(\Lambda+1)\left(\tilde{v}(t)-v_{o}\right)\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-4})+O(\rho(t)^{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}},\end{split}

as desired. Covariant derivatives follows by invoking (
3.8
) and Lemma
3.4
once more.

As for the time-derivative of
\tilde{v}(t)
, Proposition
A.16
gives:

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\tilde{v}(t)&=\partial_{t}\left(\chi_{\delta(t)}(v(t)-v_{0})\right)\\
&=\chi_{\delta(t)}\partial_{t}v(t)-\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}r_{o}\delta^{\prime}(t)\delta(t)^{-2}(v(t)-v_{0})\\
&=\chi_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2^{-}})+\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}O\left(r_{o}^{4}+\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2}\right).\end{split}

Similarly, one gets for the covariant derivatives of
\tilde{v}(t)
:

(3.9)

|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),\,k}\left(\partial_{t}\tilde{v}(t)\right)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}=\chi_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2^{-}-k})+\underbrace{O\left(\delta(t)^{4-k}+\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-k}\right)}_{\text{with support in $\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}$}}.

We conclude by invoking (

H
0

) with
\delta<2/3
.

Regarding the second estimate, recall the following commutation identity that can be found for instance in the proof of
[
CLN06
, Chapter 2, Lemma
2.33
]
:

(3.10)

\begin{split}\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),2}\tilde{v}(t)&=\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),2}\Delta_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)+\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))\ast\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)\\
&=\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),2}\Delta_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)+\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}(\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))-\Lambda\tilde{g}(t))\ast\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t),\end{split}

on
M.

By the previous estimate on
\Delta_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)+(\Lambda+1)\tilde{v}(t)
together with its derivatives, we arrive at:

\begin{split}\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),2}\tilde{v}(t)&=-(\Lambda+1)\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),2}\tilde{v}(t)+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-4})+O(\rho(t)^{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}\\
&\quad+\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))\ast\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)\\
\end{split}

Invoking Lemma
3.6
also gives:

\begin{split}&|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}(\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))-\Lambda\tilde{g}(t))\ast\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}=\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-4})+O(\rho(t)^{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-7}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}.\end{split}

The combination of the two previous estimates leads to the desired one.

As for the last estimate, the proof of
[
CLN06
, Chapter 2, Lemma
2.33
]
gives:

\begin{split}|\partial_{t}\tilde{c}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}&=\partial_{t}\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),2}\tilde{v}(t)\\
&=\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),2}\left(\partial_{t}\tilde{v}(t)\right)+\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t))\ast\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)\\
&=O\left(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-4^{-}}\right)+\underbrace{O(\delta(t)^{2})}_{\text{with support in $\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}$}}\end{split}

where we have used Lemma
3.8
together with (
3.9
) in the last line. This ends the proof of the desired estimate as expected for
k=0
. Estimates on higher covariant derivatives of
\partial_{t}\tilde{c}(t)
can be obtained similarly.

∎

Finally, we define the approximate kernel associated to the approximate solution
\tilde{g}(t)
as follows:

Definition 3.12
.

We define the approximate kernel associated to the curve
\tilde{g}(t)
as:

\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t):=\operatorname{vect}((\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t))_{i},\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{c}(t)),\quad t\leq T,\quad\text{respectively $\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t):=\operatorname{vect}((\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t))_{i},\tilde{g}(t)),\quad t\geq T.$}

3.4.
Hölder estimates for the approximate kernel

The following proposition is the first of a series of estimates on the Hölder semi-norms
[\,\cdot\,]_{\alpha,t,r}
as defined in Definition
D.3
of the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
defined in Definition
3.12
together with those of the time-derivative and Lichnerowicz laplacian acting on
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
.

Proposition 3.13
.

There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}&[\partial_{t}\tilde{g}]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{\rho(t)^{4^{-}}}\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right),\\
&[\partial_{t}\tilde{g}\ast\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{8^{-}}}{\rho(t)^{8^{-}}}\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{8^{-}}\delta(t)^{-8^{-}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}},\\
&[\partial_{t}\tilde{g}\ast\tilde{g}]_{\alpha,t,r}+[\partial_{t}\tilde{g}\ast\tilde{c}]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{\rho(t)^{4^{-}}}\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right).\end{split}

Proof.

Thanks to [(
3.5
), Lemma
3.8
], Lemma
3.4
and Lemma
D.2
:

\begin{split}[\partial_{t}\tilde{g}]_{\alpha,t,r}&=\left[\chi^{\prime}_{\delta}\frac{r_{o}\delta^{\prime}}{\delta^{2}}\left(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta}-g_{\zeta}-h_{2,\zeta}\right)+\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}\left(g+h_{2}\right)(\dot{\zeta})+(1-\chi_{\delta})d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\leq Cr^{2}\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}\left|\partial_{t}\left(\chi^{\prime}_{\delta}\frac{r_{o}\delta^{\prime}}{\delta^{2}}\left(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta}-g_{\zeta}-h_{2,\zeta}\right)\right)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&\quad+\left[\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}\left(g+h_{2}\right)(\dot{\zeta})\right]_{\alpha,t,r}+\left[(1-\chi_{\delta})d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\leq\underbrace{Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{-4^{-}}}_{\text{supported in $\{\delta(t)/2\leq r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}+\left[\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}\left(g+h_{2}\right)(\dot{\zeta})\right]_{\alpha,t,r}+\left[(1-\chi_{\delta})d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\leq\underbrace{Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{-4^{-}}}_{\text{supported in $\{\delta(t)/2\leq r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}+\underbrace{\left[d_{\zeta}\left(g+h_{2}\right)(\dot{\zeta})\right]_{\alpha,t,r}}_{\text{supported in $\{r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}+\underbrace{\left[d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})\right]_{\alpha,t,r}}_{\text{supported in $\{r_{o}\geq\delta(t)/2\}$}}.\end{split}

Here we have invoked Proposition
A.16
in the penultimate and the last inequality.

Now, Proposition
A.16
gives:

\begin{split}\left[d_{\zeta}\left(g+h_{2}\right)(\dot{\zeta})\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq Cr^{2}\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\,\leq t}|d^{2}_{\zeta(t^{\prime\prime})}(g+h_{2})(\dot{\zeta}(t^{\prime\prime}),\dot{\zeta}(t^{\prime}))|_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\\
&\quad+C\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{4^{-}}}\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\\
&\leq C\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{4^{-}}}\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right).\end{split}

An analogous estimate holds on
\left[d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})\right]_{\alpha,t,r}
:

\left[d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{4^{-}}}\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right).

This ends the estimate on
[\partial_{t}\tilde{g}]_{\alpha,t,r}
.

By invoking (
D.1
) together with the estimate on
\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}
from Lemma
3.10
:

(3.11)

[\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq\underbrace{Cr^{2}\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{4^{-}}}}_{\text{supported in $\{r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}+\underbrace{Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4}}_{\text{supported in $\{\delta(t)/2\leq r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}.

Similarly, thanks to Lemma
3.8
,

(3.12)

\begin{split}[\tilde{g}]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq r^{2}\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,\tau\,\leq\,t}|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(\tau)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\leq Cr^{2}\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{4}},\end{split}

and Lemma
3.11
gives:

(3.13)

[\tilde{c}]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq Cr^{2}\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{4^{-}}}+\underbrace{Cr^{2}\delta(t)^{2}}_{\text{supported in $\{\delta(t)/2\leq r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}.

We can now use Lemma
D.5
to estimate any contraction of
\partial_{t}\tilde{g}
with the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
with the help of estimates (
3.11
), (
3.12
) and (
3.13
).
∎

We continue by estimating the Hölder norm of the Lichnerowicz operator of the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
:

Proposition 3.14
.

There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}[\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq Cr^{2}\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{\rho(t)^{6^{-}}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-6}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}},\\
[\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{g}]_{\alpha,t,r}&=0,\\
[\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{c}]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq Cr^{2}\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{\rho(t)^{6^{-}}}+Cr^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}.\end{split}

Proof.

Since
\tilde{g}(t)
is parallel with respect to
\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}
and that the zeroth order terms of the Lichnerowicz operator preserves pure trace symmetric
2
-tensors, one gets
\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{g}(t)=0.

Observe that both terms
\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
and
\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{c}
are time-differentiable so that Lemma
D.2
, (
D.1
) and Lemma
D.5
are applicable.

Now, if
(h(t))_{t\,\leq\,T}
is a
C^{1}
family of symmetric
2
-tensors on
M
then:

(3.14)

\partial_{t}\left(\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}h(t)\right)=\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\left(\partial_{t}h(t)\right)+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),j}h(t)\ast\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),2-j}(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)).

Applying the previous identity (
3.14
) together with Lemmata
3.8
and
3.10
gives:

\begin{split}\left|\partial_{t}\left(\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\right)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq\underbrace{C\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{6^{-}}}}_{\text{supported in $\{r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}+\underbrace{C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-6}}_{\text{supported in $\{\delta(t)/2\leq r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}},\end{split}

which gives the expected result on
[\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}
thanks to (
D.1
).

Similarly, applying Lemma
3.11
and identity (
3.14
),

\begin{split}\left|\partial_{t}\left(\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{c}(t)\right)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq C\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{6^{-}}}+\underbrace{C}_{\text{supported in $\{\delta(t)/2\leq r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}.\end{split}

∎

We continue this section by estimating the Hölder semi-norms
[\,\cdot\,]_{\alpha,t,r}
of the time derivatives of the remaining components of the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
.

Proposition 3.15
.

There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}[\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{\rho(t)^{4^{-}}}\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}},\\
[\partial_{t}\tilde{c}]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{\rho(t)^{4^{-}}}\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right).\end{split}

Proof.

Recall that

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)&=\chi_{\delta(t)}\partial_{t}\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))-\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}\frac{r_{o}\delta^{\prime}(t)}{\delta(t)^{2}}\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\\
&=\chi_{\delta(t)}d_{\zeta(t)}\mathbf{o}_{i}(\dot{\zeta}(t))-\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}\frac{r_{o}\delta^{\prime}(t)}{\delta(t)^{2}}\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t)).\end{split}

Proposition
A.16
and repeated applications of (
D.1
) give:

[\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}\mathbf{o}_{i}(\dot{\zeta})]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\underbrace{\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{4^{-}}}\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)}_{\text{supported in $\{r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}+\underbrace{Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{-4^{-}}}_{\text{supported in $\{\delta(t)/2\leq r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}.

Since the term
\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}\frac{r_{o}\delta^{\prime}(t)}{\delta(t)^{2}}\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))
is time-differentiable, (
D.1
) gives:

\left[\chi^{\prime}_{\delta}\frac{r_{o}\delta^{\prime}}{\delta^{2}}\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta)\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq\underbrace{Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4}}_{\text{supported in $\{\delta(t)/2\leq r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}.

The two previous estimates lead to the desired result thanks to (

H
0

) since
\delta<1
.

As for the Hölder semi-norm of
\partial_{t}\tilde{c}(t)
, recall from the proof of Lemma
3.11
that:

(3.15)

\partial_{t}\tilde{c}(t)=\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),2}(\partial_{t}\tilde{v}(t))+\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t))\ast\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t).

In order to handle the second term on the righthand side of (
3.15
), we apply Lemma
D.9
to
S(t):=\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)
and
T(t):=\tilde{v}(t)
to get:

(3.16)

\begin{split}[\nabla^{\tilde{g}}(\partial_{t}\tilde{g})]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{5^{-}}}\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right),\\
[\nabla^{\tilde{g}}\tilde{v}]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq Cr^{2}\underbrace{\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{3^{-}}}}_{\text{supported in $\{r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}+\underbrace{Cr^{2}\delta(t)^{3}}_{\text{supported in $\{\delta(t)/2\leq r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}.\end{split}

Here we have used Lemma
3.8
,
3.11
and
3.13
in combination with Proposition
A.16
.

As an intermediate result, we get thanks to the two previous estimates (
3.16
) together with Lemma
D.5
:

(3.17)

\begin{split}[\nabla^{\tilde{g}}(\partial_{t}\tilde{g})\ast\nabla^{\tilde{g}}\tilde{v}]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{4^{-}}}\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right).\end{split}

Now, recall that:

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\tilde{v}(t)&=\chi_{\delta(t)}\partial_{t}v_{g_{\zeta(t)}}-\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}\frac{r_{o}\delta^{\prime}(t)}{\delta(t)^{2}}(v_{g_{\zeta(t)}}-v_{0})\\
&=-\chi_{\delta(t)}d_{\zeta(t)}u(\dot{\zeta}(t))-\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}\frac{r_{o}\delta^{\prime}(t)}{\delta(t)^{2}}(v_{g_{\zeta(t)}}-v_{0}).\end{split}

This implies by Lemma
D.9
:

(3.18)

\begin{split}[\nabla^{\tilde{g},\,2}(\partial_{t}\tilde{v})]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\underbrace{\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{4^{-}}}\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)}_{\text{supported in $\{r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}+\underbrace{Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{-4^{-}}}_{\text{supported in $\{\delta(t)/2\leq r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}.\end{split}

Here we have used Lemma
3.8
and
3.11
together with Proposition
A.16
.

Recalling (
3.15
), estimates (
3.17
) and (
3.18
) gives the expected result on
[\partial_{t}\tilde{c}]_{\alpha,t,r}
thanks to (

H
0

).
∎

We end this section by a careful Hölder estimate on the obstruction for the first approximation
\tilde{g}(t)
to be an exact solution to the normalized Ricci flow:

Lemma 3.16
.

There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}\Bigg[&\partial_{t}\tilde{g}+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g})-2\Lambda\tilde{g}-\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}g\left(\dot{\zeta}-2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}(\zeta)\right)-(1-\chi_{\delta})\left(d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})+\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta}^{4})}(g_{o})\right)\Bigg]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\leq C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\rho(t)^{-2^{-}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{5^{-}}\delta(t)^{-7-}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}\\
&\quad+Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{8}\rho(t)^{-10}\mathbbm{1}_{\{2\delta(t)\,\leq\,r_{o}\}},\end{split}

and,

\begin{split}&\Big[\partial_{t}\tilde{g}+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g})-2\Lambda\tilde{g}-\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}g(\dot{\zeta}-2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}(\zeta))-(1-\chi_{\delta})\left(d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})+\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta}^{4})}(g_{o})\right)\Big]_{\alpha,x,t}\leq\\
&\quad+C\left(\left(1+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,\rho(x,t)}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{\rho(t)^{2^{-}}}+\rho(t)^{2}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}\\
&\quad+C\left(1+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,\rho(x,t)}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{5^{-}}\delta(t)^{-7^{-}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\rho(t)^{-10}\mathbbm{1}_{\{2\delta(t)\,\leq\,r_{o}\}}.\end{split}

Proof.

If
T(t):=\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))-2\Lambda\tilde{g}(t)
then thanks to Lemma
C.2
, as in the proofs of Lemma
3.6
and
3.8
, let us linearize this tensor at
g_{\zeta}
so that on
\{r_{o}\leq\delta(t)\}
,

\begin{split}T&=\chi^{\prime}_{\delta}\frac{r_{o}\delta^{\prime}}{\delta^{2}}(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta}-g_{\zeta}-h_{2,\zeta})+\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}(g+h_{2})(\dot{\zeta})+(1-\chi_{\delta})d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})\\
&\quad+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g_{\zeta})-2\Lambda(g_{\zeta}+h_{2,\zeta}+(1-\chi_{\delta})(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta}-g_{\zeta}-h_{2,\zeta}))\\
&\quad-\Delta_{L,g_{\zeta}}(h_{2,\zeta}+(1-\chi_{\delta})(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta}-g_{\zeta}-h_{2,\zeta}))+\mathcal{L}_{B_{g_{\zeta}}(h_{2,\zeta}+(1-\chi_{\delta})(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta}-g_{\zeta}-h_{2,\zeta})}\tilde{g}\\
&\quad+Q_{g_{\zeta}}(h_{2,\zeta}+(1-\chi_{\delta})(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta}-g_{\zeta}-h_{2,\zeta}))\\
&=\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}g(\dot{\zeta}-2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}(\zeta))+(1-\chi_{\delta})d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})\\
&\quad+\left(\chi^{\prime}_{\delta}\frac{r_{o}\delta^{\prime}}{\delta^{2}}-2\Lambda(1-\chi_{\delta})\right)(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta}-g_{\zeta}-h_{2,\zeta})+\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}h_{2}(\dot{\zeta})-2\Lambda h_{2,\zeta}\\
&\quad-\Delta_{L,g_{\zeta}}((1-\chi_{\delta})(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta}-g_{\zeta}-h_{2,\zeta}))+\mathcal{L}_{B_{g_{\zeta}}((1-\chi_{\delta})(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta}-g_{\zeta}-h_{2,\zeta})}\tilde{g}\\
&\quad+Q_{g_{\zeta}}(h_{2,\zeta}+(1-\chi_{\delta})(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta}-g_{\zeta}-h_{2,\zeta})).\end{split}

Here we have been using Proposition
2.13
in the second equality.
In particular, on
\{r_{o}\leq\delta(t)\}
, thanks to (

H
0

), Lemma
3.4
and

\begin{split}[T-\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}&g(\dot{\zeta}-2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}(\zeta))-(1-\chi_{\delta})d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq\\
&C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{2^{-}}}+Cr^{2}\underbrace{\varepsilon(t)^{5^{-}}\delta(t)^{-7^{-}}}_{\text{supported in $\{r_{o}\geq\delta(t)/2\}$}}.\end{split}

On
\{r_{o}>\delta(t)\}
, linearizing
T
at
g_{o}
gives:

\begin{split}T&=\chi^{\prime}_{\delta}\frac{r_{o}\delta^{\prime}}{\delta^{2}}(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta}-g_{\zeta}-h_{2,\zeta})+\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}(g+h_{2})(\dot{\zeta})+(1-\chi_{\delta})d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})\\
&\quad+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g_{o})-2\Lambda(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta}+\chi_{\delta}(g_{\zeta}+h_{2,\zeta}-g_{o}-h^{4}_{\zeta}))\\
&\quad-\Delta_{L,g_{o}}(h^{4}_{\zeta}+\chi_{\delta}(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta}-g_{\zeta}-h_{2,\zeta}))+\mathcal{L}_{B_{g_{\zeta}}(h_{2,\zeta}+\chi_{\delta}(g_{\zeta}+h_{2,\zeta}-g_{o}-h^{4}_{\zeta})}\tilde{g}\\
&\quad+Q_{g_{\zeta}}(h^{4}_{\zeta}+\chi_{\delta}(g_{\zeta}+h_{2,\zeta}-g_{o}-h^{4}_{\zeta}))\\
&=(1-\chi_{\delta})d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})+\mathcal{L}_{B_{g_{\zeta}}(h^{4}_{\zeta})}\tilde{g}+\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}(g+h_{2})(\dot{\zeta})\\
&\quad+\chi^{\prime}_{\delta}\frac{r_{o}\delta^{\prime}}{\delta^{2}}(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta}-g_{\zeta}-h_{2,\zeta})-2\Lambda\chi_{\delta}(g_{\zeta}+h_{2,\zeta}-g_{o}-h^{4}_{\zeta})\\
&\quad-\Delta_{L,g_{o}}(\chi_{\delta}(g_{o}+h^{4}_{\zeta}-g_{\zeta}-h_{2,\zeta}))+\mathcal{L}_{B_{g_{\zeta}}(\chi_{\delta}(g_{\zeta}+h_{2,\zeta}-g_{o}-h^{4}_{\zeta}))}\tilde{g}\\
&\quad+Q_{g_{\zeta}}(h^{4}_{\zeta}+\chi_{\delta}(g_{\zeta}+h_{2,\zeta}-g_{o}-h^{4}_{\zeta})),\end{split}

where we have been using Proposition
2.31
in the second equality.
In particular, on
\{r_{o}\,>\,\delta(t)\}
, thanks to (

H
0

), Lemma
3.4
and Proposition
A.16
,

\begin{split}&\Big[T-(1-\chi_{\delta})d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})-(1-\chi_{\delta})\mathcal{L}_{B_{g_{\zeta}}(h^{4}_{\zeta})}g_{o}\Big]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq\\
&C\underbrace{\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{\delta(t)^{2^{-}}}}_{\text{supported in $\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}+Cr^{2}\underbrace{\varepsilon(t)^{5^{-}}\delta(t)^{-7^{-}}}_{\text{supported in $\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\leq 2\delta(t)\}$}}+Cr^{2}\underbrace{\varepsilon(t)^{8}\rho(t)^{-10}}_{\text{supported in $\{r_{o}\geq 2\delta(t)\}$}}.\end{split}

Here we have bounded from above the terms
\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4}
coming from
\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}g(\dot{\zeta})
by
\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\varepsilon(t)^{5^{-}}\delta(t)^{-7^{-}}
.

In order to get the second estimate, one invokes the previous estimate with
r=\rho(x,t)
together with Lemma
3.8
with
k=1
.
∎

4.
Projections on the approximate kernel

In this whole section, we consider
\tilde{g}(t)
defined either on
(-\infty,T)
, or on
(T,+\infty)
towards the construction of ancient and immortal flows separately. The proof are exactly the same in both cases which explains why we state the estimates for the ancient case only.

We start by projecting the obstruction of
\tilde{g}(t)
to be an exact Ricci flow on
\tilde{g}(t)
:

Proposition 4.1
.

(Projection on
\tilde{g}
)
There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}\left|\langle-\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))+\Lambda\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10},\\
\left|\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8}.\end{split}

Proof.

Thanks to Lemma
3.6
, one gets:

(4.1)

\begin{split}&\Big\langle-\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))+\Lambda\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=\\
&\Big\langle\chi_{\delta(t)}d_{\zeta(t)}g(\mathbf{R}^{+}_{p}(\zeta(t)))-(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o}),\tilde{g}(t)\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&+\Big\langle O(\rho(t)^{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,\delta(t)/2\}}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-7})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+\varepsilon(t)^{8}\rho(t)^{-10}\mathbbm{1}_{\{2\delta(t)\,\leq\,r_{o}\}},\tilde{g}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&=\Big\langle\chi_{\delta(t)}d_{\zeta(t)}g(\mathbf{R}^{+}_{p}(\zeta(t)))-(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o}),\tilde{g}(t)\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&\quad+O(\delta(t)^{6})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-3})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10}).\end{split}

Now, since
\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{\zeta(t)}}d_{\zeta(t)}g(\mathbf{R}^{+}_{p}(\zeta(t)))=0
and that
\tilde{g}(t)-g_{\zeta(t)}=O(\rho(t)^{2})
on
\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}
according to Lemma
3.4
, we get that

(4.2)

\Big\langle\chi_{\delta(t)}d_{\zeta(t)}g(\mathbf{R}^{+}_{p}(\zeta(t))),\tilde{g}(t)\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}).

Similarly, since
\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o})=2\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})
and that
\tilde{g}(t)-g_{o}=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-4})
on
\{r_{o}\,\geq\,\delta(t)/2\}
according to Lemma
3.4
, an integration by parts shows that:
we get that

(4.3)

\begin{split}\Big\langle(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o}),\tilde{g}(t)\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}&=\Big\langle(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o}),g_{o}\big\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{o})}\\
&\quad+\int_{M}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-4})|\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o})|_{g_{o}}\,d\mu_{g_{o}}\\
&=-2\langle B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4}),\nabla^{g_{o}}(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{o})}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8})\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8}).\end{split}

Here we have used Proposition
2.31
in the last line.

Combining (
4.1
), (
4.2
) and (
4.3
) together with (

H
0

) leads to the expected estimate.

As for the projection on
\tilde{g}(t)
of
\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)
, Lemma
3.8
gives:

\begin{split}\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\})}&=\int_{M}O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-5})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\rho(t)^{-8})\mathbbm{1}_{\{2\delta(t)\,\leq\,r_{o}\}}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&+\int_{M}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2^{-}})\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-1})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8})\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8}),\end{split}

as expected.
∎

The following proposition estimates the projection of the obstruction of
\tilde{g}(t)
to be an exact Ricci flow on the family
(\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t))_{i}
:

Proposition 4.2
.

(Projection on
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}
)
There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
such that the family
(\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t))_{i}
satisfies:

\begin{split}\left|\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2},\\
\Bigg|\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}-2\pi^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta_{ij}\Bigg|&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4},\\
\Bigg|\langle-2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))+2\Lambda\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}&-2\langle d_{\zeta(t)}g(\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}(\zeta(t))),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}\Bigg|\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{9}\delta(t)^{-7},\\
\Bigg|\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}&-\langle d_{\zeta(t)}g(\dot{\zeta}(t)),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}\Bigg|\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4},\\
\left|\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4},\\
\left|\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{c}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4}.\end{split}

Remark 4.3
.

The second estimate in Proposition
4.2
uses the fact that the basis
(\mathbf{o}_{i})_{1\,\leq\,i\,\leq\,3}
of the
L^{2}
-kernel
\mathbf{O}(\mathbf{eh})
of the Lichnerowicz operator associated to the Eguchi-Hanson metric
\mathbf{eh}
is naturally an orthonormal basis as explained in Section
2.2.2
. If one glues one of Kronheimer’s ALE Ricci flat metrics
g_{\zeta}
instead of
\mathbf{eh}_{\zeta}
, then Section
11.3.3
asks for an intermediate orthonormalization of the basis
(\mathbf{o}_{ij})_{i,j}
to reach the analogous results for the corresponding approximate kernel
(\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{ij})_{i,j}
.

Proof.

The estimate on
\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}
follows by integrating the estimate on
\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
from Lemma
3.10
.

Next, (
2.15
) from Section
2.2.2
and the fact that
(\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t)))_{i}
is an
L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})
-orthogonal basis,
\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}
equals
2\pi^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta_{ij}
modulo integrals of the form:

\begin{split}\int_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,\delta(t)/2\}}r_{o}^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{8}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-8}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{6}),\\
\int_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}\varepsilon(t)^{8}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-8}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4}),\\
\int_{\{r_{o}\,>\,\delta(t)/2\}}\varepsilon(t)^{8}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-8}\,d\mu_{g_{\zeta(t)}}&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4}).\end{split}

Here we have used Lemma
3.4
together with Lemma
D.10
. We conclude by invoking (

H
0

), i.e.
\delta>1/2
.

Similarly to the proof of Proposition
4.1
, Lemma
3.6
gives:

\begin{split}&\langle-\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))+\Lambda\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=\\
&\langle\chi_{\delta(t)}d_{\zeta(t)}g(\mathbf{R}^{+}_{p}(\zeta(t)))+O(\rho(t)^{2})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-7})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\})}\\
&=\langle\chi_{\delta(t)}d_{\zeta(t)}g(\mathbf{R}^{+}_{p}(\zeta(t))),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}+\int_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2})\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}++O(\varepsilon(t)^{9}\delta(t)^{-7})\\
&=\langle d_{\zeta(t)}g(\mathbf{R}^{+}_{p}(\zeta(t))),\mathbf{o}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2})++O(\varepsilon(t)^{9}\delta(t)^{-7})\\
&=\langle d_{\zeta(t)}g(\mathbf{R}^{+}_{p}(\zeta(t))),\mathbf{o}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{9}\delta(t)^{-7}).\end{split}

Here we have used a similar reasoning that led to the previous estimate on
\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}
based on Lemma
D.10
in the last line together with the fact that
\delta>5/9>1/3
by (

H
0

). This gives us the desired third estimate.

Now, according to Lemma
3.8
, one can estimate in a similar way the following
L^{2}
-projection:

(4.4)

\begin{split}\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)&,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=\\
&\langle\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4})+\chi_{\delta(t)}(d_{\zeta(t)}g(\dot{\zeta})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2^{-}})),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{6})+\langle\chi_{\delta(t)}d_{\zeta(t)}g(\dot{\zeta}),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}.\end{split}

Thanks to Lemma
D.10
again, we get:

\langle\chi_{\delta(t)}d_{\zeta(t)}g(\dot{\zeta}(t)),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=\langle d_{\zeta(t)}g(\dot{\zeta}(t)),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4}),

which gives the expected fourth estimate once it is combined with (
4.4
) together with the fact that
\delta>1/2
according to (

H
0

).

The last two estimates can be proved with the help of Lemma
3.10
and Lemma
3.11
together with the fact that
|\varepsilon(t)|\leq C|\delta(t)|
according to (

H
0

).

∎

Finally, we compute the time derivatives of the
L^{2}
-projections against
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
and
\tilde{g}(t)
, we get:

Corollary 4.4
.

There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
such that the time derivatives of the scalar products
\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}
,
1\leq i,j\leq 3
, and
\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}
satisfy:

\begin{split}\Bigg|\frac{d}{dt}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}&-4\pi^{2}\langle\dot{\zeta}(t),\zeta(t)\rangle\delta_{ij}\Bigg|\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4},\\
\left|\frac{d}{dt}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}}.\end{split}

Proof.

Similarly to the proof of Proposition
4.2
, one gets:

\begin{split}&\frac{d}{dt}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=\frac{d}{dt}\int_{M}\chi_{\delta(t)}^{2}\langle\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t)),\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta(t))\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&=\frac{d}{dt}\left(\|\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}\delta_{ij}-\int_{\{r_{o}\,\geq\,\delta(t)/2\}}\langle\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t)),\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta(t))\rangle_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\,d\mu_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\right)\\
&+\frac{d}{dt}\left(\int_{M}\chi_{\delta(t)}^{2}\langle\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t)),\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta(t))\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}-\int_{M}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,\delta(t)/2\}}\langle\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t)),\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta(t))\rangle_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\,d\mu_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\right).\end{split}

By taking into account the contribution of the boundary integrals, one gets the expected estimate.

As in the proof of Lemma
3.10
,

\begin{split}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)&=\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2})+\chi_{\delta(t)}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\left[\left(\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}-\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\right)\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\right]\\
&=\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2})+\chi_{\delta(t)}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\left[\tilde{g}(t)^{-1}\ast g_{\zeta(t)}^{-1}\ast(\tilde{g}(t)-g_{\zeta(t)})\ast\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\right]\\
&=\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2})+\chi_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2^{-}}+\varepsilon(t)^{8^{-}}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-6^{-}})\\
&=\chi^{\prime}_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2})+\chi_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2^{-}}),\end{split}

where we have used Lemma
3.8
together with Proposition
A.16
in the penultimate line.

By integrating the previous estimate on
M
gives the expected result on the time derivative of
\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}
since

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)&=\chi_{\delta(t)}^{\prime}O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-6})+\chi_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{8^{-}}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-4^{-}})\\
&=\chi_{\delta(t)}^{\prime}O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8})+\chi_{\delta(t)}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2^{-}}),\end{split}

and
\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4}\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}}
according to the choice of
\delta<2/3
in (

H
0

).

∎

The next proposition of this section estimates the projection of the obstruction of
\tilde{g}(t)
to be an exact Ricci flow on the Lie derivative
\tilde{c}(t)
:

Proposition 4.5
.

(Projection on
\tilde{c}(t)
)
There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}\left|\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))-2\Lambda\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10},\\
\left|\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4},\\
\left|\frac{d}{dt}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4},\\
\langle\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}&=0,\\
\left|\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{c}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{-4^{-}},\\
\left|\frac{d}{dt}\langle\tilde{c},\tilde{c}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{-4^{-}}.\end{split}

Proof.

Regarding the first estimate, observe that the integrand is supported in
\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}
. Lemma
3.8
and the fact that
\tilde{c}(t)
is bounded thanks to Lemma
3.11
give:

(4.5)

\begin{split}&\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))-2\Lambda\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=\\
&\int_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}O\left(\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-4}+(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{2}+\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2^{-}}\right)\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&+\int_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-7})\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}+\int_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\}}O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10})\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&+\Big\langle(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\left(d_{\zeta(t)}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})+\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o})\right),\tilde{c}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\log(1+\delta(t)/\varepsilon(t)))+O(\delta(t)^{6})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-3})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10})\\
&+\Big\langle(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\left(d_{\zeta(t)}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})+\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o})\right),\tilde{c}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10})+\Big\langle(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\left(d_{\zeta(t)}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})+\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o})\right),\tilde{c}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))},\end{split}

according to (

H
0

).

Now, on the one hand, since
\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)=O(\rho(t))
by Lemma
3.11
, an integration by parts shows:

(4.6)

\begin{split}\Big\langle(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})d_{\zeta(t)}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta}),\tilde{c}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}&=-\Big\langle\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\left((1-\chi_{\delta(t)})d_{\zeta(t)}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})\right),\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})-\Big\langle(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\left(d_{\zeta(t)}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})\right),\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})-\Big\langle(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}\left(d_{\zeta(t)}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})\right),\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&\quad+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-8})\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8})\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2}).\end{split}

Here we have used Proposition
2.31
in the second line and the fourth equality to derive that
\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}d_{\zeta(t)}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-3})
. Lemma
3.4
is used in the third equality.

On the other hand, since
\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)=O(\rho(t))
by Lemma
3.11
, an integration by parts shows:

\begin{split}&\Big\langle(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o}),\tilde{c}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=-\Big\langle\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\left((1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o})\right),\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&=-\Big\langle\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}\left(\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o})\right),(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8}).\end{split}

Let us recall the following Bochner formula that holds for any
C^{1}
vector field
X
:

\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}\mathcal{L}_{X}(g_{o})=d\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}X+g_{o}(\Delta_{g_{o}}X,\cdot)+\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g_{o})(X,\cdot).

Let us apply this formula to
X=B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})
to derive that:

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}\mathcal{L}_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o})&=d(\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4}))+g_{o}(\Delta_{g_{o}}B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4}),\cdot)+\Lambda g_{o}(B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4}),\cdot)\\
&=d(\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4}))-\Lambda g_{o}(B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4}),\cdot),\end{split}

where we have used the Bochner formula
\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}(\Delta_{L,g_{o}}h)=\Delta_{g_{o}}(\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}h)
due to Lichnerowicz
[
Lic61
]
for any symmetric
2
-tensor
h
on an Einstein manifold together with Proposition
2.31
in the last line.
To summarize,

\begin{split}\Big\langle(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o}),\tilde{c}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}&=-\Big\langle\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}(\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}(B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})(g_{o}))),(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&\quad+\Lambda\Big\langle B_{g_{o}}(h^{4}_{\zeta(t)}),(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8})\\
&=\Big\langle\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}(B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})(g_{o}))),\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\left((1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)\right)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&\quad+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8})\\
&=\Big\langle\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}(B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})(g_{o}))),(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\Delta_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&\quad+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8}).\end{split}

The last term on the righthand side of the previous estimates can be taken care of as follows:

\begin{split}&\Big\langle\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}(B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})(g_{o}))),(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\Delta_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=\\
&\Big\langle\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}(B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})(g_{o}))),(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\left(\Delta_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)+(\Lambda+1)\tilde{v}(t)\right)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&\quad+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8})\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8}).\end{split}

Here we have used the fact that
\tilde{v}(t)=O(\rho(t)^{2})
and

\Delta_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)+(\Lambda+1)\tilde{v}(t)=O(\rho(t)^{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho(t)\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-4}),

thanks to Lemma
3.11
.
As an intermediate conclusion, (

H
0

) ensures,

(4.7)

\begin{split}\Big\langle(1-\chi_{\delta(t)})\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta(t)}^{4})}(g_{o}),\tilde{c}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2}).\end{split}

The combination of (
4.5
), (
4.6
) and (
4.7
) leads to the desired estimate.

For the second estimate, integrate by parts to get:

\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=-\langle\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}.

Therefore, since
|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}=O((\varepsilon(t)+r_{o}))
by Lemma
3.11
, the previous estimate combined with Lemma
3.10
allows us to conclude that:

\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2})+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}),

as desired.
The estimate on the time-derivative of
\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}
is obtained by differentiating (Lemma
C.4
):

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}&=\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}+\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\partial_{t}\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}+\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\ast\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})-\langle\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}(\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)),\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}),\end{split}

where we have used Lemmata
3.8
and
3.11
and Proposition
4.2
together with an integration by parts in the second line. In the last line, we invoke Lemma
3.10
.

The antepenultimate estimate
\langle\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=0
is straightforward since
\langle\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}=\Delta_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)
. The penultimate estimate is obtained thanks to Lemma
3.11
and the last estimate can be proved by combining the previous one together with Lemma
3.8
and Lemma
C.4
.
∎

The next proposition establishes Hölder estimates on the scalar products between two elements of the approximate kernel.

Proposition 4.6
.

There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}[\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq Cr^{2}\left(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta_{ij}+\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4}\right),\\
[\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}},\\
[\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4},\\
[\langle\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8},\\
[\langle\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}]_{\alpha,t,r}&=0,\\
[\langle\tilde{c}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4-}\delta(t)^{-4^{-}}.\end{split}

Proof.

Apply (
D.1
) together with the time-derivative estimates from Lemma
3.8
, Proposition
4.1
, Corollary
4.4
and Proposition
4.5
.
∎

Finally, we give a careful Hölder estimate of the
L^{2}
-projections of the obstruction for the first approximation
\tilde{g}(t)
to be an exact solution to the normalized Ricci flow:

Lemma 4.7
.

There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
, for
\eta:=\dot{\zeta}-2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}(\zeta)
,
