\begin{split}\left[\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{g}+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g})-2\Lambda\tilde{g},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}-\langle d_{\zeta}g(\eta),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta)\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{9^{-}}\delta(t)^{-7^{-}},\\
\left[\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{g}+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g})-2\Lambda\tilde{g},\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10},\\
\left[\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{g}+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g})-2\Lambda\tilde{g},\tilde{c}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10}.\end{split}

Proof.

As a first step, let us notice that Lemmata
3.8
,
3.10
and
3.16
and Lemma
D.8
give:

\begin{split}\Bigg[\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{g}&+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g})-2\Lambda\tilde{g}-\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}g(\eta)-(1-\chi_{\delta})\left(d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})+\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta}^{4})}(g_{o})\right),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\Bigg]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\leq C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\left(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}}+\varepsilon(t)^{9^{+}}\delta(t)^{-7^{-}}\right)\\
&\leq C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{9^{+}}\delta(t)^{-7^{-}}.\end{split}

Here we have used that
\delta>5/9
according to (

H
0

).

Now, thanks to Proposition
2.31
, Lemma
3.10
and Lemma
D.8
, we get:

\begin{split}\Bigg[\langle(1-\chi_{\delta})\left(d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})+\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta}^{4})}(g_{o})\right),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\Bigg]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{8^{-}}\delta(t)^{-4^{-}}.\end{split}

To conclude the first estimate, it suffices to invoke (

H
0

) and Lemma
D.10
to tensors that are (difference quotients of)
d_{\zeta}g(\eta)
and
\mathbf{o}_{i}(t)
:

\begin{split}\left[\langle\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}g(\eta),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}-\langle d_{\zeta}g(\eta),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta)\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\eta]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{8^{-}}\delta(t)^{-4^{-}}.\end{split}

The last two projections against
\tilde{g}(t)
and
\tilde{c}(t)
can be estimated in the same spirit as for the previous one: invoking Lemma
3.16
and Lemma
D.8
give,

\begin{split}\Big[\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{g}+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g})&-2\Lambda\tilde{g},\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\Big]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq\\
&\left[\langle\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}g(\eta),\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}+\Big[\langle(1-\chi_{\delta})\left(d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})+\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta}^{4})}(g_{o})\right),\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\Big]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\quad+C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10}\\
&\leq\left[\langle\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}g(\eta),\chi_{\delta}g_{\zeta}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}+\left[\langle\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}g(\eta),\chi_{\delta}h_{2,\zeta}+(1-\chi_{\delta})g_{o}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\quad+\Big[\langle(1-\chi_{\delta})\left(d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})+\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta}^{4})}(g_{o})\right),\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\Big]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\quad+C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10}.\end{split}

Now, Proposition
A.16
and Lemma
D.10
and the fact that
\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{\zeta}}d_{\zeta}g(\eta)=0
give:

\begin{split}\left[\langle\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}g(\eta),\chi_{\delta}g_{\zeta}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[{\dot{\zeta}}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\left(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}}+\varepsilon(t)^{4}\right),\\
\left[\langle\chi_{\delta}d_{\zeta}g(\eta),\chi_{\delta}h_{2,\zeta}+(1-\chi_{\delta})g_{o}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[{\dot{\zeta}}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\left(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}}+\varepsilon(t)^{4}\right).\end{split}

Here we have used that
|\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}(d_{\zeta}g(\eta))|\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2}
on the annulus
\{\delta(t)/2<r_{o}<2\delta(t)\}
thanks to Lemma
3.4
.

Similarly, since
\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}h^{4}_{\zeta}=0
and
\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}\left(\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta}^{4})}(g_{o})\right)=2\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{g_{o}}B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta}^{4})
, according to Proposition
2.31
,

\begin{split}&\Big[\langle(1-\chi_{\delta})\left(d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})+\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta}^{4})}(g_{o})\right),\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\Big]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq\\
&\Big[\langle(1-\chi_{\delta})\left(d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})+\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta}^{4})}(g_{o})\right),(1-\chi_{\delta})g_{o}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\Big]_{\alpha,t,r}+C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[{\dot{\zeta}}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{8^{-}}\delta(t)^{-8^{-}}\\
&\leq\Big[\int_{M}(1-\chi_{\delta})\left(d_{\zeta}h^{4}(\dot{\zeta})+\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{o}}(h_{\zeta}^{4})}(g_{o})\right)\ast(\tilde{g}-g_{o})\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}}\Big]_{\alpha,t,r}+C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[{\dot{\zeta}}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{8^{-}}\delta(t)^{-8^{-}}\\
&\leq C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[{\dot{\zeta}}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{8^{-}}\delta(t)^{-8^{-}},\end{split}

where we have invoked Proposition
3.8
in the last line. This leads to the second desired estimate.

As for the final estimate, it can be proved along the same lines by using Lemma
3.16
.
∎

5.
Liouville theorems

We now present the Liouville theorems that will let us deal with the approximate kernel
\textbf{O}(t)
of the linearized operator (i.e. the Lichnerowicz Laplacian associated to
\tilde{g}(t)
).

Remark 5.1
.

For simplicity, and because this is the most interesting case, we assume that all of the orbifolds and ALE spaces are stable. The proofs also work if there were other unstable directions, by forcing the tensors to be
L^{2}
-orthogonal to all of the nonnegative eigentensors (of finite dimension) of the Lichnerowicz Laplacian—not just the zero eigenspace.

5.1.
On stable ALE

In this section, we state a Liouville-type result for the parabolic Lichnerowicz equation where the background metric is a
stable
Ricci flat ALE metric:

Definition 5.2
.

A Ricci flat ALE metric
(N,g_{b})
is stable if the associated Lichnerowicz operator is non-positive in the
L^{2}
sense:
\langle\Delta_{L,g_{b}}h,h\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{b})}\leq 0
for all
h\in L^{2}(g_{b})
with equality if and only if
h\in\ker_{L^{2}}\Delta_{L,g_{b}}
.

Let us notice that Eguchi-Hanson metrics and Kronheimer’s instantons of
[
Kro89a
]
are all stable in the aforementioned sense: see Proposition
11.2
.

5.1.1.
For ancient flows

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
(M^{4},g_{b})
be a stable Ricci flat ALE metric in the sense of Definition
5.2
.
Let
(h(t))_{t\,<\,0}
be an ancient solution to the homogeneous heat equation
\partial_{t}h=\Delta_{L,g_{b}}h
on
M\times(-\infty,0)
such that on
M\times(-\infty,0)
,
|h(t)|_{g_{b}}\leq C(1+r_{b})^{-\sigma}
for some uniform-in-time positive constants
C
and
\sigma
.

If
h(t)
is orthogonal to the
L^{2}
-kernel of
\Delta_{L,g_{b}}
for each time
t<0
then
h(t)\equiv 0
for
t<0
.

Remark 5.4
.

Notice that Proposition
5.3
does not assume the solution
h(t)
to lie in
L^{2}
for each time
t<0
since
\sigma
is an arbitrary positive number. In particular, the orthogonality of
h(t)
to an element of
\ker_{L^{2}}\Delta_{L,g_{b}}
is not well-defined a priori. We make a slight abuse of terminology thanks to the following remark: any element of
\ker_{L^{2}}\Delta_{L,g_{b}}
can be shown to decay as fast as
r_{b}^{-4}
at infinity
[
BKN89
]
which makes the integrand
\langle h(t),\mathbf{o}\rangle_{g_{b}}
Lebesgue integrable for any
\mathbf{o}\in\ker_{L^{2}}\Delta_{L,g_{b}}
by assumption on the pointwise decay of
h(t)
.

Proof of Proposition
5.3
.

The proof goes verbatim as in the proof of
[
BK17
, Proposition
5.2
]
.
∎

5.1.2.
For immortal flows

We now recall a general uniqueness theorem for solutions of the linear heat flow for a complete background metric due to Karp and Li:

Theorem 5.5
.

(
[
CLN06
, Theorem
7.39
]
)
Let
(M^{n},g)
be a complete Riemannian manifold. If
u
is a smooth subsolution of the heat equation
\partial_{t}u\leq\Delta_{g}u
on
M\times[0,T]
with
u(\cdot,0)\leq 0
and if there exists
C>0
such that

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{M}u_{+}^{2}(x,t)e^{-Cd_{g}(p,x)^{2}}\,d\mu_{g}dt<+\infty,

then
u\leq 0
on
M\times[0,T]
.

As a consequence of Theorem
5.5
, we get a Liouville theorem for solutions of the linear heat flow on metrics with nonnegative Ricci curvature with initial conditions.

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
(M^{n},g_{b})
be a complete Riemannian metric with nonnegative Ricci curvature and bounded curvature, i.e.
\sup_{M}|\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{b})|_{g_{b}}<+\infty
.
Let
(h(t))_{t\in[0,T]}
be a solution to the homogeneous heat equation
\partial_{t}h=\Delta_{L,g_{b}}h
on
M\times[0,T]
such that on
M\times[0,T]
,
|h(t)|_{g_{b}}\leq e^{C_{0}(d_{g_{b}}(p\,,\,\cdot)+1)^{2}}
for some uniform-in-time positive constant
C_{0}>0
.

If
h(0)\equiv 0
, then
h(t)\equiv 0
for
t\in[0,T]
.

Remark 5.7
.

The assumption on the Ricci curvature is convenient here because we will apply Proposition
5.6
to a Ricci flat ALE metric. An inspection of the proof shows however that assuming the Ricci curvature to be bounded from below or even satisfying
\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g)\geq-C(1+r_{p}^{2})g
for some positive constant
C
would be sufficient: see for instance the proof of
[
CLN06
, Corollary
7.40
]
.

Proof.

Let us consider the norm
|h(t)|^{2}_{g_{b}}
for each
t\in[0,T]
and observe that it satisfies schematically:

\frac{\partial}{\partial t}|h(t)|^{2}_{g_{b}}\leq\Delta_{g_{b}}|h(t)|^{2}_{g_{b}}+c(n)|\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{b})|_{g_{b}}|h(t)|^{2}_{g_{b}}\leq\Delta_{g_{b}}|h(t)|^{2}_{g_{b}}+c(n)C|h(t)|^{2}_{g_{b}},

where
C:=\sup_{M}|\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{b})|_{g_{b}}.
Then
u(x,t):=e^{-c(n)Ct}|h(t)|^{2}_{g_{b}}
is a subsolution of the heat equation with background metric
g_{b}
on
M^{n}\times[0,T]
and by assumption on the Ricci curvature and the growth of
h(t)
:

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{M}u^{2}(x,t)e^{-C_{1}d_{g_{b}}(p,x)^{2}}\,d\mu_{g_{b}}dt<+\infty,

as long as
C_{1}>C_{0}
since the volume growth is polynomial thanks to Bishop’s comparison theorem. Theorem
5.5
lets us conclude that
h\equiv 0
on
M\times[0,T]
as expected.
∎

5.2.
On stable Einstein orbifolds

Let us now prove Liouville theorems for the other main building blocks of our construction, Einstein orbifolds.

5.2.1.
Liouville theorems for ancient flows

Let
(M_{o}^{4},g_{o})
be a
4
-dimensional Einstein orbifold with isolated singularities and assume that it has constant sectional curvature
1
. We still denote the Ricci constant by
\Lambda
which equals
3
here. Then we investigate the triviality of ancient solutions to the heat flow associated to the linearized operator of
-2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g)+2\Lambda g
on such spaces. Let us recall that such an orbifold has at most two isolated singularities: see
[
CTZ12
, Lemma
5.1
]
for instance. In case it has two,
(M_{o},g_{o})
has two opposite singularities and is isometric to a suspension over
\mathbb{S}^{3}/\Gamma
endowed with a metric of constant curvature
1
where
\Gamma\neq\{\mathop{\rm Id}\nolimits\}
is a finite group of
\operatorname{SO(4)}
acting freely on
\mathbb{S}^{3}
.

We first recall the
L^{2}
-spectrum of (minus) the Laplacian acting on functions on a metric suspension that lies below the value
2\Lambda
:

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
(M_{o}^{4},g_{o})
be a metric suspension over
\mathbb{S}^{3}/\Gamma
endowed with a metric of constant curvature
1
with Einstein constant
\Lambda
. Then the
L^{2}
-spectrum of
-\Delta_{g_{o}}
acting on functions that lies below
2\Lambda
consists of
\{0,\Lambda+1\}
. Moreover,
\ker(-\Delta_{g_{o}})=\mathbb{R}
and
\ker(-\Delta_{g_{o}}+\Lambda+1)=\mathbb{R}v_{o}
where
v_{o}(\cdot)=\cos(\cdot)
outside the two isolated singularities of
M_{o}
and
\nabla^{g_{o}}v_{o}
is a conformal vector field such that
\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{g_{o}}v_{o}}(g_{o})=-2v_{o}g_{o}.

Proof.

If
M^{4}
is a spherical suspension over an arbitrary Einstein manifold
(N^{3},\tilde{g})
with
\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g})=(\Lambda-1)\tilde{g}
then according to
[
Kro21
, Theorem
2.1
]
for nonnegative indices
i
,
j
:

(5.1)

\begin{split}\lambda_{i,j}(g)&=\left(\sqrt{\frac{(\Lambda-1)^{2}}{4}+\lambda_{i}(\tilde{g})}-\frac{\Lambda-1}{2}+j\right)\left(\sqrt{\frac{(\Lambda-1)^{2}}{4}+\lambda_{i}(\tilde{g})}-\frac{\Lambda-1}{2}+j+\Lambda\right).\end{split}

In particular,
\lambda_{0,0}(g)=0<2\Lambda
and corresponds to
\lambda_{0}(\tilde{g})=0
whose eigenspace consists of constants.

Now, if
N^{3}
is isometric to
\mathbb{S}^{3}/\Gamma
endowed with a metric of constant curvature equal to
1
then
\lambda_{1}(\tilde{g})\geq 2(\Lambda+1)
. Combining this fact with the previous formula (
5.1
) leads to
\lambda_{1}(g)=\lambda_{0,1}(g)=\Lambda+1<2\Lambda
whose eigenspace is spanned by the function
\cos(\theta)
where
g_{o}=d\theta^{2}+\sin(\theta)^{2}g_{\mathbb{S}^{3}/\Gamma}
for
\theta\in(0,\pi)
. The property on
\nabla^{g_{o}}v_{o}
can be deduced from a straightforward computation. Finally, observe that
\lambda_{1,0}(g)>2\Lambda.

∎

Proposition 5.9
.

Let
(M_{o}^{4},g_{o})
be a metric suspension over
\mathbb{S}^{3}/\Gamma
endowed with a metric of constant curvature
1
with Einstein constant
\Lambda
and let
(h(t))_{t<0}
be a solution to
\partial_{t}h=\Delta_{L,g_{o}}h+2\Lambda h
on
M_{o}\times(-\infty,0]
such that on
M_{o}\times(-\infty,0]
,
|h(t)|\leq Cr_{o}^{-\sigma}
, for some uniform-in-time positive constants
C
and
\sigma\in(0,1)
.

If
\int_{M}\langle h(t),g_{o}\rangle_{g_{o}}\,d\mu_{g_{o}}=0
and
\int_{M}\langle h(t),\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{g_{o}}v_{o}}(g_{o})\rangle_{g_{o}}\,d\mu_{g_{o}}=0
then
h(t)=0
on
M_{o}\times(-\infty,0)
.

Remark 5.10
.

Lemma
5.8
shows that the second orthogonality condition holding on
h(t)
is equivalent in this setting to
\int_{M}\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}h(t)\cdot v_{o}\,d\mu_{g_{o}}=0
.

Remark 5.11
.

Unlike Remark
5.4
, the orthogonality is understood in the usual
L^{2}
sense. Indeed, since
h(t)
blows up at most like
r_{o}^{-\sigma}
with
\sigma\in(0,2)
,
h(t)
lies in
L^{2}
and so are
g_{o}
and
\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{g_{o}}v_{o}}(g_{o})
since they are bounded on
M_{o}
.

Before we prove Theorem
5.9
, we state an algebraic lemma:

Lemma 5.12
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
5.9
,

\partial_{t}h=\Delta_{g_{o}}h+2\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{o})\ast h\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}\overset{\circ}{h}&=\Delta_{g_{o}}\overset{\circ}{h}-2\overset{\circ}{h},\\
\partial_{t}\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}h&=\Delta_{g_{o}}\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}h+2\Lambda\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}h.\end{aligned}\right.

Proof of Lemma
5.12
.

The decoupling is simply based on the fact that
\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{o})\ast h=(\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}h)g_{o}-h
since the curvature operator of
g_{o}
satisfies in coordinates
\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{o})_{ijkl}=(g_{o})_{il}(g_{o})_{jk}-(g_{o})_{ik}(g_{o})_{jl}.

∎

Proof of Proposition
5.9
.

The assumption on the exponent
\sigma
ensures the local (hence the global) integrability of such tensor
h(t)
on
M_{o}
for each
t<0
. Moreover, by parabolic regularity, it guarantees that
\nabla^{g_{o},i}h
for
i=0,1,2
belong to
L^{2}
.

Since
\partial_{t}\overset{\circ}{h}=\Delta_{g_{o}}\overset{\circ}{h}-2\overset{\circ}{h}
by Lemma
5.12
,
multiplying across by
\overset{\circ}{h}
and integrating by parts give us:

\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\|\overset{\circ}{h}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2\|\nabla^{g_{o}}\overset{\circ}{h}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=-4\|\overset{\circ}{h}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

where the integration by parts is legitimated by the aforementioned remark on the integrability of the covariant derivatives of
h(t)
.
In particular, the function
e^{4t}\|\overset{\circ}{h}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}
is decreasing in time. By assumption, there exists a constant
C
such that
\|\overset{\circ}{h}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq C
for all
t<0
. Therefore, for
s\leq t<0
,
\|\overset{\circ}{h}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq e^{-4t}\cdot e^{4s}\|\overset{\circ}{h}(s)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq Ce^{-4t}e^{4s}
. Letting
s
go to
-\infty
in the previous estimate gives
\overset{\circ}{h}(t)=0
for all
t<0
.

Therefore, it suffices to show that
\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}h(t)=0
for all
t<0
to end the proof.

Recall from Lemma
5.12
that
\partial_{t}\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}h=\Delta_{g_{o}}\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}h+2\Lambda\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}h
. By the boundedness assumption on
h(t)
for
t<0
,
\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}h(t)
belongs to the finite dimensional eigenspaces associated to eigenvalues with respect to
-\Delta_{g_{o}}
less than or equal to
2\Lambda
. By assumption,
h(t)
is orthogonal to
g_{0}
and
\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{g_{o}}v_{o}}(g_{o})
, a fact that gives us the expected result thanks to Lemma
5.8
, i.e.
h(t)=0
on
(-\infty,0)\times M_{o}
since pointwise,
\langle h(t),g_{o}\rangle_{g_{o}}=\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}h(t)
and
\langle h(t),\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{g_{o}}v_{o}}(g_{o})\rangle_{g_{o}}=-2v_{o}\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{o}}h(t)
.
∎

5.2.2.
For immortal flows

Let us now consider an Einstein
4
-orbifold
(M_{o},g_{o})
satisfying
\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g_{o})=\Lambda g_{o}
with
\Lambda<0
, as well as
\mathbf{R}^{+}_{g_{o}}<0
pointwise everywhere on
M_{o}
as a bilinear form on
\Lambda^{+}_{g_{o}}
.

Example 5.13
.

Hyperbolic and complex hyperbolic (in the orientation opposite to that of the complex structure) orbifold are obvious examples of manifolds satisfying
\mathbf{R}^{+}<0
, this is also satisfied by the Einstein manifolds constructed by Fine-Premoselli
[
FP20
]
.

Proposition 5.14
.

Let
(h(t))_{t\in[0,T]}
be a solution to the homogeneous heat equation
\partial_{t}h=(\Delta_{L,g_{o}}+2\Lambda)h
on
M_{o}\times[0,T]
with initial condition
h(0)=0
such that on
M_{o}\times[0,T]
,
|h(t)|_{g_{o}}\leq Cr_{o}^{-\sigma}
for some uniform-in-time positive constants
C>0
and
\sigma\in(0,1)
.

If
h(0)\equiv 0
on
\mathbb{R}^{4}\backslash\{0\}
, then
h(t)\equiv 0
for
t\in[0,T]
.

Proof.

Proposition
5.14
can be proved along the same lines as Proposition
5.6
by invoking Theorem
5.5
. The only subtlety here is that Theorem
5.5
only applies to complete metrics and smooth subsolutions to the heat equation. A straightforward adaptation of its proof shows that it still holds for subsolutions to the heat equation on
M_{o}
satisfying the growth
|h(t)|_{g_{o}}\leq Cr_{o}^{-\sigma}
with
\sigma<\frac{n-2}{2}=1
here. Note that we do not need the assumption
\mathbf{R}^{+}<0
here.

∎

We also have a similar statement for ancient flow whose proof is a little more complicated relying on the following formalism. Recall the canonical identification between
\Lambda^{-}\otimes\Lambda^{+}
and
\operatorname{Sym}^{2}_{0}(M_{o})
through
\alpha^{-}\otimes\beta^{+}\mapsto\alpha^{-}\circ\beta^{-}
where
(\alpha^{-}\circ\beta^{-})_{ij}={(\alpha^{-})_{i}}^{k}(\beta^{+})_{kj}
is the usual composition from (
2.4
). Denote
d_{-}:\Lambda^{1}\otimes\Lambda^{+}\to\Lambda^{-}\otimes\Lambda^{+}
the composition of the covariant exterior derivative on
\Lambda^{1}\otimes\Lambda^{+}
and the projection
\Lambda^{2}\otimes\Lambda^{+}\to\Lambda^{-}\otimes\Lambda^{+}
on the anti-selfdual part. The action of the selfdual curvature
\mathbf{R}^{+}:\Lambda^{+}\to\Lambda^{+}
extends to an action on
\operatorname{Sym}^{2}_{0}(M_{o})
through
\mathbf{R}^{+}(\alpha^{-}\circ\beta^{+})=\alpha^{-}\circ\mathbf{R}^{+}(\beta^{+})
.

Proposition 5.15
.

Let
(h(t))_{t\in(-\infty,0]}
be a solution to the homogeneous heat equation
\partial_{t}h=(\Delta_{L,g_{o}}+2\Lambda)h
on
M_{o}\times(-\infty,0]
such that on
M_{o}\times(-\infty,0]
,
|h(t)|_{g_{o}}\leq Cr_{o}^{-\sigma}
for some uniform-in-time positive constants
C>0
and
\sigma\in(0,1)
.

Then
h(t)\equiv 0
for
t\in(-\infty,0]
.

Proof.

Since the pure trace part is easily dealt with (since
\Lambda<0
), and
\Delta_{L,g_{o}}+2\Lambda
preserves the pure trace and traceless decomposition, we will focus on the case where
h
is traceless.

The key point here is that a Weitzenböck formula from
[
BR09
, 4.21]
, (see also
[
BO23
, (4)]
and
[
NO24
]
) states that on a traceless symmetric
2
-tensor
h\in\operatorname{Sym}^{2}_{0}(M_{o})
, one has

(5.2)

(\Delta_{L,g_{o}}+2\Lambda)h=-2(d_{-}d_{-}^{*}-\mathbf{R}^{+})h,

hence for any
2
-tensor
h
on
M_{o}
. Now, if
\sigma\in(0,1)
, then one has:

(5.3)

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{M_{o}}|h(t)|^{2}_{g_{o}}\,d\mu_{g_{o}}=\int_{M_{o}}\langle(\Delta_{L,g_{o}}+2\Lambda)h,\,h\rangle_{g_{o}}\,d\mu_{g_{o}}\leq 2\lambda\int_{M_{o}}|h(t)|^{2}_{g_{o}}\,d\mu_{g_{o}}\leq 0,

for
\lambda<0
the least negative eigenvalue of
\mathbf{R}^{+}
where the integration by parts is allowed as long as
|\nabla^{g_{o},\,l}h(t)|_{g_{o}}\leq Cr_{o}^{-\sigma-l}
for
\sigma<1
.

The quantity
A(t):=\int_{M_{o}}|h(t)|^{2}_{g_{o}}\,d\mu_{g_{o}}
therefore satisfies
A^{\prime}(t)\leq 4\lambda A(t)
. Note that in the case of an initial condition
h(0)=0
, this proves the result since
A(0)=0
and
A(t)\geq 0
. For an ancient flow
(h(t))_{t\in(-\infty,0]}
it implies that
A(t_{0})e^{4\lambda t_{0}}\leq A(t)e^{4\lambda t}\leq Ce^{4\lambda t}
for
t\leq t_{0}
by the uniform assumption
|h(t)|_{g_{o}}\leq Cr_{o}^{-\sigma}
. This implies that
A(t_{0})=0
, i.e.
h(t_{0})=0
for all
t_{0}\leq 0
by sending
t
to
-\infty
.
∎

5.3.
On Euclidean space

We state a Liouville-type result for the homogenous heat equation on Euclidean space proved in
[
BK17
, Proposition
5.3
]
:

Proposition 5.16
.

Let
(h(t))_{t\,<\,0}
be a solution to
\partial_{t}h=\Delta_{\mathbf{e}}h
on
\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\{0\}\times(-\infty,0)
such that on
\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\{0\}\times(-\infty,0)
,
|h(x,t)|\leq C|x|^{-\sigma}
, for some uniform-in-time positive constants
C
and
\sigma\in(0,2)
.

Then
h(t)=0
on
\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\{0\}\times(-\infty,0)
.

The Liouville-type result for the homogenous heat equation on Euclidean space proved in
[
BK17
, Proposition
5.3
]
by interpolation with the heat kernel extends directly to the simpler case of initial condition zero and yields the following statement.

Proposition 5.17
.

Let
(h(t))_{t\in[0,T]}
be a solution to
\partial_{t}h=\Delta_{\mathbf{e}}h
on
\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\{0\}\times[0,T]
such that on
\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\{0\}\times[0,T]
,
|h(x,t)|\leq C|x|^{-\sigma}
, for some uniform-in-time positive constants
C
and
\sigma\in(0,2)
.

If
h(0)\equiv 0
, then
h(t)=0
on
\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\{0\}\times[0,T]
.

6.
Linear theory: the unconstrained case

6.1.
Definition and first properties

We now define the weighted Hölder spaces which will be used along the article. We start by defining their
C^{0}
counterpart.

•

For
T<0
, a smooth function
\gamma:(-\infty,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
, and
\sigma>0
,

\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}:=\sup\left\{\gamma(t)\rho(x,t)^{\sigma}|h(x,t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\quad|\quad t\in(-\infty,T],\,x\in M\right\}.

The use of
\varepsilon(t)
or
\varepsilon_{0}(t)
leads to uniform equivalent norms thanks to
(

H
0

)
.

•

Let us now define our weighted Hölder norms.

For a tensor
(h(t))_{t\,\leq\,T}
, define the following semi-norm:

[h]_{\alpha,x,t}:=\rho(x,t)^{2\alpha}\sup_{(y,t^{\prime})\in P_{\rho}(x,t)\setminus\{(x,t)\}}\frac{|(P_{x,y}^{t})^{*}h(y,t^{\prime})-h(x,t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}}{(d_{\tilde{g}(t)}(y,x)^{2}+|t^{\prime}-t|)^{\alpha}},

where
P_{\rho}(x,t):=B_{\tilde{g}(t)}(x,\rho_{0}(x,t))\times(t-\rho_{0}(x,t)^{2},t]
and
\rho_{0}(x,t):=\iota_{0}\rho(x,t)=\iota_{0}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o}(x))

with
\iota_{0}>0
a positive constant such that
1/4\mathop{\rm inj}\nolimits_{x}(\tilde{g}(t))\leq\iota_{0}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o}(x))\leq 1/2\mathop{\rm inj}\nolimits_{x}(\tilde{g}(t))
for all
(x,t)\in M\times(-\infty,T]
. Here
P_{x,y}^{t}
denotes parallel transport along the unique minimizing geodesic with respect to
\tilde{g}(t)
joining
x
to
y
. For ease of reading, we will avoid the dependency of
P_{\rho}(x,t)
on
(x,t)
and will simply denote such a parabolic neighborhood by
P_{\rho}
whenever there is no source of confusion.

•

This allows us to define the space
C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
:

\begin{split}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}:=&\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+\sup_{(x,t)\in M\times(-\infty,T]}\gamma(t)(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o}(x))^{\sigma}[h]_{\alpha,x,t}.\\
\|h\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}:=&\sum_{0\,\leq\,j\,\leq\,2}\|\nabla^{\tilde{g},\,j}h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,j+\sigma,T}}+\|\partial_{t}h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.\end{split}

By a slight abuse of notation, we will use the same notation for norms on immortal flows obtained with the same definition, up to flipping the sign
t\mapsto-t
,
T\mapsto-T
. This will make some statements more streamlined as they will be valid for both ancient and immortal flows. When it matters, it will be clear from the context which of the spaces is used from the sign of
t
and
T
.

More precisely,

•

the first norm extends as follows to tensors defined on
[T,+\infty)\times M
.

For
T>0
, a smooth function
\gamma:[T,+\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
, and
\sigma>0
,

\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}:=\sup\left\{\gamma(t)\rho(x,t)^{\sigma}|h(x,t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\quad|\quad t\in[T,+\infty),\,x\in M\right\}.

•

For a tensor
(h(t))_{t\,\geq\,T}
, define the following semi-norm:

[h]_{\alpha,x,t}:=\rho(x,t)^{2\alpha}\sup_{(y,t^{\prime})\in P_{\rho}(x,t)\setminus\{(x,t)\}}\frac{|(P_{x,y}^{t})^{*}h(y,t^{\prime})-h(x,t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}}{(d_{\tilde{g}(t)}(y,x)^{2}+|t^{\prime}-t|)^{\alpha}},

where
P_{\rho}(x,t):=B_{\tilde{g}(t)}(x,\rho_{0}(x,t))\times(\max\{0,t-\rho_{0}(x,t)^{2}\},t]
and
\rho_{0}(x,t):=\iota_{0}\rho(x,t)=\iota_{0}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o}(x))
with
\iota_{0}>0
a positive constant such that
1/4\mathop{\rm inj}\nolimits_{x}(\tilde{g}(t))\leq\iota_{0}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o}(x))\leq 1/2\mathop{\rm inj}\nolimits_{x}(\tilde{g}(t))
for all
(x,t)\in M\times[T,+\infty)
. Here
P_{x,y}^{t}
denotes parallel transport along the unique minimizing geodesic with respect to
\tilde{g}(t)
joining
x
to
y
.

From now on, we assume
\gamma:(-\infty,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
(respectively
\gamma:[T,+\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
)

(
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}
)

\begin{split}\gamma(t):=\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-\gamma_{0}},\quad t\leq T,\quad{\text{respectively $t\geq T$},}\end{split}

for some
\gamma_{0}>0
which will be chosen in Section
8.3
.

6.2.
Weighted Schauder estimates

We now prove Schauder estimates in the above weighted Hölder spaces.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
,
\sigma\in(0,2)
and
\gamma
satisfying (

H
γ

). Then there exists
C>0
such that if
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
and
\psi\in C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
are
2
-tensors on
(-\infty,T]\times M
satisfying

(6.1)

\partial_{t}h-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}h-2\Lambda h=\psi,

then, we have the following control

\|h\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\leq C\Big(\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\Big).

Remark 6.2
.

The constraint on the weight
\gamma
is consistent with the setting of the present paper. An inspection of the proof of Proposition
6.1
shows that it suffices to assume that there is
C>0
such that
|\gamma^{\prime}(t)|\leq C\gamma(t)
for
t\leq T
.

Proof.

We prove the result in the ancient case first.

Fix
(x_{0},t_{0})\in M\times(-\infty,T]
and define
\rho_{0}:=\iota_{0}(\varepsilon(t_{0})+r_{o}(x_{0}))
. Consider the rescaled tensor for
\sigma>0
and
(y,s)\in B_{\tilde{g}(t_{0})}(x_{0},\rho_{0})\times(-1,0]
:

\tilde{g}_{0}(y,s):=\rho_{0}^{-2}\tilde{g}(y,\rho_{0}^{2}s+t_{0}),\quad h_{0}(y,s):=\rho_{0}^{\sigma-2}h(y,\rho_{0}^{2}s+t_{0}),\quad\psi_{0}(y,s):=\rho_{0}^{\sigma}\psi(y,\rho_{0}^{2}s+t_{0}).

Thanks to (
6.1
) and the scaling properties of the curvature tensor, observe that the above triple of tensors satisfies on
B_{\tilde{g}_{0}(0)}(x_{0},1)\times(-1,0]
:

(6.2)

\partial_{s}h_{0}-\Delta_{\tilde{g}_{0}(s)\,,\,L}h_{0}-2\Lambda\rho_{0}^{2}h_{0}=\psi_{0}.

Now, the curvature estimates of Lemma
3.4
and the fact that
\mathop{\rm inj}\nolimits_{x_{0}}(\tilde{g}_{0}(0))\geq 2>0
ensures that the metric
\tilde{g}_{0}(0)
is uniformly equivalent to the Euclidean metric through the exponential map
\exp_{x_{0}}^{\tilde{g}_{0}(0)}.
The same holds for higher (Euclidean) derivatives of the metric
\tilde{g}_{0}(0)
in this chart. Moreover, the estimates on the covariant derivatives of the time derivative
\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)
from Lemma
3.8
shows that the coefficients of the parabolic equation (
6.2
) are uniformly
C^{1}
in time. These regularity properties allow us to apply standard parabolic Schauder interior estimates that can be found in
[
Kry96
, Section
8.11
]
: for
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
, there exists a positive constant
C(n,\alpha)>0
such that

(6.3)

\|h_{0}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(B_{\mathbf{e}}(0,1/2)\times(-1/2,0])}\leq C\left(\|h_{0}\|_{C^{0}(B_{\mathbf{e}}(0,1)\times(-1,0])}+\|\psi_{0}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}(B_{\mathbf{e}}(0,1)\times(-1,0])}\right),

where
B_{\mathbf{e}}(0,r)
denotes the Euclidean ball centered at the origin of radius
r>0.
Here, the norms are the standard Hölder parabolic norms as defined in
[
Kry96
, Section
8.5
]
. Unravelling the definitions of the rescaled tensors
\tilde{g}_{0}(s)
,
h_{0}(s)
and
\psi_{0}(s)
gives the expected Schauder estimate once (
6.3
) gets multiplied by
\gamma(t_{0})
and once the supremum is taken on
(x_{0},t_{0})\in M\times(-\infty,T]
. This ends the proof in the ancient case.

The proof of the corresponding estimate is totally analogous in the immortal case: one has to invoke
[
Kry96
, Section
9.2
]
instead to get the standard Schauder estimate for parabolic equations with the desired regularity on the coefficients.
∎

6.3.
Linear controls of solutions orthogonal to the approximate kernel in the ancient case

The main result of this section is the following a priori
C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
- estimate on any solution to a non-homogeneous heat equation with data lying in
C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
.

Proposition 6.3

(Linear estimates in weighted Hölder spaces)
.

Under the assumption and notations of (

H
0

), let
(\tilde{g}(t))_{t\,\leq\,T}
be the associated curve of metrics defined with respect to
\zeta(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
and
\delta(t)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Let
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
,
\sigma\in(0,1)
and
\gamma
as in (

H
γ

). Then there exists
T<0
and
C>0
such that the following holds. Assume that

(1)

h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
and
\psi\in C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
satisfy on
M\times(-\infty,T]

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)=\psi(t),

(2)

h(t)
is
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal to the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
introduced in Definition
3.12
for all
t\in(-\infty,T]
.

Then one has the a priori estimate:

(6.4)

\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\leq C\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.

Remark 6.4
.

An important additional assumption when dealing with other Kronheimer’s instantons is that we will want
\zeta(t)/|\zeta(t)|
to stay close to
{\zeta_{0}}/|{\zeta_{0}}|
to prevent the formation of further orbifold singularities.

Indeed, if the parameter
\zeta(t)
is uncontrolled, new orbifold singularities may form and make the analysis more difficult. Luckily, we will see in Lemma
8.1
from Section
8
that we can always choose gluing directions in which the assumptions of this proposition are satisfied.

Proof.

Let us argue by contradiction and assume that for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
, there is a sequence
T_{i}\to-\infty
so that we have:

•

h_{i}\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T_{i}}
and
\psi_{i}\in C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T_{i}}
satisfy on
M\times(-\infty,T_{i}]
,

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h_{i}(t)=\psi_{i}(t),

•

h_{i}(t)
is
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal to the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for all
t\in(-\infty,T_{i}]
,

•

\|h_{i}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T_{i}}}=1
and
\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}\|\psi_{i}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T_{i}}}=0
.

By that last point, we can pick
(x_{i},t_{i})\in M\times(-\infty,T_{i}]
such that

(6.5)

\gamma(t_{i})(\varepsilon_{0}(t_{i})+r_{o}(x_{i}))^{\sigma}|h_{i}(x_{i},t_{i})|_{\tilde{g}(t_{i})}\geq\frac{1}{2}.

There are three situations to consider depending on where a subsequence of
(x_{i},t_{i})
ends up compared to our different regions of
(M,\tilde{g}(t))_{t\,\leq\,T_{i}}
:

(1)

\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}(\varepsilon_{0}(t_{i}))^{-1}r_{o}(x_{i})<+\infty
,

(2)

\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}(\varepsilon_{0}(t_{i}))^{-1}r_{o}(x_{i})=+\infty
and
\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}r_{o}(x_{i})=0
,

(3)

or
\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}(\varepsilon_{0}(t_{i}))^{-1}r_{o}(x_{i})=+\infty
and
\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}r_{o}(x_{i})>0
.

First case:
\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}\varepsilon(t_{i})^{-1}r_{o}(x_{i})<\infty
.

In that case, we define the following parabolic rescalings for
t\in\left(-\infty,\frac{T_{i}-t_{i}}{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}}\right]
by recalling that
\varepsilon(t)^{2}:=|\zeta(t)|
,

•

\tilde{x}_{i}:=s_{\zeta(t_{i})}^{-1}(x_{i})
,

•

\tilde{g}_{i}(t):=|\varepsilon(t_{i})|^{-2}s_{\zeta(t_{i})}^{*}\tilde{g}(t_{i}+\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}t)
,

•

\tilde{h}_{i}(t):=\gamma(t_{i})\varepsilon(t_{i})^{\sigma-2}s_{\zeta(t_{i})}^{*}h_{i}(t_{i}+\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}t)
, and

•

\tilde{\psi}_{i}(t):=\gamma(t_{i})\varepsilon(t_{i})^{\sigma}s_{\zeta(t_{i})}^{*}\psi_{i}(t_{i}+\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}t)
.

These satisfy:
(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{i}(t)}-2\Lambda\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2})\tilde{h}_{i}(t)=\tilde{\psi}_{i}(t),
as well as the suitably rescaled orthogonality conditions.

After taking a limit as
i\to+\infty
, up to the action of a diffeomorphism,
\tilde{g}_{i}(t)\to g_{b}

in (space-time)
C^{2,\alpha}_{loc}
by Lemma
3.4
. Now, the tensors
\tilde{\psi}_{i}
are uniformly bounded in
C^{0,\alpha}_{loc}
, so that by parabolic regularity, the solutions
\tilde{h}_{i}
are uniformly bounded in (space-time)
C^{2,\alpha}_{loc}
for any
0<\alpha<1/2
. By compactness, we therefore find a time-dependent
2
-tensor
\hat{h}
in
C^{2,\alpha}_{loc}
on
N\times\left(-\infty,0\right]\subset N\times\left(-\infty,\lim_{i}\frac{T_{i}-t_{i}}{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}}\right]
satisfying

(1+r_{b}(\hat{x}))^{\sigma}|\hat{h}(\hat{x},0)|_{g_{b}}\geq\frac{1}{2},

and for all
(x,t)
in
N\times\left(-\infty,0\right]
,

(6.6)

(1+r_{b}(x))^{\sigma}|\hat{h}(x,t)|_{g_{b}}\leq 1.

Indeed, since
r_{o}=\varepsilon(t)(s_{\zeta(t)})_{\ast}r_{b}
and
\gamma(t)(\varepsilon_{0}(t)+r_{o}(x))^{\sigma}|h_{i}(x,t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\leq 1
, we therefore have the following control over the rescaled
\tilde{h}_{i}
thanks to the definition of
\tilde{g}_{i}(t)
: for
t\leq 0
,

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{h}_{i}(x,t)\right|_{\tilde{g}_{i}(t)}

\displaystyle=\gamma(t_{i})\varepsilon(t_{i})^{\sigma-2}\left|s_{\zeta(t_{i})}^{*}h_{i}(x,t_{i}+\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}t)\right|_{\tilde{g}_{i}(t)}

\displaystyle=\gamma(t_{i})\varepsilon(t_{i})^{\sigma-2}s_{\zeta(t_{i})}^{*}\left|h_{i}(x,t_{i}+\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}t)\right|_{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{-2}\tilde{g}(t_{i}+\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}t)}

\displaystyle=\gamma(t_{i})\varepsilon(t_{i})^{\sigma}s_{\zeta(t_{i})}^{*}\left|h_{i}(x,t_{i}+\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}t)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t_{i}+\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}t)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma(t_{i})}{\gamma(t_{i}+\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}t)(1+r_{b}(x))^{\sigma}}\leq\frac{1}{(1+r_{b}(x))^{\sigma}}.

Here we have used (

H
γ

) in the last inequality.

Moreover, it satisfies on
N\times\left(-\infty,0\right]
,

(6.7)

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,g_{b}})\hat{h}(t)=0.

By the very construction of
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
, any rescaled element from the
(\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}(t))_{j}
decays like
(1+r_{b})^{-4}
uniformly along the sequence with respect to the rescaled metrics
\tilde{g}_{i}(t)
while
\tilde{h}_{i}=O((1+r_{b})^{-\sigma})
uniformly along the sequence as checked in (
6.6
). In particular, this implies that the orthogonality conditions (
2
) are preserved at the limit, i.e. the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem implies that for all
j\in\{1,2,3\}
:

\int_{N}\langle\hat{h}(t),\mathbf{o}_{j}\rangle_{g_{b}}\,d\mu_{g_{b}}=0,\quad t\leq 0.

This means that
\hat{h}
is
L^{2}(g_{b})
-orthogonal to the
L^{2}
-kernel of
\Delta_{L,g_{b}}
. This contradicts our Liouville Theorem, Proposition
5.3
.

Second case:
\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}\varepsilon_{0}(t_{i})^{-1}r_{o}(x_{i})=\infty
and
\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}r_{o}(x_{i})=0
.

In that case, we define the following parabolic rescalings: for
t\in\left(-\infty,\frac{T_{i}-t_{i}}{r_{o}(x_{i})^{2}}\right]
, denote
\phi_{s}:\mathbb{R}^{4}/\Gamma\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{4}/\Gamma
the diffeomorphism sending
(r,x)
to
(sr,x)
,

•

\tilde{x}_{i}:=\phi_{r_{o}(x_{i})}^{-1}(x_{i})
,

•

\tilde{g}_{i}(t):=r_{o}(x_{i})^{-2}\phi_{r_{o}(x_{i})}^{*}\tilde{g}(t_{i}+r_{o}(x_{i})^{2}t)
,

•

\tilde{h}_{i}(t):=\gamma(t_{i})r_{o}(x_{i})^{\sigma-2}\phi_{r_{o}(x_{i})}^{*}h_{i}(t_{i}+r_{o}(x_{i})^{2}t)
, and

•

\tilde{\psi}_{i}(t):=\gamma(t_{i})r_{o}(x_{i})^{\sigma}\phi_{r_{o}(x_{i})}^{*}\psi_{i}(t_{i}+r_{o}(x_{i})^{2}t)
.

These satisfy
(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{i}(t)}-2\Lambda r_{o}(x_{i})^{2})\tilde{h}_{i}(t)=\tilde{\psi}_{i}(t).

In this case, after taking a limit as
i\to+\infty
,
\tilde{g}_{i}(t)\to\mathbf{e}
smoothly locally and we find a time-dependent
2
-tensor
\hat{h}
on
\mathbb{R}^{4}/\penalty\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\left(-\infty,\lim_{i}\frac{T_{i}-t_{i}}{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}}\right]
satisfying

r^{\sigma}|\hat{h}(\hat{x},0)|_{\mathbf{e}}\geq\frac{1}{2},

and for all
(x,t)
in
\mathbb{R}^{4}/\penalty\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\left(-\infty,\lim_{i}\frac{T_{i}-t_{i}}{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}}\right]
,

r^{\sigma}|\hat{h}(x,t)|_{\mathbf{e}}\leq 1.

This contradicts the Liouville Theorem from Proposition
5.16
because
\sigma\in(0,2)
.

Third case:
\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}r_{o}(x_{i})>0
.

In that case, we do not need to rescale. Indeed, we only need to translate time by
t_{i}
, i.e. we define
\tilde{g}_{i}(t):=\tilde{g}(t_{i}+t)
and similarly for the solution
h
and the tensor
\psi
.

As
i\to+\infty
,
\tilde{g}_{i}(t)\to g_{o}
for all
t\in(-\infty,0]
smoothly locally on
M_{o}
outside the orbifold points and we find a time-dependent
2
-tensor
\hat{h}
on
M_{o}\times\left(-\infty,0\right]
satisfying

r^{\sigma}|\hat{h}(\hat{x},0)|_{g_{o}}\geq\frac{1}{2},

and for all
(x,t)
in
M_{o}\times\left(-\infty,0\right]
,

(6.8)

r^{\sigma}|\hat{h}(x,t)|_{g_{o}}\leq 1.

As in the first case, an application of the dominated convergence theorem shows that the orthogonality conditions are satisfied. Indeed, the elements of the
L^{2}
-kernel of the orbifold
like
\tilde{c}
are bounded close to the singularity and
\hat{h}
satisfies (
6.8
) with
\sigma<1<4
.
This contradicts the Liouville Theorem from Proposition
5.9
and proves the proposition.
∎

6.4.
Linear control of solutions orthogonal to the kernel in the immortal case

Proposition 6.5

(Linear estimates in weighted Hölder spaces)
.

Under the assumption and notations of (

H
0

), let
(\tilde{g}(t))_{t\geq T}
be the associated curve of metrics defined with respect to
\zeta(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
and
\delta(t)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Let
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
,
\sigma\in(0,1)
and
\gamma
as in (

H
γ

). Then there exists
T>0
and
C>0
such that the following holds. Assume that

(1)

h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
and
\psi\in C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
satisfy on
M\times[T,+\infty)

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)=\psi(t),\quad h(T)\equiv 0,

(2)

h(t)
is
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal to the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
introduced in Definition
3.12
for all
t\in[T,+\infty)
.

Then one has the a priori estimate:

(6.9)

\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\leq C\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.

Proof.

Let us argue by contradiction and assume that for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
, there is a sequence
T_{i}\to+\infty
so that we have:

•

h_{i}\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T_{i}}
with
h_{i}(T_{i})=0
and
\psi_{i}\in C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T_{i}}
satisfy on
M\times[T_{i},+\infty)
,

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h_{i}(t)=\psi_{i}(t),

•

h_{i}(t)
is
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal to the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for all
t\in[T_{i},+\infty)
,

•

\|h_{i}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T_{i}}}=1
and
\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}\|\psi_{i}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T_{i}}}=0
.

By that last point, we can pick
(x_{i},t_{i})\in M\times[T_{i},+\infty)
such that

(6.10)

\gamma(t_{i})(\varepsilon(t_{i})+r_{o}(x_{i}))^{\sigma}|h_{i}(x_{i},t_{i})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\geq\frac{1}{2}.

As in Proposition (
6.3
) which dealt with the ancient case, there are three situations to consider depending on where a subsequence of
(x_{i},t_{i})
ends up compared to our different regions of
(M,g_{i}(t))
:

(1)

\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}(\varepsilon(t_{i}))^{-1}r_{o}(x_{i})<+\infty
,

(2)

\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}(\varepsilon(t_{i}))^{-1}r_{o}(x_{i})=+\infty
and
\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}r_{o}(x_{i})=0
,

(3)

or
\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}(\varepsilon(t_{i}))^{-1}r_{o}(x_{i})=+\infty
and
\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}r_{o}(x_{i})>0
.

In each situation, we will take the same rescalings, reparametrizations and limits as in the proof of Proposition (
6.3
).

The difference with the ancient case is that we will also have further cases:

•

either
t_{i}
stays comparable to
T_{i}
in the sense that
\frac{t_{i}-T_{i}}{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}}\geq 0
stays bounded, hence we still “see” the initial condition
h_{i}(T_{i})=0
and may use the Liouville Theorems from Propositions from Propositions
5.6
,
5.14
,
5.17
,

•

or
t_{i}
grows much faster than
T_{i}
, and we obtain an ancient (and even eternal) flow for which we may use the Liouville Theorems from Propositions
5.3
,
5.15
,
5.16
.

First case:
\lim\varepsilon(t_{i})^{-1}r_{o}(x_{i})<\infty
.

As in the proof of Proposition
6.3
that corresponds to the ancient case, after parabolic rescaling, we find a time-dependent
2
-tensor
\hat{h}
in
C^{2,\alpha}_{loc}
on
N\times\left(\lim_{i}\frac{T_{i}-t_{i}}{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}},+\infty\right)
satisfying

(1+r_{b}(\hat{x}))^{\sigma}|\hat{h}(\hat{x},0)|_{g_{b}}\geq\frac{1}{2},

and for all
(x,t)
in
N\times\left(\lim_{i}\frac{T_{i}-t_{i}}{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}},+\infty\right)
,

(6.11)

(1+r_{b}(x))^{\sigma}|\hat{h}(x,t)|_{g_{b}}\leq 1.

Moreover, it satisfies on
N\times\left(\lim_{i}\frac{T_{i}-t_{i}}{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}},+\infty\right)
,

(6.12)

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,g_{b}})\hat{h}(t)=0.

First, if
T_{\infty}:=\lim_{i}\frac{T_{i}-t_{i}}{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}}>-\infty
, observe that by Lemma
3.8
, for
t\geq\frac{T_{i}-t_{i}}{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}}
,

(6.13)

|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}_{i}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{i}(t)}=\left(s_{\zeta(t_{i})}^{*}|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}|_{\tilde{g}}\right)(\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}t+t_{i})\leq C.

Here we have not used the full decay in space of Lemma
3.8
. Estimate (
6.13
) implies in particular that for
t\geq\frac{T_{i}-t_{i}}{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}}
:

(6.14)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}|\tilde{h}_{i}|^{2}_{\tilde{g}_{i}}&\leq 2|\partial_{t}\tilde{h}_{i}|_{\tilde{g}_{i}}|\tilde{h}_{i}|_{\tilde{g}_{i}}+C|\tilde{h}_{i}|_{\tilde{g}_{i}}^{2}\\
&\leq\frac{C}{(1+r_{b})^{2+\sigma}}|\tilde{h}_{i}|_{\tilde{g}_{i}}+C|\tilde{h}_{i}|_{\tilde{g}_{i}}^{2}\\
&\leq C,\end{split}

where
C
denotes a positive constant that may vary from line to line. The penultimate inequality is obtained by unravelling the definitions of the rescaled tensors
\tilde{h}_{i}
together with Proposition
6.1
. The last inequality is simply obtained by invoking the boundedness of
\tilde{h}_{i}
on the region under consideration. Therefore, integrating (
6.14
) implies the following uniform estimate (with respect to the index
i
):

|\tilde{h}_{i}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{i}(t)}\leq C\left(t-\frac{T_{i}-t_{i}}{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}}\right),\quad t\geq\frac{T_{i}-t_{i}}{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}}.

As
i
goes to
+\infty
, we therefore get

(6.15)

|\hat{h}(t)|_{g_{b}}\leq C(t-T_{\infty}),\quad\text{ for $t\geq T_{\infty}$ uniformly in space on $N$.}

Therefore the growth (
6.15
) together with (
6.12
) enables us to use the Liouville Theorem from Proposition
5.6
to derive that
\hat{h}=0
which leads to a contradiction.

Second, if
\lim_{i}\frac{T_{i}-t_{i}}{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}}=-\infty
, then we have an ancient solution to (
6.12
).
By the construction of
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
, any rescaled element decays like
(1+r_{b})^{-4}
uniformly along the sequence with respect to the rescaled metrics
\tilde{g}_{i}(t)
while
|\tilde{h}_{i}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{i}(t)}=O((1+r_{b})^{-\sigma})
uniformly along the sequence as checked in (
6.11
). In particular, this implies that the orthogonality conditions are preserved at the limit, i.e. the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem implies for all
j\in\{1,2,3\}
:

\int_{N}\langle\hat{h}(t),\mathbf{o}_{j}(t)\rangle_{g_{b}}\,d\mu_{g_{b}}=0,\quad t\geq\lim_{i}\frac{T_{i}-t_{i}}{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}}.

in this region we also similarly satisfy the orthogonality conditions against the kernel of
\Delta_{L,g_{b}}
.

These facts contradict the Liouville Theorem from Proposition
5.3
.

The other cases are dealt with in a similar way: the blow-ups are as in the proof of Proposition
6.3
and the limits of
\lim_{i}\frac{T_{i}-t_{i}}{\varepsilon(t_{i})^{2}}
determine whether we use the Liouville Theorems with initial conditions from Propositions
5.14
and
5.17
or the Liouville Theorem for ancient solutions from Propositions
5.15
and
5.16
.
∎

6.5.
Estimates on Hölder norms of elements of the approximate kernel

We end this section by recalling how to estimate the previously introduced Hölder norm in Section
6.1
for a tensor times a function and contractions of tensors. The proof is straightforward from the definition and Lemma
D.6
.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
,
(S(t))_{t\,\leq\,T}
be a tensor on
M
and let
f:(-\infty,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a function. Then,
if
\sigma>0
,

\begin{split}\|f\cdot S\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}&\leq\|f\cdot S\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+\sup_{(x,t)\in M\times(-\infty,T]}\gamma(t)\sup_{t-\rho_{0}(x,t)^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|f(t^{\prime})|\rho(x,t)^{\sigma}[S]_{\alpha,x,t}\\
&\quad+\sup_{(x,t)\in M\times(-\infty,T]}\gamma(t)[f]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\rho(x,t)^{\sigma}|S(x,t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}.\end{split}

Finally, if
(S_{i}(t))_{t\,\leq\,T}
,
i=1,2
, denotes two families of tensors such that
S_{i}\in C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma_{i},T}
for
\sigma_{i}>0
then:

\begin{split}\|S_{1}\ast S_{2}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma^{2},\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2},T}}&\leq C\|S_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma_{1},T}}\|S_{2}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma_{2},T}}+C\|S_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma_{1},T}}\|S_{2}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma_{2},T}}.\end{split}

The next proposition estimates the Hölder norms of each element of the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
:

Proposition 6.7
.

Let
\sigma\in(0,1)
,
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
and let
f:(-\infty,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a function. Then the following general
C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
-estimates hold:

\begin{split}\|f\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq\|f\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\sup_{t\in(-\infty,T]}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{2+\sigma}\sup_{t-4\delta(t)^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|f(t^{\prime})|\\
&\quad+\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}\rho^{-2+\sigma}[f]_{\alpha,t,\rho},\\
\|f\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq\|f\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sup_{t-c_{0}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|f(t^{\prime})|\\
&\quad+\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\sup_{M}\rho^{2+\sigma}[f]_{\alpha,t,\rho},\\
\|f\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq\|f\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\sup_{t-c_{0}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|f(t^{\prime})|\\
&\quad+\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\sup_{M}\rho^{2+\sigma}[f]_{\alpha,t,\rho},\end{split}

for some uniform positive constant
c_{0}
.

Proof.

The proof follows from Lemma
6.6
in combination with Lemma
3.10
(estimates holding on
\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
and
\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
), Lemma
3.8
(estimate holding on
\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)
) and Lemma
3.11
(estimates holding on
\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{c}(t)
and
\partial_{t}\tilde{c}(t)
).

∎

7.
Linear theory: the constrained case

7.1.
Decoupling the linearized equation

The purpose of this section is to explain our strategy to solve at a heuristic level and discuss different possible strategies that we have not explored but which might sound plausible.
We start with the following straightforward but crucial lemma:

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
(M,g(t))_{t}
be a one-parameter
C^{1}
family of
C^{2}
Riemannian metrics on a closed manifold
M
and let
(S(t))_{t}
and
(T(t))_{t}
be two families of
C^{2}
tensors on
M
. Then:

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}&\langle S(t),T(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(g(t))}=\int_{M}\left\langle\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,g(t)}\right)S(t),T(t)\right\rangle_{g(t)}\,d\mu_{g(t)}\\
&+\int_{M}\left\langle S(t),\left(\partial_{t}+\Delta_{L,\,g(t)}+\frac{\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g(t)}\partial_{t}g(t)}{2}\operatorname{Id}-\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits(\partial_{t}g(t)\circ\cdot)\right)T(t)\right\rangle_{g(t)}\,d\mu_{g(t)},\end{split}

where in coordinates, one has
\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits(a\circ b)_{ij}=g^{pq}a_{ip}b_{qj}+g^{pq}b_{ip}a_{qj}
.

In particular, if
S(t)
satisfies
\partial_{t}S(t)-\Delta_{L,g(t)}S(t)=\psi(t)
for some non-homogeneous data
\psi(t)
then:

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}&\langle S(t),T(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(g(t))}=\int_{M}\left\langle\psi(t),T(t)\right\rangle_{g(t)}\,d\mu_{g(t)}\\
&+\int_{M}\left\langle S(t),\left(\partial_{t}+\Delta_{L,\,g(t)}+\frac{\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g(t)}\partial_{t}g(t)}{2}\operatorname{Id}-\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits(\partial_{t}g(t)\circ\cdot)\right)T(t)\right\rangle_{g(t)}\,d\mu_{g(t)}.\end{split}

In the next section, we will estimate projections of a solution
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
orthogonal to the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
as defined in Definition
3.12
and satisfying for
t\leq T
,

(7.1)

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)-\psi(t)\in\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t),

A couple of remarks following the previous steps.

•

Motivated by Lemma
7.1
, we could solve alternatively a backward Cauchy problem:

\left\{\begin{aligned} 0&=\left(\partial_{t}+\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}+\frac{\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g(t)}\partial_{t}g(t)}{2}\operatorname{Id}-\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits(\partial_{t}g(t)\circ\cdot)\right)e_{i}(t),\\
e_{i}(t_{0})&=\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t_{0}),\quad\text{approximate "geometric" tensor at time $t_{0}$}.\end{aligned}\right.

in order to define the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
coming from the
L^{2}
-kernel of the Lichnerowicz operator of the Eguchi-Hanson metric (or more generally a hyperkähler ALE metric).

One advantage of this choice over the original one (
3.6
) is their canonical aspect. One drawback is that we will have to estimate these solutions and compare them with the first choice anyway.

•

Another final idea is that we could take different bases
(e_{i}(t))_{i}
and
(f_{i}(t))_{i}
for the kernel and for the cokernel: meaning that we would project
\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)
and the abstract solution
h(t)
on some basis
(e_{i}(t))_{i}
but we would then project
\psi
,
\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))
or
\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}h(t)
against some different basis
(f_{i}(t))_{i}
. Formally speaking, the kernel seems to naturally evolve by
\partial_{t}e_{i}(t)=\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}^{-1}(Q_{\tilde{g}(t)}(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t),e_{i}(t)))
and the cokernel satisfies: for all
h
,

\langle\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}h,f_{i}(t)\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}=0.

7.2.
Orthogonal projections for abstract data: pointwise estimates

The aim of this section is to establish pointwise estimates in time on orthogonal projections of the non-homogeneous data of the parabolic equation (
7.1
) against the elements of the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t).

This is echoing
[
BK17
, Proposition
6.5
]
in our setting.

We will deal with both the case of ancient solutions, the immortal case being completely analogous.

7.2.1.
Orthogonal projections against
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)

We start with a useful property between integral and pointwise norms:

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
\sigma\in(0,4)
and
\psi\in C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
. There exists
C>0
such that one has the following domination property in terms of the
C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
norm:

\left|\langle\psi(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}.

Moreover the
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal projection of a symmetric
2
-tensor
\psi\in{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}
on the line
\mathbb{R}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
and denoted by
L_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
, satisfies

|L_{i}(t)|:=\frac{\left|\langle\psi(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}\leq\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{-\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}},

and,

(7.2)

\|L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\leq C\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}.

Proof.

Using the very definition of the norm
C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
and the fact that
|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}=O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-4})
, one gets immediately the first expected estimate. Since
C^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\leq\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t)}^{2}\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4}
thanks to Proposition
4.2
, one also gets the corresponding estimate on
L_{i}(t)
.

Finally,

\gamma(t)(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{\sigma}|L_{i}(t)||\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-4+\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\leq C\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}},

where we have used the previous estimate on
L_{i}(t)
. This proves (
7.2
).
∎

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
h\in C^{2}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
,
\sigma\in(0,2)
, such that
h(t)
is
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal to the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
. Then,

\begin{split}\left|\langle\partial_{t}h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}.\end{split}

Proof.

The proof is the same for ancient and immortal flows.

By differentiating the orthogonality condition
\langle h(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=0
, one gets thanks to Lemma
C.4
:

\begin{split}\left|\langle\partial_{t}h(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&=\left|\Big\langle h(t),\Big(\partial_{t}+\frac{\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)}{2}\operatorname{Id}-\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)\circ\cdot)\Big)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq C\int_{M}|h(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\left(\left|\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}+|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\right)\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}.\end{split}

Since the tensor
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
is supported in
\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}
by definition, the previous integral on the righthand side can be estimated as follows. We first consider terms supported in
\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}
and we arrive at the following bounds

\begin{split}C\left(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}+\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{-\sigma^{-}}\right)\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma},\end{split}

since
\delta<1
by (

H
0

). Here we have used Lemmata
3.8
and
3.10
.
Similarly, terms supported in
\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}
can be bounded from above by:

\begin{split}C\int_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-4-\sigma}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}.\end{split}

∎

The next proposition estimates a priori the
L^{2}
-projection of the non-homogeneous data of the parabolic equation (
7.1
) against the elements
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
of the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t).

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
\psi\in C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
and let
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
such that
h(t)
is
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal to the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
(respectively
t\geq T
in the immortal case) and satisfying for all
t\leq T
(respectively
t\geq T
in the immortal case),

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)-\psi(t)\in\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t),

i.e.

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)=\psi(t)+\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t).

Then there exists a uniform positive constant
C
such that for
t\leq T
(respectively
t\geq T
in the immortal case),

(7.3)

\begin{split}\left|\langle\psi(t)+\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}|\nu(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}|\mu(t)|,\\
\varepsilon(t)^{4}|\lambda_{i}(t)|&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}|\nu(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}|\mu(t)|.\end{split}

In particular, there exists some uniform positive constant
C
such that:

\begin{split}\|\lambda_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\quad+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{2+\sigma}|\mu(t)|+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{2+\sigma}\delta(t)^{2}|\nu(t)|.\end{split}

Proof.

We provide a proof for ancient flows, but the statement also holds for immortal flows by flipping the sign of time. Indeed, the controls are always made on
\partial_{t}
and
\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}
separately so looking at
\partial_{t}+\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}
or
\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}
yields the same estimate.

Since
h(t)
is orthogonal to
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
, a spatial integration by parts yields:

\begin{split}\langle\psi(t)+\lambda_{j}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}(t)&+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=\Big\langle(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)})h(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&=\Big\langle\partial_{t}h(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}-\Big\langle h(t),\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}.\end{split}

In particular, Lemmata
7.3
and
3.10
give:

(7.4)

\begin{split}&\left|\langle\psi(t)+\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\leq\left|\langle\partial_{t}h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\\
&+C\int_{M}|h(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}|\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}+|\nu(t)|\left|\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\\
&+|\mu(t)|\left|\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|+\sum_{j\neq i}|\lambda_{j}(t)|\left|\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq C\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}+\varepsilon(t)^{-\sigma})\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}|\nu(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}|\mu(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4}\sum_{j\neq i}|\lambda_{j}(t)|\\
&\leq C\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}|\nu(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}|\mu(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4}\sum_{j\neq i}|\lambda_{j}(t)|,\end{split}

where
C
is a time-independent positive constant that may vary from line to line. Here we have invoked Proposition
4.2
and Proposition
4.5
in the penultimate inequality. Finally, (

H
0

) is used in the last inequality to ensure that
(2+\sigma)\delta\geq\sigma
since
\sigma\in(0,1)\subset(0,2)
and
\delta\geq 1/2
.

Now, Lemma
7.2
applied to
\psi(t)
with
\sigma+2
in lieu of
\sigma
leads to:

\begin{split}|\lambda_{i}(t)|\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}|\nu(t)|\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}|\mu(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4}\sum_{j\neq i}|\lambda_{j}(t)|+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.\end{split}

Since
\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}
is proportional to
\varepsilon(t)^{4}
thanks to Proposition
4.2
, one gets:

(7.5)

\begin{split}|\lambda_{i}(t)|&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}+C\delta(t)^{2}|\nu(t)|+C|\mu(t)|\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4}\sum_{j\neq i}|\lambda_{j}(t)|+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{-2-\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.\end{split}

By summing (
7.5
) over
i
and by absorption, one gets the second half of the first desired estimates, i.e.

(7.6)

\begin{split}\varepsilon(t)^{4}|\lambda_{i}(t)|&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon^{4}(t)\delta(t)^{2}|\nu(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}|\mu(t)|+\sum_{j}\left|\langle\psi(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon^{4}(t)\delta(t)^{2}|\nu(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}|\mu(t)|+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.\end{split}

By inserting (
7.6
) back to the righthand side of (
7.4
), one gets the first half of the first desired estimate
in (
7.3
).

Now, since the tensor
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
is supported in
\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}
, one also gets the following pointwise estimate from (
7.6
) :

\begin{split}\|\lambda_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq C\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\varepsilon(T)^{2+\sigma}\delta(T)^{-2-\sigma}+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{2+\sigma}\delta(t)^{2}|\nu(t)|\\
&\quad+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{2+\sigma}|\mu(t)|+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{2+\sigma}\delta(t)^{2}|\nu(t)|\\
&\quad+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{2+\sigma}|\mu(t)|+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}},\end{split}

where
C
is a time-independent positive constant that may vary from line to line. Here we have used that
\mathbf{o}_{i}
decays like
\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-4}
and the assumption
\sigma\in(0,2)
in the first line together with the choice of
\gamma(t)
in the last line.
∎

7.2.2.
Orthogonal projections against
\tilde{g}(t)

We continue with an analogous property established in Lemma
7.2
between integral and pointwise norms, here we use the essential fact that the function
(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-\sigma}
is in
L^{1}(\tilde{g}(t))
uniformly in time if
\sigma\in(0,4)
.

Lemma 7.5
.

One has the following domination property in terms of the
C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
norm if
\sigma\in(0,4)
:

\left|\langle\psi(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\leq\gamma(t)^{-1}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}.

Moreover the
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal projection of a symmetric
2
-tensor
\psi\in{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}
on the line
\mathbb{R}\cdot\tilde{g}(t)
denoted by
M(t)\tilde{g}(t)
, satisfies

(7.7)

\|M\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\leq C\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}},

for some uniform positive constant
C
.

The proof of Lemma
7.5
is along the same lines as those of the proof of Lemma
7.2
.

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
h\in C^{2}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
such that
h(t)
is
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal to the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
. Then,

\begin{split}\left|\langle\partial_{t}h,\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}.\end{split}

Proof.

By differentiating the orthogonality condition
\langle h(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=0
, one gets thanks to Lemma
C.4
:

\begin{split}\left|\langle\partial_{t}h(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&=\left|\Big\langle h(t),\Big(\partial_{t}+\frac{\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)}{2}\operatorname{Id}-\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)\circ\cdot)\Big)\tilde{g}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq C\int_{M}|h(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\int_{M}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-\sigma}\left|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)},\end{split}

where
C
is a time-independent positive constant that may vary from line to line. By invoking Lemma
3.8
, terms supported in
\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}
can be estimated by:

\begin{split}\int_{M}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-\sigma}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-\sigma}.\end{split}

Terms supported in
\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}
can be bounded by

\begin{split}\int_{M}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-\sigma}\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-4}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma},\end{split}

where we have used (

H
0

) in the last inequality.

Finally, terms supported in
\{r_{o}\,\geq\,2\delta(t)\}
can be bounded by

\begin{split}\int_{M}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-\sigma}\varepsilon(t)^{4}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-4}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\geq\,2\delta(t)\}}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma},\end{split}

Once again, the proof is the same for the ancient and immortal situations.
∎

The next proposition estimates a priori the
L^{2}
-projection of the non-homogeneous data of the parabolic equation (
7.1
) against the elements
\tilde{g}(t)
of the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t).

Proposition 7.7
.

Let
\psi\in C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
and
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
such that
h(t)
is
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal to the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
(respectively
t\geq T
in the immortal case) and satisfying for
t\leq T
(respectively
t\geq T
in the immortal case),

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)-\psi(t)\in\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t),

i.e.

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)=\psi(t)+\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t).

Then there exists a uniform positive constant
C
such that for
t\leq T
(respectively if
t\geq T
in the immortal case),

\begin{split}\left|\langle\psi(t)+\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}},\quad\text{and,}\\
\left|\langle\psi(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}\sum_{i}|\lambda_{i}(t)|.\end{split}

In particular, there exists some uniform positive constant
C
such that:

\begin{split}\|\nu\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}\sum_{i}|\lambda_{i}(t)|,\end{split}

Proof.

Since
\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{g}(t)=0
, the proofs are exactly the same for the ancient and immortal situations. By differentiating the orthogonality condition
\langle h(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=0
and Lemma
7.1
, one gets:

\begin{split}\langle\psi(t)+&\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=\\
&\left\langle(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)})h(t),\tilde{g}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=\left\langle\partial_{t}h(t),\tilde{g}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))},\end{split}

since
\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{g}(t)=0
. In particular, since
\langle\tilde{c}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t)}=0
by Proposition
4.5
, Lemma
7.6
ensures:

\begin{split}&\left|\langle\psi(t)+\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\leq C\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}.\end{split}

The triangular inequality leads to:

\begin{split}\left|\langle\psi(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}+|\lambda_{i}(t)|\left|\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}\sum_{i}|\lambda_{i}(t)|,\end{split}

as claimed. Here we have used Proposition
4.2
in the last line.

Finally, Lemma
7.5
applied to
\psi(t)
leads to the following pointwise estimate:

\begin{split}\|\nu\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq C\varepsilon(T)^{4}\delta(T)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}\sum_{i}|\lambda_{i}(t)|+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}},\end{split}

which implies the desired estimate.
∎

7.2.3.
Orthogonal projections against
\tilde{c}(t)

Lemma 7.8
.

One has the following domination property in terms of the
C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
norm:

\left|\langle\psi(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}},

for some uniform positive constant
C
.
Moreover the
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal projection of a symmetric
2
-tensor
\psi\in{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}
denoted by
N(t)\tilde{c}(t)
, satisfies

(7.8)

\|N(t)\tilde{c}(t)\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq C\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}},

for some uniform positive constant
C
.

Proof.

Lemma
3.11
ensures that the tensor
\tilde{c}(t)
is bounded uniformly in time with respect to
\tilde{g}(t)
. The desired estimate follows immediately from this fact.
∎

Lemma 7.9
.

Let
h\in C^{2}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
, orthogonal to the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
. Then,

\begin{split}\left|\langle\partial_{t}h,\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}.\end{split}

Proof.

By differentiating the orthogonality condition
\langle h(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=0
, one gets thanks to Lemma
C.4
:

\begin{split}&\left|\langle\partial_{t}h(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|=\left|\Big\langle h(t),\Big(\partial_{t}+\frac{\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)}{2}\operatorname{Id}-\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)\circ\cdot)\Big)\tilde{c}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq C\int_{M}|h(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\left(\left|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}|\tilde{c}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}+\left|\partial_{t}\tilde{c}(t)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\right)\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)},\end{split}

where
C
is a time-independent positive constant that may vary from line to line. By invoking Lemma
3.11
, for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}\int_{M}|h(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\left|\partial_{t}\tilde{c}(t)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\left(\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}+\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\right)\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma},\end{split}

for some uniform positive constant
C
thanks to (

H
0

) that ensures that
\delta<(3-\sigma)/(5-\sigma)<(4-\sigma)/(6-\sigma)
. Finally, similarly to the proof of Proposition
7.7
based on Lemma
3.8
, for
t\leq T\leq 0
,

\int_{M}|h(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\left|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)\right|_{\tilde{g}(t)}|\tilde{c}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma},

for some uniform positive constant
C
.
∎

The next proposition estimates a priori the
L^{2}
-projection of the non-homogeneous data of the parabolic equation (
7.1
) against the elements
\tilde{c}(t)
of the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t).

Proposition 7.10
.

Let
\psi\in C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
and
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
such that
h(t)
is
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal to
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
satisfying for
t\leq T
,

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)-\psi(t)\in\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t),

i.e.

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)=\psi(t)+\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t).

Then there exists a uniform positive constant
C
such that for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}\left|\langle\psi(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sum_{i}|\lambda_{i}(t)|.\end{split}

In particular,
there exists some uniform positive constant
C
such that:

\begin{split}\|\mu\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sum_{i}|\lambda_{i}(t)|.\end{split}

Proof.

Lemma
7.1
gives:

\begin{split}&\langle\psi(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&=\left\langle(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)})h(t),\tilde{c}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}-\lambda_{i}(t)\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}-\nu(t)\langle\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&=\left\langle\partial_{t}h(t),\tilde{c}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}-\left\langle h(t),\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{c}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}-\lambda_{i}(t)\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))},\end{split}

since
\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{c}(t)=\Delta_{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)
has zero mean value as observed in Proposition
4.5
.

According to Lemma
3.11
, one has

\begin{split}&\left\langle h(t),\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{c}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=-(\Lambda+1)\Big\langle h(t),\tilde{c}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&+\left\langle h(t),O(\rho(t)^{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-7})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho(t)^{-4})\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}.\end{split}

In particular, since
h(t)
is
L^{2}
-orthogonal to
\tilde{c}(t)
by assumption, we get the following estimate:

\left|\left\langle h(t),\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{c}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\delta(t)^{4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}},\quad t\leq T,

for some uniform positive constant
C
.
In order to handle the
L^{2}
-projection of
\tilde{c}(t)
on
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
, we invoke Proposition
4.5
:

\begin{split}\left|\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4},\end{split}

for some uniform positive constant
C
. To summarize our set of estimates, we have obtained, thanks to Lemma
7.9
:

\begin{split}\left|\langle\psi(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\delta(t)^{4-\sigma}+\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sum_{i}|\lambda_{i}(t)|,\end{split}

which leads directly to the desired first estimate since
\delta>5/9>1/2
by (

H
0

).

As for the second estimate, Lemma
7.8
applied to
\psi(t)
leads to the following pointwise estimate:

\begin{split}\|\mu\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sum_{i}|\lambda_{i}(t)|,\end{split}

which implies the desired estimate by assumption on
\gamma(t)
with respect to
\gamma(t)
.

∎

7.2.4.
Orthogonal projections: pointwise estimates

By combining Propositions
7.4
,
7.7
and
7.10
, we get by for
T
small enough by absorption:

Corollary 7.11
.

There exists a uniform positive constant
C
and
T<0
negative enough (respectively large enough) such that the following holds: if
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
is such that
h(t)
is
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal to
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
and satisfies for
t\leq T
,

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)-\psi(t)\in\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t),

i.e.

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)=\psi(t)+\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t),

then,

\begin{split}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sum_{j}|\lambda_{j}(t)|&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}},\\
|\nu(t)|&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}},\\
|\mu(t)|&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}},\end{split}

and,

\sum_{j}\|\lambda_{j}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\|\nu\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\|\mu\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.

7.3.
Orthogonal projections for abstract data: Hölder estimates

The purpose of this section is to obtain corresponding Hölder estimates of the orthogonal projections considered in the previous Section
7.2
.

7.3.1.
Orthogonal projections against
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
: Hölder estimates

Lemma 7.12
.

Let
h\in C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
. Then for
t\leq T
,
