\left[\langle h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma-2\alpha}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}.

Proof.

According to Lemma
D.4
and Lemma
D.8
:

\begin{split}\left[\langle h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma-2\alpha}+r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}+r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-\sigma}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma-2\alpha}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}},\end{split}

as expected. Here we have used Lemma
3.10
in the first inequality together with (
D.1
) applied to
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}
.
∎

The next lemma is the Hölder counterpart of Lemma
7.3
:

Lemma 7.13
.

Let
h\in C^{2}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
such that
h(t)
is
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal to
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
(respectively for
t\geq T
). Then,

\begin{split}\left[\langle\partial_{t}h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(\frac{r}{\varepsilon(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}+r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}.\end{split}

Proof.

The proof is the same for ancient and immortal flows.

Starting from the proof of Lemma
7.3
and thanks to Lemma
D.4
and Lemma
D.8
:

\begin{split}\left[\langle\partial_{t}h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&=\left[\Big\langle h(t),\Big(\partial_{t}+\frac{\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)}{2}\operatorname{Id}-\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)\circ\cdot)\Big)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\int_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}\frac{r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{4}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{4+\sigma+2\alpha}}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\max\{(r/\delta(t))^{2\alpha};1\}\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{-\sigma^{-}}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\int_{M}\frac{1}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{\sigma}}\left([\partial_{t}\tilde{g}\ast\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}+[\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}\right)\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\left(r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma-2\alpha}+\max\{(r/\delta(t))^{2\alpha};1\}\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{-\sigma^{-}}\right)\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\int_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4-}}{(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{4^{-}+\sigma}}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&\quad+C\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\varepsilon(t)^{8^{-}}\delta(t)^{-4^{-}-\sigma^{-}}\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(\frac{r}{\varepsilon(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}+r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}.\end{split}

Here we have used Lemmata
3.8
and
3.10
in the second line together with Propositions
3.13
and
3.15
in the third inequality. The choice of parameters from (

H
0

) has also been invoked in the last line. The result follows by invoking (

H
0

) once more.
∎

Proposition 7.14
.

Let
\psi\in C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
and let
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
such that
h(t)
is
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal to
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
(respectively
t\geq T
in the immortal case) and satisfying for all
t\leq T
(respectively
t\geq T
in the immortal case),

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)-\psi(t)\in\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t),

i.e.

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)=\psi(t)+\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t).

Then there exists a uniform positive constant
C
such that for
t\leq T
(respectively
t\geq T
in the immortal case),

(7.9)

\begin{split}&\left[\langle\psi+\lambda_{i}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}[\nu]_{\alpha,t,r}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}[\mu]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma-2\alpha}+r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{6-\sigma}\delta(t)^{-4}\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.\end{split}

and,

\begin{split}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\delta(t)^{2}[\nu]_{\alpha,t,r}+C[\mu]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\delta(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}+r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{-2-\sigma}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{-2-\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.\end{split}

In particular, there exists some uniform positive constant
C
such that:

\begin{split}\|\lambda_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}\rho^{-2+\sigma}[\nu]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
&\quad+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}\rho^{-2+\sigma}[\mu]_{\alpha,t,\rho}.\end{split}

Proof.

We provide a proof for ancient flows, but the statement also holds for immortal flows by flipping the sign of time. Indeed, the controls are always made on
\partial_{t}
and
\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}
separately so looking at
\partial_{t}+\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}
or
\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}
yields the same estimate.

Since
h(t)
is orthogonal to
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
, a spatial integration by parts yields:

(7.10)

\begin{split}\left[\langle\psi+\lambda_{i}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq\left[\langle\partial_{t}h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}+\left[\langle h,\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}+\left[\nu\cdot\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i},\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\quad+\left[\mu\cdot\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i},\tilde{c}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}+\sum_{j\neq i}\left[\lambda_{j}\cdot\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}.\end{split}

Now, Lemma
7.13
and (

H
0

) give:

(7.11)

\begin{split}\left[\langle\partial_{t}h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(\frac{r}{\varepsilon(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}+r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(\frac{r}{\varepsilon(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}+1\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}},\end{split}

and Lemma
3.10
, Proposition
3.14
and Lemma
D.8
give in turn:

(7.12)

\begin{split}\left[\langle h,\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\left(r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{4-2\alpha-\sigma}+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma-2\alpha}+\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}\right)\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\left(r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}+r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}\right)\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\delta(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}+r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}},\end{split}

where
C
is a time-independent positive constant.
Here we have invoked (

H
0

) to ensure that
\delta>(\sigma+2\alpha)/(2+\sigma+2\alpha)
. Similarly, repeated applications of Lemma
D.8
gives:

(7.13)

\begin{split}\left[\nu\cdot\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i},\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}[\nu]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\quad+Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}}\left(\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+\gamma(t)^{-1}\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\right).\end{split}

Here we have used Proposition
4.2
, Proposition
4.6
and Corollary
7.11
.

A similar reasoning based on Proposition
4.5
, Proposition
4.6
and Corollary
7.11
gives:

(7.14)

\begin{split}\left[\mu\cdot\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i},\tilde{c}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4}[\mu]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\quad+Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\left(\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+\gamma(t)^{-1}\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\right),\end{split}

and for
i\neq j
,

(7.15)

\begin{split}&\left[\lambda_{j}\cdot\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4}[\lambda_{j}]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\quad+Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4}\left(\gamma(t)^{-1}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{-2-\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\right).\end{split}

Here we have used Proposition
4.2
and (

H
0

) together with Proposition
4.6
and Corollary
7.11
.

The combination of estimates (
7.11
), (
7.12
), (
7.13
), (
7.14
), (
7.15
) gives thanks to (
7.10
) and (

H
0

):

(7.16)

\begin{split}&\left[\langle\psi+\lambda_{i}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4}\sum_{j\neq i}[\lambda_{j}]_{\alpha,t,r}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}[\nu]_{\alpha,t,r}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}[\mu]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\delta(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}+r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&+Cr^{2}\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{6-\sigma}\delta(t)^{-4}\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.\end{split}

Now, Lemma
D.4
and Lemma
D.8
applied to
\psi(t)
and
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
lead to:

\begin{split}[\langle\psi,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C(1+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha})\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.\end{split}

Here we have used (
3.11
).

By summing (
7.16
) over
i
and by absorption, the previous estimate combined with (
7.16
) gives in turn:

(7.17)

\begin{split}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\delta(t)^{2}[\nu]_{\alpha,t,r}+C[\mu]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\delta(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}+r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{-2-\sigma}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{-2-\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}},\end{split}

thanks to (

H
0

)
and the fact that
\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}
is proportional to
\varepsilon(t)^{4}
thanks to Proposition
4.2
. Here we have invoked Corollary
7.11
together with Proposition
4.6
to handle the terms involving
|\lambda_{i}|\Big[\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\Big]_{\alpha,t,r}
.

By inserting (
7.17
) back to the righthand side of (
7.16
), one gets the first half of the first desired estimates:

\begin{split}&\left[\langle\psi+\lambda_{i}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}[\nu]_{\alpha,t,r}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}[\mu]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\delta(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}+r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{6-\sigma}\delta(t)^{-4}\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.\end{split}

Now, since the tensor
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
is supported in
\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}
, one also gets the following pointwise estimate from Propositions
6.7
and
7.4
, Corollary
7.11
and (
7.17
):

\begin{split}\|\lambda_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq\|\lambda_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{2+\sigma}\sup_{t-4\delta(t)^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|\lambda_{i}(t^{\prime})|\\
&\quad+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}\rho^{-2+\sigma}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
&\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\quad+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}\rho^{-2+\sigma}[\nu]_{\alpha,t,\rho}+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}\rho^{-2+\sigma}[\mu]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
&\quad+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\left((\varepsilon(t)/\delta(t))^{2+\sigma}+(\delta(t)/\varepsilon(t))^{\sigma}\varepsilon(t)^{2}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\quad+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}\rho^{-2+\sigma}[\nu]_{\alpha,t,\rho}+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}\rho^{-2+\sigma}[\mu]_{\alpha,t,\rho},\end{split}

where
C
is a time-independent positive constant that may vary from line to line.

∎

7.3.2.
Orthogonal projections against
\tilde{g}(t)
and
\tilde{c}(t)
: Hölder estimates

Lemma 7.15
.

Let
h\in C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
. Then for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}\left[\langle h,\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}+\left[\langle h,\tilde{c}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}.\end{split}

Proof.

According to Lemma
D.4
and Lemma
D.8
:

\begin{split}\left[\langle h,\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(1+r^{4-\sigma}+r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}+r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-\sigma}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}},\end{split}

as expected. Here we have used Lemma
3.8
(respectively Lemma
3.11

) in the first (respectively second) inequality together with (
D.1
) applied to
\tilde{g}
. A similar proof works for
\tilde{c}
.
∎

Lemma 7.16
.

Let
h\in C^{2}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
such that
h(t)
is
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal to
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
(respectively for
t\geq T
). Then,

\begin{split}\left[\langle\partial_{t}h,\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(\frac{r}{\delta(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}+r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}},\\
\left[\langle\partial_{t}h,\tilde{c}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(\frac{r}{\delta(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}+r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}.\end{split}

The proof of Lemma
7.16
goes along the same lines as those of the proof of Lemma
7.13
.

Proposition 7.17
.

Let
\psi\in C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
and
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
such that
h(t)
is
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal to
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
(respectively
t\geq T
in the immortal case) and satisfying for all
t\leq T
(respectively
t\geq T
in the immortal case),

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)-\psi(t)\in\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t),

i.e.

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)=\psi(t)+\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t).

Then there exists a uniform positive constant
C
such that for
t\leq T
(respectively if
t\geq T
in the immortal case),

\begin{split}&\left[\langle\psi+\lambda_{i}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}+\nu\tilde{g},\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(r^{2}+\left(\frac{r}{\varepsilon(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}},\quad\text{and,}\\
\Big[\langle\psi+&\nu\tilde{g},\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\Big]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(r^{2}+\left(\frac{r}{\varepsilon(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{2^{+}-\sigma}\delta(t)^{2^{-}}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r},\end{split}

and,

\begin{split}\left[\nu\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(1+\left(\frac{r}{\varepsilon(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}.\end{split}

In particular,
there exists some uniform positive constant
C
such that:

\begin{split}\|\nu\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sup_{M}\rho^{2+\sigma}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}.\end{split}

Proof.

Since
\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{g}(t)=0
, the proofs are exactly the same for the ancient and immortal situations.

By differentiating the orthogonality condition
\langle h(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=0
and Lemma
7.1
, one gets:

\begin{split}\langle\psi(t)+&\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=\\
&\left\langle(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)})h(t),\tilde{g}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=\left\langle\partial_{t}h(t),\tilde{g}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))},\end{split}

since
\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{g}(t)=0
. In particular, since
\langle\tilde{c}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}=0
by Proposition
4.5
, Lemma
7.16
ensures that:

\begin{split}&\left[\langle\psi+\lambda_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}+\nu\cdot\tilde{g},\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(\frac{r}{\delta(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}+r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}.\end{split}

The triangular inequality leads to:

(7.18)

\begin{split}&\left[\langle\psi+\nu\cdot\tilde{g},\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(\frac{r}{\delta(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}+r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+\sum_{i}\left[\lambda_{i}\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i},\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(\frac{r}{\delta(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}+r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4^{+}}\delta(t)^{2^{-}}\sum_{i}|\lambda_{i}(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(\frac{r}{\delta(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}+r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{2^{+}-\sigma}\delta(t)^{2^{-}}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r},\end{split}

as claimed once one observes that
\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\leq C(r/\varepsilon(t))^{2\alpha}
thanks to (

H
0

) and Lemma
D.4
. Here we have used Proposition
4.2
, Proposition
4.6
in the penultimate line and Corollary
7.11
in the last line.

As for the second estimate, Lemma
D.4
and Lemma
D.8
applied to
\psi(t)
and
\tilde{g}(t)
lead to:

\left[\langle\psi,\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.

In particular, one gets the following intermediate estimate on the basis of (
7.18
) and the fact that
\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\leq C(r/\varepsilon(t))^{2\alpha}
thanks to (

H
0

) and Lemma
D.4
:

\begin{split}\left[\nu\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(1+\left(\frac{r}{\varepsilon(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(1+r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{2^{+}-\sigma}\delta(t)^{2^{-}}\right)\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}.\end{split}

Here we have used the fact that
\|\tilde{g}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}
is uniformly bounded from below. We have also invoked Corollary
7.11
together with Proposition
4.6
to handle the terms involving
|\nu|\Big[\|\tilde{g}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\Big]_{\alpha,t,r}
. This leads to the third estimate.

Then one gets the following pointwise estimate thanks to Proposition
6.7
and Corollary
7.11
:

\begin{split}\|\nu\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq\|\nu\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sup_{t-c_{0}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|\nu(t^{\prime})|\\
&\quad+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\sup_{M}\rho^{2+\sigma}[\nu]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
&\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\sup_{M}\rho^{2+\sigma}[\nu]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
&\leq\left(o_{T}(1)+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\left(\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}+1\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\quad+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sup_{M}\rho^{2+\sigma}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
&\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sup_{M}\rho^{2+\sigma}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho},\end{split}

since
4-2\alpha>(4+\sigma)\delta>0
according to (

H
0

) and
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
.

This implies the desired last estimate.
∎

The next proposition is the counterpart of the previous Proposition
7.17
where the metric
\tilde{g}
is replaced by
\tilde{c}
.

Proposition 7.18
.

Let
\psi\in C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
and
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
such that
h(t)
is
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal to
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
(respectively
t\geq T
in the immortal case) and satisfying for all
t\leq T
(respectively
t\geq T
in the immortal case),

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)-\psi(t)\in\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t),

i.e.

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)=\psi(t)+\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t).

Then there exists a uniform positive constant
C
such that for
t\leq T\leq 0
,

\begin{split}\left[\langle\psi+\mu\cdot\tilde{c},\tilde{c}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(1+\left(\frac{r}{\varepsilon(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+Cr^{2}\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r},\end{split}

and,

\begin{split}\left[\mu\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(1+\left(\frac{r}{\varepsilon(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}.\end{split}

In particular,
there exists some uniform positive constant
C
such that:

\begin{split}\|\mu\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sup_{M}\rho^{2+\sigma}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}.\end{split}

Proof.

From the proof of Proposition
7.10
,

\begin{split}&\langle\psi(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
&=\left\langle\partial_{t}h(t),\tilde{c}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}+\left\langle h(t),(\Lambda+1)\,\tilde{c}(t)+\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{c}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}-\lambda_{i}(t)\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}.\end{split}

In particular, we have obtained, thanks to Lemma
7.16
:

(7.19)

\begin{split}\left[\langle\psi+\mu\cdot\tilde{c},\tilde{c}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(\frac{r}{\delta(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}+r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\delta(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}+r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}+r^{2}\delta(t)^{4-\sigma}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+Cr^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sum_{i}|\lambda_{i}(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(1+\left(\frac{r}{\varepsilon(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}+\left(\frac{r}{\varepsilon(t)}\right)^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+Cr^{2}\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(1+\left(\frac{r}{\varepsilon(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+Cr^{2}\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}.\end{split}

Here we have invoked Lemma
3.11
and Proposition
3.14
in the second line, Propositions
4.5
and
4.6
in the third line and Corollary
7.11
in the second inequality.

This leads directly to the desired first estimate.

As for the second estimate, Lemma
D.4
and Lemma
D.8
applied to
\psi(t)
and
\tilde{c}(t)
lead to:

\left[\langle\psi,\tilde{c}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.

In particular, one gets the following intermediate estimate on the basis of (
7.19
):

\begin{split}\left[\mu\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(1+\left(\frac{r}{\varepsilon(t)}\right)^{2\alpha}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,r}.\end{split}

Here we have used the fact that
\|\tilde{c}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}
is uniformly bounded from below. We have also invoked Corollary
7.11
together with Proposition
4.6
to handle the terms involving
|\mu|\Big[\|\tilde{c}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\Big]_{\alpha,t,r}
.
Then one gets the following pointwise estimate thanks to Proposition
6.7
and Corollary
7.11
:

\begin{split}\|\mu\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq\|\mu\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\sup_{t-c_{0}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|\mu(t^{\prime})|\\
&\quad+\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\sup_{M}\rho^{2+\sigma}[\mu]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
&\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\sup_{M}\rho^{2+\sigma}[\mu]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
&\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sup_{M}\rho^{2+\sigma}\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}.\end{split}

∎

By combining Propositions
7.14
,
7.17
and
7.18
and using the fact that
2>\sigma+2\alpha
if
\sigma\in(0,1)
and
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
, we get for
T
small enough by absorption:

Corollary 7.19
.

There exists a uniform positive constant
C
and
T<0
negative enough (respectively large enough) such that the following holds: if
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
is such that
h(t)
is
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-orthogonal to
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
and satisfies for
t\leq T
,

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)-\psi(t)\in\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t),

i.e.

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)=\psi(t)+\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t),

then,

\begin{split}\sum_{j}[\lambda_{j}]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\delta(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}+r^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{-2-\sigma}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{-2-\sigma}(1+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha})\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}},\\
[\nu]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}},\\
[\mu]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}},\end{split}

and,

\sum_{j}\|\lambda_{j}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\|\nu\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\|\mu\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.

7.4.
A parabolic Lyapunov-Schmidt reduction

Proposition 7.20
.

Let
\psi\in C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
. Then there exists a unique
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
orthogonal to the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
for each
t\leq T
(respectively for each
t\geq T
in the immortal case such that
h(T)\equiv 0
) and such that

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)-\psi(t)\in\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t),

i.e. there exists real coefficients
\lambda_{i}
,
\nu
and
\mu
defined on
(-\infty,T]
(respectively
[T,+\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
in the immortal case such that

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h(t)=\psi(t)+\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t).

Moreover there exists a uniform constant
C
such that:

\begin{split}\|h\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}&\leq C\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}},\\
\sum_{i}\|\lambda_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\|\nu\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\|\mu\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq o_{T}(1)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.\end{split}

Proof.

Let us explain why the core of the proof is to show the existence of solutions on intervals of finite size
[T^{\prime},T]
which will be treated in Appendix
B
.

First, the uniqueness and controls of the solution come from Propositions
6.3
and
6.5
, so we are left with proving the existence of
h
, together with that of the coefficients
\lambda_{i}(t)
,
\mu(t)
and
\nu(t)
. Moreover, by the a priori controls of Propositions
6.3
and
6.5
, we may approximate
\psi
by compactly supported tensors
\psi_{k}:=\chi_{k}\psi
, for
\chi_{k}
a cut-off function supported on
[-k,T]
. From Proposition
B.1
in Appendix
B
, there exist associated
h_{k}(t)
,
\lambda_{i,k}(t)
,
\mu_{k}(t)
and
\nu_{k}(t)
so that

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda)h_{k}(t)=\chi_{k}\psi(t)+\lambda_{i,k}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+\nu_{k}(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\mu_{k}(t)\tilde{c}(t).

Then, by the a priori controls of Proposition
6.3
(and
6.5
in the immortal case) together with Corollary
7.19
, the pointwise limit
h:=\lim_{k}h_{k}
exists on
(-\infty,T]\times M
and lies in
C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
and the pointwise limits of the coefficients
\lambda_{i}(t):=\lim_{k}\lambda_{i,k}(t)
,
\mu(t):=\lim_{k}\mu_{k}(t)
and
\nu(t):=\lim_{k}\nu_{k}(t)
exist for each
t\leq T
and satisfy (after absorption):

\begin{split}\|h\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\sum_{i}\|\lambda_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\|\nu\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\|\mu\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq C\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.\end{split}

∎

8.
Non-linear analysis

8.1.
Set-up for ancient solutions

In this section, we consider a
4
-dimensional Einstein orbifold
(M_{o}^{4},g_{o})
with Einstein constant
\Lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, i.e.
\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g_{o})=\Lambda g_{o}
on the regular part of
M_{o}
. We assume from now on that
M_{o}
has one single orbifold singularity modeled on
\mathbb{R}^{4}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
at a point
p_{o}
. The case where the orbifold singularity is modeled on
\mathbb{R}^{4}/\Gamma
where
\Gamma\subset\operatorname{SU(2)}
is finite and acts freely on
\mathbb{C}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
is handled in Section
11
. A finite number of orbifold singularities is allowed as explained in Remark
8.3
.

We start by setting up the notations together with the heuristics explaining each term. Let
T\leq 0
and
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
, and consider
\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
interpreted as a “gluing parameter”. Denote the smallest eigenvalue of
2\mathbf{R}^{+}_{p}
by
\Lambda_{0}
. Define also an approximate bound for the size of bubbles by

(8.1)

\varepsilon_{0}(t):=e^{\frac{\Lambda_{0}}{2}t},\quad t\leq T.

•

The family of symmetric
2
-tensors
(k(t))_{t\,\leq\,T}
is thought of as a metric perturbation of the first approximation
\tilde{g}(t)
(which is not defined yet) lying in
C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
.

•

The vector
\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
is related to the approximate orthogonal projection of our desired solution against
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
and it determines the size of the bubble parametrized by
\zeta
in the next approximation. It lies in the following function space:

\|\eta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}:=\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\left\{e^{-\eta_{0}t}\left(|\eta(t)|+[\eta]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon_{0}(t)}\right)\right\}<+\infty,

where
\eta_{0}>0
will be chosen appropriately in Lemma
8.1
and [(

H
1

), Section
8.3
].

•

The function
\beta
is related to the approximate orthogonal projection of the metric perturbation against the background metric
\tilde{g}(t)
(which is not defined yet). It lies in the following function space:

\|\beta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}}:=\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\left\{e^{-\beta_{0}t}\left(|\beta(t)|+[\beta]_{\alpha,t,1}\right)\right\}<+\infty,

where
\beta_{0}>0
will be chosen in [(

H
1

), Section
8.3
].

•

The function
\tau
denotes the approximate orthogonal projection of the metric perturbation against the approximate conformal deformation
\tilde{c}(t)
(which is not defined yet).
It lies in the following function space:

\|\tau\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\tau_{0},T}}:=\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\left\{e^{-\tau_{0}t}\left(|\tau(t)|+[\tau]_{\alpha,t,1}\right)\right\}<+\infty,

where
\tau_{0}>0
will be chosen in [(

H
1

), Section
8.3
].

We define a function space
\mathcal{X}
as follows:

(8.2)

\begin{split}\mathcal{X}&:=C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}\times C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}\times C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}\times C^{0,\alpha}_{\tau_{0},T},\\
\\
\|(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)\|_{\mathcal{X}}&:=\|k\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}+\|\beta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}}+\|\tau\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\tau_{0},T}}.\end{split}

The normed space
\mathcal{X}
is a Banach space, its unit ball will be denoted by
\mathcal{B}.

For a given
\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
and a quadruplet
(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)\in\mathcal{B}
, we formally define a map
\Phi(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)=:(h,\xi,\nu,\mu)
whose fixed point set is made of ancient solutions to the DeTurck-Ricci flow only. More precisely,

(1)

Given
\eta\in C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}
such that
\|\eta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}\leq 1
, we define
\zeta(t)
through the following differential equation:

(8.3)

\dot{\zeta}(t)=2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}(\zeta(t))+\eta(t),\quad t\leq T,\quad\zeta(T):=\exp(2T\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+})\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}.

Equivalently, thanks to the Duhamel principle, one gets the following more explicit formula for
\zeta(t)
:

(8.4)

\zeta(t)=\exp\left(2t\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}\right)\zeta_{0}-\int_{t}^{T}\exp\left(2(t-s)\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}\right)\eta(s)\,ds,\quad t\leq T.

We need to ensure that
\zeta(t)
stays controlled in norm and in direction close to that of the initial condition
\zeta_{0}
in order to make sense of
g_{\zeta(t)}
. Denote by
\operatorname{sp}(2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+})=:(\Lambda_{i})_{i}
the spectrum of
2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}
: since
2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}
is a symmetric matrix with real entries,
\operatorname{sp}(2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+})
is contained in
\mathbb{R}.

Lemma 8.1
.

Assume
\eta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\setminus\operatorname{sp}(2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+})
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that for
t\leq T\leq 0
:

(8.5)

\left|\zeta(t)-\exp\left(2t\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}\right)\zeta_{0}\right|\leq C\left(\sum_{\eta_{0}>\Lambda_{i}}\frac{e^{\Lambda_{i}t}}{\eta_{0}-\Lambda_{i}}+\sum_{\eta_{0}<\Lambda_{i}}\frac{e^{\eta_{0}t}}{\Lambda_{i}-\eta_{0}}\right).

In particular,

(a)

if
\operatorname{sp}(2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+})=:\{\Lambda_{0}\}
,
\eta_{0}>\Lambda_{0}>0
and
\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
such that
(\eta_{0}-\Lambda_{0})|\zeta_{0}|>c_{0}
for some universal positive constant
c_{0}
then there exist positive constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
depending on
|\zeta_{0}|
and
\eta_{0}-\Lambda_{0}
only such that for all
t\leq T\leq 0
:

C_{1}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2}=C_{1}e^{\Lambda_{0}t}\leq|\zeta(t)|\leq C_{2}e^{\Lambda_{0}t}=C_{2}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2}.

Moreover, if
\eta_{0}\in(\Lambda_{0},2\Lambda_{0}]
, there exists
C>0
depending on
\Lambda_{0}
only such that

[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon_{0}(t)}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}},\quad t\leq T.

(b)

if
\operatorname{sp}(2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+})
contains at least two distinct eigenvalues
0<\Lambda_{0}<\Lambda_{1}
,
\eta_{0}\in(\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{1})
and
\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
is such that its projection on
\ker(2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}-\Lambda_{1}\mathop{\rm Id}\nolimits)
is non zero and such that
(\eta_{0}-\Lambda_{0})|\zeta_{0}|>c_{0}
for some universal positive constant
c_{0}
then there exist positive constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
depending on
|\zeta_{0}|
,
\eta_{0}-\Lambda_{0}
and
\Lambda_{1}-\eta_{0}
only such that for all
t\leq T\leq 0
:

C_{1}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2}\leq|\zeta(t)|\leq C_{2}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2}.

Moreover, if
\eta_{0}\in(\Lambda_{0},\min\{\Lambda_{1},2\Lambda_{0}\}]
, there exists
C>0
depending on the operator norm
\|\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}\|
only such that

[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon_{0}(t)}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}},\quad t\leq T.

Remark 8.2
.

This lemma also applies and is crucial in the case of hyperkähler ALE metrics, where it is even more important to make sure that
\zeta(t)/|\zeta(t)|
stays close to
\zeta_{0}/|\zeta_{0}|
. This ensures that other orbifold singularities are not created along the flow, see Remark
6.4
.

Remark 8.3
.

The setting we consider allows a finite number of orbifold singularities
(p_{i})_{i\in I}\subset M_{o}
on the condition that all the corresponding “gluing” parameters introduced previously
\zeta_{0}^{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
and
\Lambda_{0}^{i}>0
satisfy:
\Lambda_{0}^{i}=\Lambda_{0}
and
\zeta_{0}^{i}
have a non zero projection on
\ker(2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}-\Lambda_{0}\mathop{\rm Id}\nolimits)
for all
i\in I
.

Proof.

The proof of (
8.5
) is essentially based on formula (
8.4
). Indeed, let
(\mathbf{e}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq 3}
be an orthonormal basis of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that
2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}\mathbf{e}_{i}=\Lambda_{i}\mathbf{e}_{i}
for
i=1,2,3
. Then write
\eta(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\eta_{i}(t)\cdot\mathbf{e}_{i}
to estimate:

\begin{split}\left|\zeta(t)-\exp\left(2t\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}\right)\zeta_{0}\right|&\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{3}\int_{t}^{T}e^{\Lambda_{i}(t-s)}|\eta_{i}(s)|\,ds\\
&\leq C\|\eta\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}\sum_{i=1}^{3}e^{\Lambda_{i}t}\int_{t}^{T}e^{(\eta_{0}-\Lambda_{i})s}\,ds\\
&\leq C\left(\sum_{\eta_{0}>\Lambda_{i}}\frac{e^{\Lambda_{i}t}}{\eta_{0}-\Lambda_{i}}+\sum_{\eta_{0}<\Lambda_{i}}\frac{e^{\eta_{0}t}}{\Lambda_{i}-\eta_{0}}\right).\end{split}

Here we have used that
\|\eta\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}\leq 1
by assumption and
C
is a universal positive constant.

We turn to the proof of item
1a
.

Now, if
\operatorname{sp}(2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+})=:\{\Lambda_{0}\}
,
\eta_{0}>\Lambda_{0}>0
and
\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
then the triangular inequality together with estimate (
8.5
) gives:

\begin{split}|\zeta(t)|&\geq e^{\Lambda_{0}t}|\zeta_{0}|-e^{\Lambda_{0}t}\frac{C}{\eta_{0}-\Lambda_{0}}=\left(|\zeta_{0}|-\frac{C}{\eta_{0}-\Lambda_{0}}\right)e^{\Lambda_{0}t}>0,\end{split}

if
|\zeta_{0}|>\frac{C}{\eta_{0}-\Lambda_{0}}
. Here we have used that
T\leq 0
. A similar upper bound holds.

Observe that we get an intermediate upper bound for the speed
|\dot{\zeta}(t)|
:

(8.6)

|\dot{\zeta}(t)|\leq\left(\Lambda_{0}+1\right)e^{\Lambda_{0}t}=\left(\Lambda_{0}+1\right)\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2},\quad t\leq T,

since again
\|\eta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}\leq 1
and
\eta_{0}>\Lambda_{0}
.

Finally, the mean value theorem ensures for
t\leq T\leq 0
and
t-\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2}\leq t^{\prime}<t
:

\begin{split}\frac{|\dot{\zeta}(t)-\dot{\zeta}(t^{\prime})|}{|t-t^{\prime}|^{\alpha}}&\leq\Lambda_{0}\frac{|\zeta(t)-\zeta(t^{\prime})|}{|t-t^{\prime}|^{\alpha}}+\frac{|\eta(t)-\eta(t^{\prime})|}{|t-t^{\prime}|^{\alpha}}\\
&\leq\Lambda_{0}\max_{s\in(t,t^{\prime})}|\dot{\zeta}(s)||t-t^{\prime}|^{1-\alpha}+\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2\alpha}e^{\eta_{0}t}\|\eta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}\\
&\leq\Lambda_{0}\left(\Lambda_{0}+1\right)e^{\Lambda_{0}(t+\varepsilon_{0}(t))}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2(1-\alpha)}+\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2\alpha}e^{\eta_{0}t}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2\alpha},\end{split}

for some constant
C
that depends on
\Lambda_{0}
only. Here we have used the assumption
\eta_{0}\leq 2\Lambda_{0}
.

The proof of item
1b
is similar and is therefore left to the reader.
∎

Thanks to Lemma
8.1
, we can define
\zeta(t)
and hence
\tilde{g}(t)
whenever
\|\eta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}\leq 1
and
\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
lies outside a sufficiently large ball whose radius depends on
\eta_{0}
and the spectrum of
2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}
.

(2)

Non-linear terms coming from the linearization of the Ricci tensor involving
k\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
with
|k(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\leq 1/2
for
t\leq T
are incorportated in the data
\psi
:

(8.7)

\psi(t):=-Q_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t))+\beta(t)k(t)+\frac{1}{2}\tau(t)\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)}k(t)-\left(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))-2\Lambda\tilde{g}(t)\right),

where
Q_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t))
is defined by

\begin{split}Q_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t))&:=2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t)+k(t))-2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))+\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}k(t)-\mathcal{L}_{B_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t))}(\tilde{g}(t)+k(t)),\\
B_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t))&:=\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}k(t)-\frac{1}{2}d\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}k(t).\end{split}

Recall that if
|k(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\leq 1/2
for
t\leq T
, a fact that will be guaranteed by the choice of parameters made below in [(

H
1

), Section
8.3
] if
T\leq 0
is negative enough, then:

(8.8)

\begin{split}Q_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t))&=g(t)^{-1}\ast k(t)\ast\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),\,2}k(t)+g(t)^{-1}\ast g(t)^{-1}\ast\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}k(t)\ast\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}k(t)\\
&\quad+g(t)^{-1}\ast\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))\ast k(t)\ast k(t).\end{split}

(3)

The first component
h
and the last two components
(\nu,\mu)
of the quadruplet
(h,\xi,\nu,\mu)
are then defined as follows: the tensor
h
is the unique solution to the linear (Lichnerowicz) heat equation modulo obstructions with the non-homogeneous term
\psi
defined previously:

(8.9)

\partial_{t}h(t)-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}h(t)-2\Lambda h(t)=\psi(t)+\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\mu(t)\tilde{c}(t),\quad t\leq T,

whose existence is ensured by Proposition
7.20
once
\psi
lies in
C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
, a fact that will be checked in Proposition
8.6
.

(4)

The vectorial function
\xi(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
is then obtained by taking into account obstructions coming from
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
which are formally encoded by
\eta
and
(\lambda_{i})_{i}
:

(8.10)

d_{\zeta(t)}g(\xi(t))=d_{\zeta(t)}g(\eta(t))-\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}(t)\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\frac{\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))}{\|\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\|^{2}_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}},\quad t\leq T.

Remark 8.4
.

In case there are no rotations involved, i.e. if the symmetries one would impose were implying
\lambda_{i}(t)=0
for
i=2,3
then
\zeta(t)
,
\eta(t)
and
\xi(t)
would consist of real functions and if we denote
\operatorname{R}^{+}_{11}:=\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathbf{R}^{+}_{p}{\omega_{1}^{+}},\omega_{1}^{+}\rangle
,

\eta(t)=:2\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}(t)}{\varepsilon(t)}+\operatorname{R}^{+}_{11}\right),\quad t\leq T,\quad\varepsilon(T)=\exp(-\lambda_{1}T).

Equivalently,
\varepsilon(t)=\exp\left(-\operatorname{R}^{+}_{11}t+\int_{t}^{T}\eta(s)\,ds\right)
,
t\leq T
and,

\xi(t):=\eta(t)-(2\pi^{2})^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{-4}\operatorname{R}^{+}_{11}\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{1}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))},\quad t\leq T.

Remark 8.5
.

In
[
BK17
]
, the definition of
\varepsilon(t)^{2}=|\zeta(t)|
is:
\eta(t)=4\varepsilon^{\prime}(t)\varepsilon(t)^{-5}-32\omega
for
t\leq T
,
\varepsilon(T)^{-4}=32\omega(-T)
, where
\omega
is a numerical constant. The presence of an additional
\varepsilon(t)^{-4}
factor is explained by the degenerate curvature of the orbifold
\mathbb{T}^{4}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
which satisfies
\mathbf{R}^{+}
. One needs a higher order of precision to detect
\mathbf{R}^{+}\propto\varepsilon^{4}
as computed in
[
Ozu21
]
, and symmetry assumptions are necessary to “force” the flow to stay in the direction of the unique positive eigenvalue of
\mathbf{R}^{+}
.

8.2.
Set-up for immortal solutions

We now develop the notations in the immortal case with
0<T\leq t<+\infty
, the heuristics are essentially the same as in the ancient case. Let
T\geq 0
and
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
. Denote the largest eigenvalue of
2\mathbf{R}^{+}_{p}
by
\Lambda_{0}
assumed to be negative. Define also an approximate bound for the size of bubbles by

(8.11)

\varepsilon_{0}(t):=e^{\frac{\Lambda_{0}}{2}t},\quad t\geq T.

•

The family of symmetric
2
-tensors
(k(t))_{t\geq T}
is thought of as a metric perturbation of the first approximation
\tilde{g}(t)
(which is not defined yet) lying in
C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
, such that
h(T)\equiv 0
.

•

The vector
\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
lies in the following function space:

\|\eta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}:=\sup_{t\,\geq\,T}\left\{e^{\eta_{0}t}\left(|\eta(t)|+[\eta]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon_{0}(t)}\right)\right\}<+\infty,

where
\eta_{0}>0
will be chosen appropriately in Lemma
8.1
.

•

The function
\beta
lies in the following function space:

\|\beta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}}:=\sup_{t\,\geq\,T}\left\{e^{\beta_{0}t}\left(|\beta(t)|+[\beta]_{\alpha,t,1}\right)\right\}<+\infty,

where
\beta_{0}>0
will be chosen in [(

H
1

), Section
8.3
].

We define a function space
\mathcal{X}
as follows:

(8.12)

\begin{split}\mathcal{X}&:=C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}\times C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}\times C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T},\\
\\
\|(k,\eta,\beta)\|_{\mathcal{X}}&:=\|k\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}+\|\beta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}}.\end{split}

The normed space
\mathcal{X}
is a Banach space, its unit ball will be denoted by
\mathcal{B}.

For
(k,\eta,\beta)\in\mathcal{B}
, we formally define a map
\Phi(k,\eta,\beta)=:(h,\xi,\nu)
whose fixed point set is made of ancient solutions to the DeTurck Ricci flow only. More precisely,

(1)

Given
\eta\in C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}
such that
\|\eta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}\leq 1
, we define
\zeta(t)
through the following differential equation:

(8.13)

\dot{\zeta}(t)=2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}(\zeta(t))+\eta(t),\quad t\geq T,\quad\zeta(T):=\exp(2T\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+})\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\},

or equivalently through a more explicit formula for
\zeta(t)
:

(8.14)

\zeta(t)=\exp\left(2t\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}\right)\zeta_{0}+\int_{T}^{t}\exp\left(2(t-s)\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}\right)\eta(s)\,ds,\quad t\geq T.

We need to ensure that
\zeta(t)
stays in
\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
in order to make sense of
g_{\zeta(t)}
.
As in the ancient case, flipping the signs of
t
and
T
, we use Lemma
8.1
, to define
\zeta(t)
and hence
\tilde{g}(t)
whenever
\|\eta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}\leq 1
and
\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
lies outside a sufficiently large ball whose radius depends on
\eta_{0}
and the spectrum of
2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}
.

(2)

Non-linear terms coming from the linearization of the Ricci tensor involving
k\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
with
|k(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\leq 1/2
for
t\geq T
are incorportated in the data
\psi
:

(8.15)

\psi(t):=-Q_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t))+\beta(t)k(t)-\left(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))-2\Lambda\tilde{g}(t)\right),

where
Q_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t))
is defined and controlled as in the ancient case.

Here notice that the underlying Einstein orbifold has no non-trivial kernel as proved in Proposition
5.15
. In particular, there is an additional simplification compared to the ancient case that needs to take conformal diffeomorphisms into account.

(3)

The first component
h
and the last component
\nu
from the triplet
(h,\xi,\nu)
are then defined as follows: the tensor
h
is the unique solution to the linear (Lichnerowicz) heat equation modulo obstructions which vanishes at
t=T
with the non-homogeneous term
\psi
defined previously:

(8.16)

\partial_{t}h(t)-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}h(t)-2\Lambda h(t)=\psi(t)+\lambda_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+\nu(t)\tilde{g}(t),\quad t\geq T,

whose existence is ensured by Proposition
7.20
once
\psi
lies in
C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
, a fact that will be checked in Proposition
8.6
.

(4)

The vectorial function
\xi(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
is then obtained by taking into account obstructions coming from
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
which are formally encoded by
\eta
and
(\lambda_{i})_{i}
:

(8.17)

d_{\zeta(t)}g(\xi(t))=d_{\zeta(t)}g(\eta(t))-\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}(t)\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\frac{\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))}{\|\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\|^{2}_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}},\quad t\geq T.

8.3.
Choice of the parameters and estimates of the non-homogeneous term

In this section, we restrict ourselves to the ancient case, that is the setting of Section
8.1
.

The purpose of this section is to estimate each element of the image of a vector in
\mathcal{B}
under the map
\Phi
defined in the previous Section
8.1
.

Before doing so, we make a choice of all the parameters introduced in Section
8.1
.

We consider
T\leq 0
(or
T\geq 0
in the immortal case),
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
,
\sigma\in(0,1/2)
. Let
\eta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\iota\in(0,2\sigma^{-1}-4)
and
\delta\in\left(\frac{5}{9},\frac{3-(1+\iota)\sigma}{5-\sigma}\right)
such that:

(
\mathcal{H}_{1}
)

0<\iota\sigma<2(\eta_{0}/\Lambda_{0}-1)<(5-\sigma)(1-\delta)-2.

According to [
1a
, Lemma
8.1
], let us consider
\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
such that
(\eta_{0}-\Lambda_{0})|\zeta_{0}|>c_{0}
for some positive universal constant
c_{0}
. Then define:

\begin{split}&\delta(t):=\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{\delta},\quad\beta_{0}\in(6-10\delta,2]\ni\tau_{0},\quad\gamma(t):=\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2-(1+\iota)\sigma},\end{split}

so that
|\beta(t)|
and
|\tau(t)|
converges to
0
as
t
goes to
-\infty
at an exponential rate
\Lambda_{0}
and are bounded from above by
\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2}
. Moreover,
\tau_{0}
and
\beta_{0}
will be less than
8-10\delta
.

Observe that the conditions (

H
0

) are satisfied under the choices made in (

H
1

). Moreover, observe that any
k\in\mathcal{B}
converges to
0
together with its covariant derivatives (up to order
2
) and its time derivative since
\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2-\sigma}=\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{\iota\sigma}
. The upper bound on
\eta_{0}
will be made clearer in Proposition
8.10
from Section
8.4
.

Proposition 8.6
.

For a quadruplet
(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)\in\mathcal{B}
, recall the non-homogenous term:

\psi(t):=-Q_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t))+\beta(t)k(t)+\tau(t)\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)}k(t)-\left(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))-2\Lambda\tilde{g}(t)\right).

Then
\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)-\iota\sigma}
and,

\begin{split}|\langle\psi(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}&+\langle d_{\zeta(t)}g(\eta(t)),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}|\\
&\leq C\left(\gamma(t)^{-2}\varepsilon(t)^{2-2\sigma}+\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma+\beta_{0}}+\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma+\tau_{0}}+\varepsilon(t)^{9}\delta(t)^{-7}\right),\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{9-7\delta},\\
\left|\langle\psi(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\left(\varepsilon(t)^{\beta_{0}}\gamma(t)^{-1}+\varepsilon(t)^{\tau_{0}}\gamma(t)^{-1}+\gamma(t)^{-2}+\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10}\right)\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{8-10\delta},\\
\left|\langle\psi(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|&\leq C\left(\varepsilon(t)^{\beta_{0}}\gamma(t)^{-1}+\varepsilon(t)^{\tau_{0}}\gamma(t)^{-1}+\gamma(t)^{-2}+\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10}\right)\\
&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{8-10\delta}.\end{split}

Proof.

We prove the result for the ancient case, the proof in the immortal case is analogous.

Claim 1
.

\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)-\iota\sigma}.

Proof of Claim
1
.

Since
\|k\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\leq 1
,

|Q_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t))|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\leq C(n)\left(|k(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),2}k(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}+|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}k(t)|^{2}_{\tilde{g}(t)}\right),

pointwise. Multiplying across the previous estimate with
\gamma(t)(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{2+\sigma}
gives:

\begin{split}\|Q_{\tilde{g}}(k)\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\gamma(t)^{-2}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-\sigma}\|k\|^{2}_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\leq\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{-\sigma},\end{split}

since again,
k
belongs to the unit ball with respect to the norm of
C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
.
By choice of
\gamma
with respect to
\varepsilon
made in (

H
1

), this gives

\|Q_{\tilde{g}}(k)\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2+\iota\sigma}.

Similarly,

\|\beta\cdot k\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}|\beta(t)|.

Since
\beta(t)
is
O(\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{\beta_{0}})
, this gives

\|\beta\cdot k\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{\beta_{0}}.

A similar reasoning applies to
\tau(t)\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)}k(t)
. Indeed, thanks to Lemma
3.11
,

(8.18)

\begin{split}|\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)}k(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}&\leq C\left(|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}k(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}+|k(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),2}\tilde{v}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\right)\\
&\leq C\|k\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\gamma(t)^{-1}\left((\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-\sigma-1}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})+(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-\sigma}\right)\\
&\leq C\|k\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\gamma(t)^{-1}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-\sigma},\end{split}

one easily gets:

\|\tau\cdot\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}}\tilde{v}}k\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}|\tau(t)|.

This allows us to conclude that
\|\tau\cdot\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}}\tilde{v}}k\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{\tau_{0}}
by choice of
\tau
in (

H
1

).

Now, thanks to Lemma
3.8
,

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g})-2\Lambda\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\Bigg(\varepsilon(t)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}+\sigma}+\delta(t)^{4+\sigma}+\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{\sigma^{+}}\\
&\quad+\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-5+\sigma}+\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8+\sigma}\Bigg)\\
&\leq\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}+\sigma},\end{split}

thanks to (

H
1

) which imposes
0<\iota\sigma<2(\eta_{0}/\Lambda_{0}-1)<(5-\sigma)(1-\delta)-2
. By choice of
\gamma
with respect to
\delta
in (

H
1

), we also get

\|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g})-2\Lambda\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)-\iota\sigma}.

which leads to the first conclusion, i.e.
\lim_{T\rightarrow+\infty}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}=0
.
∎

Claim 2
.

\begin{split}|\langle\psi(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)&\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}+\langle d_{\zeta(t)}g(\dot{\zeta}(t)-2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}(\zeta(t))),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}|\\
&\leq C\left(\gamma(t)^{-2}\varepsilon(t)^{2-2\sigma}+\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma+\beta_{0}}+\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma+\tau_{0}}+\varepsilon(t)^{9}\delta(t)^{-7}\right)\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{9-7\delta}.\end{split}

Proof of Claim
2
.

Let us now estimate the projection of
\psi
against
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
.
By definition of the norm
C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
, one gets:

\begin{split}\left|\int_{M}\langle Q_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t)),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\right|&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-2}\int_{M}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2-2\sigma}|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-2}\varepsilon(t)^{2-2\sigma}.\end{split}

Similarly, since
\sigma>0
,

\begin{split}\left|\int_{M}\langle\beta(t)k(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\right|\leq C|\beta(t)|\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}.\end{split}

And similarly, based on (
8.18
),

\begin{split}\left|\int_{M}\langle\tau(t)\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)}k(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\right|\leq C|\tau(t)|\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}.\end{split}

As an intermediate step,

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{M}\langle\psi(t)+\left(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))-2\Lambda\tilde{g}(t)\right),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\right|\\
&\leq C\left(\gamma(t)^{-2}\varepsilon(t)^{2-2\sigma}+\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma+\beta_{0}}+\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma+\tau_{0}}\right).\end{split}

Proposition
4.2
gives in turn:

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{M}\langle\psi(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}+\langle d_{\zeta(t)}g(\dot{\zeta}(t)-2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}(\zeta(t))),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}\right|\leq\\
&C\left(\gamma(t)^{-2}\varepsilon(t)^{2-2\sigma}+\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma+\beta_{0}}+\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma+\tau_{0}}+\varepsilon(t)^{9}\delta(t)^{-7}\right),\end{split}

which is exactly the first desired estimate by definition of
\zeta
in (
8.3
).

The second estimate follows directly from this first estimate together with the choice of parameters made in (

H
1

) since
2+\iota\sigma>2>2\delta
.
∎

Let us now estimate the projection of
\psi
against
\tilde{g}(t)
.
Since
\sigma\in(0,1/2)\subset(0,2)
, the function
(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2\sigma-2}
is integrable on
M
uniformly in time and,

\begin{split}\left|\langle Q_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t)),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)})}\right|\leq C\gamma(t)^{-2}.\end{split}

Similarly,

\begin{split}\left|\langle\beta(t)k(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)})}\right|\leq C|\beta(t)|\gamma(t)^{-1},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\left|\int_{M}\langle\tau(t)\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)}k(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\right|\leq C|\tau(t)|\gamma(t)^{-1}.\end{split}

To summarize the three previous estimates, we get:

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{M}\langle\psi(t)+\left(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))-2\Lambda\tilde{g}(t)\right),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\right|\\
&\leq C\left(\gamma(t)^{-2}+\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\beta_{0}}+\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\tau_{0}}\right).\end{split}

Based on Proposition
4.1
, we obtain the desired estimate:

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{M}\langle\psi(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\right|\leq C\left(\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\beta_{0}}+\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\tau_{0}}+\gamma(t)^{-2}+\varepsilon(t)^{10}\delta(t)^{-8}\right).\end{split}

We are finally in a position to estimate the projection of
\psi
against
\tilde{c}(t)
. Since
\tilde{c}(t)
is bounded uniformly in time by Lemma
3.11
,

\begin{split}\left|\int_{M}\langle Q_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t)),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\right|&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-2}\int_{M}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2-2\sigma}|\tilde{c}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-2}.\end{split}

Similarly,

\begin{split}\left|\int_{M}\langle\beta(t)k(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\right|&\leq C|\beta(t)|\gamma(t)^{-1},\\
\left|\int_{M}\langle\tau(t)\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)}k(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\right|&\leq C|\tau(t)|\gamma(t)^{-1}.\end{split}

Based on Proposition
4.5
and (

H
1

) (i.e.
\beta_{0}
and
\tau_{0}
less than
8-10\delta
), we get the desired estimate:

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{M}\langle\psi(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\right|\leq C\left(\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\beta_{0}}+\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\tau_{0}}+\gamma(t)^{-2}+\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10}\right)\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{8-10\delta}.\end{split}

∎

Next, we establish the corresponding Hölder estimates of Proposition
8.6
:

Proposition 8.7
.

For a quadruplet
(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)\in\mathcal{B}
, recall the non-homogenous term:

(8.19)

\psi(t):=-Q_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t))+\beta(t)k(t)+\tau(t)\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)}k(t)-\left(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))-2\Lambda\tilde{g}(t)\right).

Then
\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)-\iota\sigma}
and,

\begin{split}&\left[\langle\psi,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}+\langle d_{\zeta}g(\eta)),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta)\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\leq C\left(\gamma(t)^{-2}\varepsilon(t)^{2-2\sigma}+\left(r^{2}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,r}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{9^{-}}\delta(t)^{-7^{-}}\right),\\
&\left[\langle\psi,\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\min\{\beta_{0},\tau_{0}\}}+C\left(r^{2}+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\,\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10},\\
&\left[\langle\psi,\tilde{c}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\min\{\beta_{0},\tau_{0}\}}+C\left(r^{2}+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\,\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10}.\end{split}

Proof.

Let us first estimate the Hölder norm of
\psi
. Regarding the quadratic term
Q_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t))
, we invoke Lemma
C.2
together with Lemma
6.6
and Proposition
8.6
to get:

(8.20)

\begin{split}\|Q_{\tilde{g}}(k)\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2+\iota\sigma}.\end{split}

Now, Lemma
6.6
and the fact that
(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)\in\mathcal{B}
imply:

\begin{split}\|\beta\cdot k\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{\beta_{0}}+\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\varepsilon(t)^{\beta_{0}}\|\beta\|_{C^{0}_{\beta_{0},T}}\|k\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\varepsilon(t)^{2}\|\beta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}}\|k\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{\beta_{0}}.\end{split}

A similar estimate on
\|\tau\cdot\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}}\tilde{v}}k\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}
holds:

(8.21)

\begin{split}\|\tau\cdot\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}}\tilde{v}}k\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{\tau_{0}}.\end{split}

Invoking Lemma
3.16
gives:

(8.22)

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g})-2\Lambda\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)-\iota\sigma}.\end{split}

The combination of estimates (
8.20
), (
8.21
) and (
8.22
) leads to the expected estimate thanks to (

H
1

) that ensures that
2\geq\min\{\beta_{0},\tau_{0}\}>6-10\delta>(5-\sigma)(1-\delta)-2>2(\eta_{0}/\Lambda_{0}-1).

As for the Hölder norm of the orthogonal projections under consideration, observe first that thanks to Lemma
D.8
, the fact that
\|k\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\leq 1
and Lemma
3.10
,

\begin{split}\left[\int_{M}\langle Q_{\tilde{g}}(k),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{\tilde{g}}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-2}\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\varepsilon(t)^{2-2\sigma}.\end{split}

Similarly, since
\sigma>0
, the fact that
\|k\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\leq 1
, and Lemma
3.10
,

\begin{split}\left[\int_{M}\langle\beta\cdot k,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{\tilde{g}}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\beta_{0}+4-\sigma}\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\|\beta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}}.\end{split}

And similarly, based on (
8.18
),

\begin{split}\left[\int_{M}\langle\tau\cdot\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}}\tilde{v}}k,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{\tilde{g}}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\tau_{0}+4-\sigma}\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\|\tau\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}}.\end{split}

As an intermediate step, the definition of
\psi
recalled in (
8.19
) together with the three previous estimates give,

\begin{split}&\left[\int_{M}\langle\psi+\left(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g})-2\Lambda\tilde{g}\right),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{\tilde{g}}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\leq C\left(\gamma(t)^{-2}\varepsilon(t)^{2-2\sigma}+\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\min\{\beta_{0},\tau_{0}\}+4-\sigma}\right)\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-2}\varepsilon(t)^{2-2\sigma}\left(1+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha}\right).\end{split}

Here we have used the fact that
\iota\sigma<\min\{\beta_{0},\tau_{0}\}
in the last inequality.

Thanks to Lemma
4.7
, (

H
1

) and the triangular inequality, we get the desired first estimate since
\gamma(t)^{-2}\varepsilon(t)^{2-2\sigma}<\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4}
.

Let us now estimate the projection of
\psi
against
\tilde{g}(t)
.
Since
\sigma+\alpha\in(0,1)
thanks to (

H
1

), the function
(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2-2\sigma-2\alpha}
is integrable on
M
uniformly in time and since
\|k\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\leq 1
,

\begin{split}\left[\langle Q_{\tilde{g}}(k),\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-2}.\end{split}

Similarly,

\begin{split}\left[\langle\beta\cdot k,\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\beta_{0}}\|\beta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\left[\int_{M}\langle\tau\cdot\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}}\tilde{v}}k,\tilde{g}\rangle_{\tilde{g}}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\tau_{0}}\|\tau\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}}.\end{split}

To summarize the three previous estimates, we get thanks to the definition of
\gamma
in (

H
1

):

\begin{split}&\left[\int_{M}\langle\psi+\left(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g})-2\Lambda\tilde{g}\right),\tilde{g}\rangle_{\tilde{g}}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\gamma(t)^{-1}+\varepsilon(t)^{\min\{\beta_{0},\tau_{0}\}}\right)\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\min\{\beta_{0},\tau_{0}\}}.\end{split}

Based on Lemma
4.7
, we obtain the desired estimate:

\begin{split}&\left[\int_{M}\langle\psi,\tilde{g}\rangle_{\tilde{g}}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\min\{\beta_{0},\tau_{0}\}}+C\left(r^{2}+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\,\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10}.\end{split}

We are finally in a position to estimate the projection of
\psi
against
\tilde{c}(t)
. Since
\tilde{c}(t)
is bounded uniformly in time by Lemma
3.11
, and based on Lemma
4.7
and (

H
1

), we get the desired estimate:

\begin{split}&\left[\int_{M}\langle\psi,\tilde{c}\rangle_{\tilde{g}}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{\min\{\beta_{0},\tau_{0}\}}+C\left(r^{2}+r^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2\alpha}\right)\,\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10}.\end{split}

∎

Next, we analyse each element of the image of a quadruplet
(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)
under the map
\Phi
in view of the previous estimates. This is echoing
[
BK17
, Corollary
7.2
]
.

Corollary 8.8
.

If
(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)
lies in the ball
\mathcal{B}
, then
(h,\xi,\nu,\mu):=\Phi(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)
satisfies:

(1)

The
C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
-norm of
h
is arbitrarily small as
T
tends to
-\infty
:
\lim_{T\rightarrow-\infty}\|h\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}=0
.

(2)

The coefficients
(\lambda_{i})_{i}
satisfy for
t\leq T
,

\sum_{i}|\lambda_{i}(t)|\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}.\\

(3)

The vector
\xi
satisfies for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}&|\xi(t)|\leq C\sum_{i}\left|\left\langle d_{\zeta(t)}g(\xi(t)),\frac{\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))}{\|\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\|_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}\right|\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2+(2+\sigma)(1-\delta)+\iota\sigma}.\end{split}

(4)

The function
\nu
satisfies for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}|\nu(t)|&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{8-10\delta}.\end{split}

(5)

The function
\mu
satisfies for
t\leq T
,

|\mu(t)|\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{8-10\delta}.

Proof.

The estimate on
h
is straightforward by combining Propositions
7.20
together with Proposition
8.7
:

(8.23)

\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\leq C\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\rightarrow 0,\quad\text{as $T\rightarrow-\infty$.}

Regarding the penultimate estimate, the combination of Propositions
7.7
and
8.6
gives by adding and subtracting the data
\psi
in the scalar product:

\begin{split}|\nu(t)|\langle\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\delta(t)^{2}\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}\delta(t)^{2}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10},\end{split}

since
\delta>1/2
thanks to (

H
1

). Here we have used the uniform boundedness of
h
proved in (
8.23
) and that of
\psi
established in Proposition
8.6
. We also have made use of Corollary
7.11

in the first inequality.

As for the estimate on
\mu(t)
, Propositions
7.10
and
8.6
together with Corollary
7.11
give by adding and subtracting
\psi
similarly to the previous estimate on
|\nu(t)|
:

\begin{split}|\mu(t)|&\langle\tilde{c}(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sum_{i}|\lambda_{i}(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10}\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}+C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10}\\
&\leq C\delta(t)^{8-10\delta},\end{split}

since
\delta>1/2
thanks to (

H
1

).

As for the estimate on
\xi
, the combination of Propositions
7.4
and
8.6
leads to:

(8.24)

\begin{split}\Big|\lambda_{i}(t)&\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}-\langle d_{\zeta(t)}g(\dot{\zeta}(t)-2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}(\zeta(t))),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}\Big|\leq\\
&C\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}+C\varepsilon^{4}(t)\delta(t)^{2}|\nu(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}|\mu(t)|\\
&+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{9}\delta(t)^{-7}\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}+C\varepsilon^{4}(t)\delta(t)^{2}|\nu(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}|\mu(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{9}\delta(t)^{-7},\end{split}

where we have used the uniform boundedness of
h
proved in (
8.23
) and that of
\psi
established in Proposition
8.6
.

Recalling the definition of
\xi
given in (
8.10
) with respect to
\eta
given in (
8.3
), the previously established estimates on
\nu(t)
and
\mu(t)
let us conclude:

\begin{split}\left|\langle d_{\zeta(t)}g(\xi(t)),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}\right|&=\left|\langle d_{\zeta(t)}g(\eta(t)),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}-\lambda_{i}(t)\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\left(\gamma(t)^{-1}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma}+\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10}+\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-7}\right).\end{split}

To show that
\left|\langle d_{\zeta(t)}g(\xi(t)),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}\right|\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4+(2+\sigma)(1-\delta)+\iota\sigma}
, observe that by (

H
1

), the smaller exponent of
\varepsilon_{0}(t)
in the previous estimate is
(2+\sigma)(1-\delta)+\iota\sigma<5-7\delta<8-10\delta
since
(5-\sigma)\delta<3-(1+\iota)\sigma.

Finally, the estimate on the coefficients
\lambda_{i}(t)
follows from the previous estimate on
\xi(t)
and the triangular inequality:

\begin{split}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4}|\lambda_{i}(t)|&\leq\left|\langle d_{\zeta(t)}g(\xi(t)),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}\right|+\left|\langle d_{\zeta(t)}g(\eta(t)),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}\right|\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4+(2+\sigma)(1-\delta)+\iota\sigma}+C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4+2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4+2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)},\end{split}

as expected. Here we have made use of the constraint and
(2+\sigma)(1-\delta)+\iota\sigma>(2+\sigma)(1-\delta)>(5-\sigma)(1-\delta)-2>2(\eta_{0}/\Lambda_{0}-1)
from (

H
1

) in the last estimate.

∎

Corollary 8.9
.

If
(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)
lies in the ball
\mathcal{B}
, then
(h,\xi,\nu,\mu):=\Phi(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)
satisfies:

(1)

The coefficients
(\lambda_{i})_{i}
satisfy for
t\leq T
,

\sum_{i}[\lambda_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}.\\

(2)

The vector
\xi
satisfies for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}&[\xi]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon(t)}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2+(2+\sigma+2\alpha)(1-\delta)+\iota\sigma}.\end{split}

(3)

The function
\nu
satisfies for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}[\nu]_{\alpha,t,1}&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{8-(10+2\alpha)\delta}.\end{split}

(4)

The function
\mu
satisfies for
t\leq T
,

[\mu]_{\alpha,t,1}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{8-(10+2\alpha)\delta}.

Proof.

Regarding the penultimate estimate, the combination of Proposition
7.17
, Corollary
7.19
and Proposition
8.7
gives by adding and subtracting the data
\psi
in the scalar product:

\begin{split}[\nu(t)\mathop{\rm Vol}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}M]_{\alpha,t,1}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-2\alpha}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}\delta(t)^{2}\|\psi\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10-2\alpha}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-10-2\alpha},\end{split}

where we have used the uniform boundedness of
h
proved in (
8.23
) and that of
\psi
established in Proposition
8.7
together with (

H
1

).

Similarly, Proposition
7.18
, Corollary
7.19
and Proposition
8.7
give:

\begin{split}[\mu\cdot\langle\tilde{c},\tilde{c}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}]_{\alpha,t,1}&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4-2\alpha}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma-2\alpha}\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta^{-10-2\alpha}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{8-(10+2\alpha)\delta}.\end{split}

As for the estimate on
\xi
, the combination of Propositions
7.14
and
8.7
leads to:

(8.25)

\begin{split}&\left[\lambda_{i}\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}-\langle d_{\zeta}g(\eta),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta)\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta})}\right]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon}\leq C\left(\gamma(t)^{-2}\varepsilon(t)^{2-2\sigma}+\frac{[\dot{\zeta}]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon(t)}}{\varepsilon(t)^{2}}\varepsilon(t)^{9^{-}}\delta(t)^{-7^{-}}\right)\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\varepsilon(t)^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma-2\alpha}+\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}\right)\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{6-\sigma}\delta(t)^{-4}\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}[\nu]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon(t)}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}[\mu]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon(t)}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4+(2+\sigma)(1-\delta)+\iota\sigma}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}[\nu]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon(t)}+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}[\mu]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon(t)},\end{split}

where we have used the uniform boundedness of
h
proved in (
8.23
) and that of
\psi
established in Proposition
8.7
together with (

H
1

) since
9-7\delta>4+(2+\sigma)(1-\delta)+\iota\sigma
. Indeed, this condition is equivalent to
(5-\sigma)\delta<(3-\sigma-\iota\sigma).

Recalling the definition of
\xi
given in (
8.10
) with respect to
\eta
given in (
8.3
), the previously established estimates on
