\nu(t)
and
\mu(t)
let us conclude:

\begin{split}&\left[\langle d_{\zeta}g(\xi),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta)\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta})}\right]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon(t)}=\left[\langle d_{\zeta}g(\eta),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta)\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta})}-\lambda_{i}\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon(t)}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4+(2+\sigma)(1-\delta)+\iota\sigma},\end{split}

which gives the first desired estimate once we divide by the
L^{2}
norm of
\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))
.

Finally, the Hölder estimate on the coefficients
\lambda_{i}(t)
follows from the previous Hölder estimate on
\xi(t)
and the triangular inequality:

\begin{split}\left[\lambda_{i}\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon(t)}&\leq\left[\langle d_{\zeta}g(\xi),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta)\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta})}\right]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon(t)}+\left[\langle d_{\zeta}g(\eta),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta)\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta})}\right]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon(t)}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4+(2+\sigma)(1-\delta)+\iota\sigma}+C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4+2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4+2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}.\end{split}

Here we have used that
[\eta]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon(t)}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}
by definition of
\|\eta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}
. Here also, we have made use of the constraint
2(\eta_{0}/\Lambda_{0}-1)<(5-\sigma)(1-\delta)-2<(2+\sigma)(1-\delta)+\iota\sigma
from (

H
1

) in the last estimate.

Once the previous estimate is divided by
\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}
, one gets the expected estimate by invoking Corollary
8.8
and Lemma
4.6
on
\big[\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\big]_{\alpha,t,\varepsilon(t)}
.
∎

8.4.
A fixed point argument and choice of the parameters

In this section, we restrict ourselves to the ancient case, that is the setting of Section
8.1
.

More precisely, we reformulate the previous estimates obtained in Section
8.3
in terms of the function space
\mathcal{X}
and the map
\Phi
introduced in Section
8.1
.
Our main result Proposition
8.10
of this section is that a fixed point of the map
\Phi
is an ancient solution to the Ricci flow up to rescalings and reparametrizations by a family of diffeomorphisms.

If
0<\iota^{\prime}<\iota
,
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,\alpha)
,
\beta_{0}^{\prime}\in(0,\beta_{0})
, and
\eta^{\prime}_{0}\in(\Lambda_{0},\eta_{0})
, denote by
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
the function space defined as in (
8.12
).

Proposition 8.10
.

Let
\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
,
\eta_{0}>\Lambda_{0}
such that
(\eta_{0}-\Lambda_{0})|\zeta_{0}|>c_{0}
for some universal positive constant
c_{0}
and let
\mathcal{X}
be defined with parameters satisfying (

H
1

).
The spaces
\mathcal{B}\subset\mathcal{X}
,
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
and the map
\Phi
satisfy:

(1)

The identity map
\mathop{\rm Id}\nolimits:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
is a compact embedding. In particular,
\mathcal{B}
endowed with the topology induced by that of
\mathcal{X}
is a compact subset of
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
.

(2)

There exists
T\leq 0
sufficiently small such that
\Phi(\mathcal{B})\subset\mathcal{B}
.

(3)

The map
\Phi
is continuous on
\mathcal{B}
with respect to the topology induced by that of
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
.

In particular, there exists a fixed point
(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)
of
\Phi
in
\mathcal{B}
, i.e. there exists an ancient solution
g(t):=\tilde{g}(t)+k(t)
to the following approximate DeTurck Ricci flow:

\begin{split}\partial_{t}g(t)&=-2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g(t))+(2\Lambda+\beta(t))g(t)+\mathcal{L}_{V(t)}(g(t)),\\
g(t)(V(t),\cdot)&:=\frac{\tau(t)}{2}g(t)(\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t),\cdot)+B_{\tilde{g}(t)}(g(t)-\tilde{g}(t)).\end{split}

Proof.

Let
(k_{i},\eta_{i},\beta_{i},\tau_{i})_{i}\in\mathcal{X}
be a bounded sequence. By rescaling, we can assume that
(k_{i},\eta_{i},\beta_{i},\tau_{i})_{i}
lies in
\mathcal{B}
, the unit ball of
\mathcal{X}:=C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}\times C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}\times C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}\times C^{0,\alpha}_{\tau_{0},T}
. Proving the first point is equivalent to showing that each component of the vector
(k_{i},\eta_{i},\beta_{i},\tau_{i})_{i}
admits a subsequence converging with respect to the corresponding norm. We only give a proof for
C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}
, the extraction of a convergent subsequence with respect to the other norms is either identical or can be adapted easily.

Recall that for
T^{\prime}<T
, the embedding
\mathop{\rm Id}\nolimits:C^{0,\alpha}([T^{\prime},T])\rightarrow C^{0,\alpha^{\prime}}([T^{\prime},T])
is compact for
\alpha^{\prime}<\alpha
so that by a diagonal argument, there exists a subsequence still denoted by
(\eta_{i})_{i}
converging to a function
\eta
in
C_{loc}^{0,\alpha^{\prime}}((-\infty,T])
. Moreover, the function
\eta
lies in
\mathcal{B}
by pointwise convergence. It remains to show that
(\eta_{i})_{i}
converges to
\eta
with respect to the norm
\|\cdot\|_{C^{0,\alpha^{\prime}}_{\eta_{0}^{\prime},T}}
. Indeed, there exists a positive constant
C
such that for
T^{\prime}<2T
,

\begin{split}\|\eta-\eta_{i}\|_{C^{0,\alpha^{\prime}}_{\eta_{0}^{\prime},T^{\prime}}}&\leq\|\eta\|_{C^{0,\alpha^{\prime}}_{\eta_{0}^{\prime},T^{\prime}}}+\|\eta_{i}\|_{C^{0,\alpha^{\prime}}_{\eta_{0}^{\prime},T^{\prime}}}\leq C\exp^{(\eta_{0}-\eta^{\prime}_{0})T^{\prime}}\left(\|\eta\|_{C^{0,\alpha^{\prime}}_{\eta_{0},T}}+\|\eta_{i}\|_{C^{0,\alpha^{\prime}}_{\eta_{0},T}}\right)\\
&\leq C\exp^{(\eta_{0}-\eta^{\prime}_{0})T^{\prime}}\left(\|\eta\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}+\|\eta_{i}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}\right)\\
&\leq 2C\exp^{(\eta_{0}-\eta^{\prime}_{0})T^{\prime}}.\end{split}

In particular, if
\epsilon>0
is given, there exists
T^{\prime}<2T
such that
\|\eta-\eta_{i}\|_{C^{0,\alpha^{\prime}}_{\eta_{0}^{\prime},T^{\prime}}}\leq\epsilon

since
\eta_{0}^{\prime}<\eta_{0}
.

Then there exists an index
i_{0}
depending on
T^{\prime}
such that for
i\geq i_{0}
,

\sup_{t\in[T^{\prime},T]}\left\{e^{-\eta_{0}^{\prime}t}\left(|\eta(t)-\eta_{i}(t)|+\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\alpha^{\prime}}\sup_{0\,<\,t-t^{\prime}\,\leq\,\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2}}\frac{|(\eta(t)-\eta(t^{\prime}))-(\eta_{i}(t)-\eta_{i}(t^{\prime}))|}{|t-t^{\prime}|^{\alpha^{\prime}}}\right)\right\}\leq\epsilon,

so that
\|\eta-\eta_{i}\|_{C^{0,\alpha^{\prime}}_{\eta_{0}^{\prime},T^{\prime}}}\leq 2\epsilon
for
i\geq i_{0}
as expected.

Let us now show that there exists
T\leq 0
sufficiently small such that
\Phi
preserves the unit ball
\mathcal{B}
in order to prove the second point. Let
(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)\in\mathcal{B}
and denote
\Phi(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)=:(h,\xi,\nu,\mu)
as defined in Section
8.1
. Item
1
from Corollary
8.8
ensures that there exists
T_{0}
sufficiently small such that for
T\leq T_{0}
,
\|h\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\leq 1/4.

Now, [(
3
), Corollary
8.8
] and [(
2
), Corollary
8.9
] give by definition of the norm
C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}
:

\|\xi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2+(2+\sigma)(1-\delta)+\iota\sigma-2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\rightarrow 0,\quad\text{as $T\rightarrow-\infty$.}

Here we have made use of the constraint
(2+\sigma)(1-\delta)+\iota\sigma>2\eta_{0}/\Lambda_{0}-2
from (

H
1

). Again, let us consider
T_{1}\leq T_{0}
sufficiently small such that for
T\leq T_{1}
,
\|\xi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}\leq 1/4
.

Finally, [(
4
), (
5
), Corollary
8.8
] and [(
3
), (
4
), Corollary
8.9
] give by definition of the norms
C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}
and
C^{0,\alpha}_{\tau_{0},T}
:

\|\nu\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}}+\|\mu\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\tau_{0},T}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{\min\{8-10\delta-\max\{\beta_{0},\tau_{0}\}\}}\rightarrow 0,\quad\text{as $T\rightarrow-\infty$.}

Therefore, there is some
T_{2}\leq T_{1}
sufficiently small such that
\|\nu\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}}+\|\mu\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\tau_{0},T}}\leq 1/2
if
T\leq T_{2}
.

We have then shown that
\|\Phi(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)\|_{\mathcal{X}}\leq 1
as promised for
T\leq T_{2}\leq 0
.

Finally, we show the third point: the map
\Phi
is continuous on
\mathcal{X}
with respect to the topology induced by that of
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
. Let
(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)
in
\mathcal{B}
and a sequence
(k_{i},\eta_{i},\beta_{i},\tau_{i})_{i}\in\mathcal{B}
converging to
(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)
with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}.
Then we know that
(\Phi(k_{i},\eta_{i},\beta_{i},\tau_{i}))_{i}
lies in
\mathcal{B}
since
\Phi(\mathcal{B})\subset\mathcal{B}
. Since
\mathop{\rm Id}\nolimits:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
is a compact embedding, there is a subsequence still denoted by
(\Phi(k_{i},\eta_{i},\beta_{i},\tau_{i}))_{i}
that converges to some element
(h,\xi,\nu,\mu)
lying in
\mathcal{B}
. It then suffices to show that
\Phi(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)=(h,\xi,\nu,\mu)
since in that case, the sequence
(\Phi(k_{i},\eta_{i},\beta_{i},\tau_{i}))_{i}
will have a unique accumulation point.

If
(h_{i},\xi_{i},\nu_{i},\mu_{i}):=\Phi(k_{i},\eta_{i},\beta_{i},\tau_{i})
then let
\tilde{g}_{i}(t)
be the corresponding background metric associated to
\eta_{i}
with parameter
\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
fixed once and for all. The sequence of metrics
\tilde{g}_{i}(t)
converges to
\tilde{g}(t)
corresponding to the parameter
\eta
in
C^{2,\alpha^{\prime}}_{loc}(M\times(-\infty,T])
. Similarly, denote the corresponding approximate kernel by
(\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{i}_{j}(t))_{1\,\leq\,j\,\leq\,3}
and
\tilde{c}_{i}(t):=\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}_{i}(t)}\tilde{v}_{i}(t)}\tilde{g}_{i}(t)
. Then by construction, the approximate kernel converges to that defined with respect to
\tilde{g}(t)
in
C^{0,\alpha^{\prime}}_{loc}(M\times(-\infty,T])
. Let
h_{i}
be the unique solution in
C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
to

(8.26)

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}h_{i}(t)-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{i}(t)}h_{i}(t)-2\Lambda h_{i}(t)=\sum_{j}\lambda^{i}_{j}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{i}_{j}(t)+\nu_{i}(t)\tilde{g}_{i}(t)+\mu_{i}(t)\tilde{c}_{i}(t)+\psi_{i}(t),\quad t\leq T,\\
&\psi_{i}(t):=-Q_{\tilde{g}_{i}(t)}(k_{i}(t))+\beta_{i}(t)k_{i}(t)+\frac{1}{2}\tau_{i}(t)\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}_{i}(t)}\tilde{v}_{i}(t)}k_{i}(t)-\left(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}_{i}(t)+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}_{i}(t))-2\Lambda\tilde{g}_{i}(t)\right).\end{split}

While the lefthand side of the equation satisfied by
h_{i}
converges to
\partial_{t}h(t)-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}h(t)-2\Lambda h(t)
in
C^{0,\alpha^{\prime}}_{loc}(M\times(-\infty,T])
, the data
\psi_{i}(t)
converges to the data
\psi(t)
defined with respect to
(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)
in
C^{0,\alpha^{\prime}}_{loc}(M\times(-\infty,T])
. Moreover,
\psi
lies in
C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
thanks to Proposition
8.6
and Proposition
8.7
. Finally, the definition of
\xi_{i}
in (
8.10
) in Section
8.1
forces the convergence in
C^{0,\alpha^{\prime}}_{loc}((-\infty,T])
of the coefficients
\lambda_{j}^{i}(t)
to those defining
\xi
with respect to
(k,\eta,\beta,\tau).
Finally, since each
h_{i}
is orthogonal to the approximate kernel defined with respect to
\tilde{g}_{i}(t)
,
h
is also orthogonal to the approximate kernel defined with respect to
\tilde{g}(t)
.
Therefore
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
solves (
8.26
) with data
\psi\in C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
and is orthogonal to the approximate kernel defined with respect to
\tilde{g}(t)
: it must be the unique solution provided by Proposition
7.20
which lets us conclude that
(h,\xi,\nu,\mu):=\Phi(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)
as expected.

Finally, the Schauder fixed point theorem ensures the existence of a fixed point
(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)
of
\Phi
in
\mathcal{B}
. In particular, observe that it implies that
\lambda_{i}(t)=0
for all
i
and
t\leq T
and that
k
solves:

\begin{split}\partial_{t}k(t)-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}k(t)-2\Lambda k(t)&=\psi(t)+\beta(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\tau(t)\tilde{c}(t)\\
&=-Q_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t))+\beta(t)k(t)+\frac{1}{2}\tau(t)\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)}k(t)\\
&\quad-\left(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))-2\Lambda\tilde{g}(t)\right)+\beta(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\tau(t)\tilde{c}(t)\\
&=-2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t)+k(t))+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}k(t)\\
&\quad+\mathcal{L}_{B_{\tilde{g}(t)}(k(t))}(\tilde{g}(t)+k(t))+\beta(t)k(t)+\frac{1}{2}\tau(t)\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\tilde{v}(t)}k(t)\\
&\quad-\left(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)+2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t))-2\Lambda\tilde{g}(t)\right)+\beta(t)\tilde{g}(t)+\tau(t)\tilde{c}(t)\\
&=-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}k(t)-\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)-2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}(t)+k(t))+2\Lambda\tilde{g}(t)\\
&\quad+\beta(t)(\tilde{g}(t)+k(t))+\mathcal{L}_{V(t)}(\tilde{g}(t)+k(t)),\end{split}

where the vector field
V(t)
is defined as in the statement of this proposition. This leads to the expected modified normalized DeTurck-Ricci flow.
∎

9.
Solutions through Picard’s fixed point theorem

This section ensures not only the existence of a fixed point for the map
\Phi
in the ball
\mathcal{B}
but also a fast iteration scheme through Picard’s fixed point theorem together with a continuous dependence on the parameter
\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}.

Theorem 9.1
.

Let
\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
,
\eta_{0}>\Lambda_{0}
such that
(\eta_{0}-\Lambda_{0})|\zeta_{0}|>c_{0}
for some universal positive constant
c_{0}
and let
\mathcal{X}
be defined with parameters satisfying (

H
1

).

Then there exists
T
sufficiently large such that
\Phi:\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
is a contraction: there exists
K\in(0,1)
such that for every
(k_{i},\eta_{i},\tau_{i},\beta_{i})\in\mathcal{B}
,
i=1,2
,

\left\|\Phi(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-\Phi(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}\leq K\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}.

In particular, there exists
T
sufficiently large such that
\Phi
admits a unique fixed point in
\mathcal{B}
, i.e. there exists
T
sufficiently large such that there exists a solution to the normalized DeTurck Ricci flow with background metric
\tilde{g}(t)
.

9.1.
Gram matrix of the approximate kernel

We describe the
L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
-projection of an arbitrary symmetric
2
-tensor on the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
: we restrict our discussion in the ancient case when
t<T<0
since the proof is completely analogous in the immortal case.

Given
h(t)\in L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))
, define the coefficients
L_{i}(t)
,
M(t)
and
N(t)
to be:

\pi(h(t))=:L_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+M(t)\tilde{g}(t)+N(t)\tilde{c}(t),

where
\pi
denotes the
L^{2}
-projection on the approximate kernel
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
with respect to
\tilde{g}(t).

Given
h\in C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
, we recall first how to express the coefficients
L_{i}(t)
,
M(t)
and
N(t)
in terms of
h
and the approximate kernel spanned by
(\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t))_{i}
,
\tilde{g}(t)
and
\tilde{c}(t)
. Let us denote by
G(t)
the Gram matrix of the set of vectors
(\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t))_{i}
and
\tilde{g}(t)
so that for
t\leq T
:

(9.1)

G(t)\begin{pmatrix}L_{1}(t)\\
L_{2}(t)\\
\vdots\\
M(t)\\
N(t)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\langle h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{1}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
\langle h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{2}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
\vdots\\
\langle h,\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\\
\langle h,\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\end{pmatrix}.

The next lemma gathers properties of the above Gram matrix:

Lemma 9.2
.

The Gram matrix satisfies:

(9.2)

\begin{split}&G(t)=\\
&\begin{pmatrix}2\pi^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4})&O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4})&\dots&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2})&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})\\
O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4})&2\pi^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{4}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4})&\dots&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2})&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2})&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2})&\dots&4\mathop{\rm Vol}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}M&0\\
O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})&\dots&0&\|\tilde{c}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t)}^{2}\end{pmatrix}.\end{split}

Proof.

The proof of (
9.2
) is a combination of Proposition
4.2
and Proposition
4.5
.
∎

Remark 9.3
.

If the elements
(\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta))_{i}
of
\ker_{L^{2}}(\Delta_{L,g_{\zeta}})
are not
L^{2}(g_{\zeta})
-orthogonal, as in the case of general hyperkähler ALE metrics (see Section
11
for their description), the resulting matrix may be more complicated. We would need to orthonormalize the appropriate natural basis before applying the techniques of this section to more general Ricci-flat ALE metrics.

The next lemma gathers properties of the inverse of the Gram matrix:

Lemma 9.4
.

The inverse of the Gram matrix
G(t)^{-1}
satisfies:

(9.3)

\begin{split}&G(t)^{-1}=\\
&\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4})}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{1}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}&O(\delta(t)^{-4})&\dots&O(\delta(t)^{2})&O(1)\\
O(\delta(t)^{-4})&\frac{1+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4})}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{2}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}&\ddots&O(\delta(t)^{2})&O(1)\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
O(\delta(t)^{2})&O(\delta(t)^{2})&\dots&\frac{1+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{4})}{4\mathop{\rm Vol}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}M}&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2})\\
O(1)&O(1)&\dots&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2})&\frac{1+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})}{\|\tilde{c}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}},\end{pmatrix}\end{split}

or equivalently,

(9.4)

\begin{split}L_{i}(t)&=(1+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4}))\frac{\langle h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4})\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{\langle h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}\\
&\quad+O(\delta(t)^{2})\langle h,\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}+O(1)\langle h,\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))},\\
M(t)&=(1+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{4}))\frac{\langle h,\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}{4\mathop{\rm Vol}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}M}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2})\sum_{i}\frac{\langle h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}\\
&\quad+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2})\langle h,\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))},\\
N(t)&=(1+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})\frac{\langle h,\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}{\|\tilde{c}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})\sum_{i}\frac{\langle h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}\\
&\quad+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2})\langle h,\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}.\end{split}

In particular,

(9.5)

\varepsilon(t)^{2}\sum_{i}|L_{i}(t)|+|M(t)|+|N(t)|\leq C\|\pi(h)\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))},

and,

(9.6)

\begin{split}\sum_{i}|L_{i}(t)|&\leq C\left(\varepsilon(t)^{-4}\sum_{j}|\langle h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}|+|\langle h,\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}|+|\langle h,\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}|\right)\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}(\varepsilon(t)^{-\sigma}+1)\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}.\end{split}

Proof.

Thanks to (
9.2
), one gets:

\begin{split}L_{i}(t)&=\frac{\langle h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4})\sum_{j\neq i}L_{j}(t)+O(\delta(t)^{2})M(t)+O(1)N(t),\\
M(t)&=\frac{\langle h,\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}{4\mathop{\rm Vol}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}M}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2})\sum_{i}L_{i}(t),\\
N(t)&=\frac{\langle h,\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}{\|\tilde{c}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})\sum_{i}L_{i}(t).\end{split}

This implies (
9.4
) which can be summarized as (
9.3
). As for (
9.5
), it is a direct consequence of (
9.4
).

Regarding the final estimate, once the previous estimate (
9.3
) on the inverse of
G(t)
is invoked, the result follows by using Lemmata
7.2
,
7.5
and
7.8
.
∎

The next lemma gathers properties of the derivative of the Gram matrix:

Lemma 9.5
.

The first derivative of the Gram matrix satisfies:

(9.7)

\begin{split}&G^{\prime}(t)=\\
&\begin{pmatrix}4\pi^{2}\langle\dot{\zeta}(t),\zeta(t)\rangle+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{8}}{\delta(t)^{4}}\right)&O\left(\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{8}}{\delta(t)^{4}}\right)&\dots&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}})&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})\\
O\left(\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{8}}{\delta(t)^{4}}\right)&4\pi^{2}\langle\dot{\zeta}(t),\zeta(t)\rangle+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{8}}{\delta(t)^{4}}\right)&\dots&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}})&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}})&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}})&\dots&O\left(\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{8}}{\delta(t)^{8}}\right)&0\\
O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})&\dots&0&O\left(\frac{\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}}{\delta(t)^{4^{-}}}\right)\end{pmatrix}.\end{split}

Proof.

Observe that Corollary
4.4
, Proposition
4.1
, Proposition
4.2
and Proposition
4.5
lead to the proof of (
9.7
).
∎

The last technical lemma of this section is stated for clarity of the formulas that will be needed in the next section. It can be skipped until it is being invoked.

Lemma 9.6
.

The following estimates hold:

(9.8)

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}&\langle h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}=\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\dot{L_{i}}(t)+\left\langle\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\dot{M}(t)+\left\langle\tilde{c}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\dot{N}(t)\\
&\quad+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4})\sum_{j\neq i}|\dot{L_{j}}(t)|+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})\sum_{i}|L_{i}(t)|+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}})|M(t)|+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})|N(t)|,\\
\frac{d}{dt}&\langle h,\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}=\left(4\mathop{\rm Vol}\nolimits_{\tilde{g}(t)}M\right)\dot{M}(t)+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2})\sum_{i}|\dot{L_{i}}(t)|\\
&\quad+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}})\sum_{i}|L_{i}(t)|+O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8})|M(t)|,\\
\frac{d}{dt}&\langle h,\tilde{c}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}=\|\tilde{c}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t)}^{2}\dot{N}(t)+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})\sum_{i}|\dot{L_{i}}(t)|\\
&\quad+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})\sum_{i}|L_{i}(t)|+O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{-4^{-}})|N(t)|,\end{split}

and,

(9.9)

\begin{split}&\Bigg|\frac{d}{dt}\langle h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}-\dot{L_{i}}(t)\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}-\dot{M}(t)\left\langle\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}-\dot{N}(t)\left\langle\tilde{c}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\Bigg|\leq\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4}\left|\frac{d}{dt}\sum_{i}\langle h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right|+C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-2}\left|\frac{d}{dt}\langle h,\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right|\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4}\left|\frac{d}{dt}\langle h,\tilde{c}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right|+C\varepsilon(t)^{2}\|\pi(h(t))\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}.\end{split}

Proof.

The estimates in (
9.8
) can be proved by differentiating the vector-valued relation (
9.1
) together with [(
9.7
), Lemma
9.5
].

Summing the first line of (
9.8
) over
i
gives by absorption:

\begin{split}&\sum_{i}|\dot{L_{i}}(t)|\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\leq\left|\frac{d}{dt}\sum_{i}\langle h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right|+|\dot{M}(t)|\sum_{i}|\left\langle\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}|\\
&+|\dot{N}(t)|\sum_{i}|\left\langle\tilde{c}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}|+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sum_{i}|L_{i}(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}}|M(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}|N(t)|.\end{split}

Now, according to Proposition
4.2
, Proposition
4.5
and [(
9.5
), Lemma
9.4
],

\begin{split}\sum_{i}|\dot{L_{i}}(t)|\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}&\leq\left|\frac{d}{dt}\sum_{i}\langle h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right|+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}|\dot{M}(t)|\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}|\dot{N}(t)|+C\varepsilon(t)^{2}\|\pi(h(t))\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}.\end{split}

Therefore, feeding back the previous estimate with (
9.8
), one gets by absorption,

(9.10)

\begin{split}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sum_{i}|\dot{L_{i}}(t)|&\leq C\left|\frac{d}{dt}\sum_{i}\langle h,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right|+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{2}\left|\frac{d}{dt}\langle h,\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right|+C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\left|\frac{d}{dt}\langle h,\tilde{c}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right|\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{2}\|\pi(h(t))\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}.\end{split}

Finally, invoking the first estimate of (
9.8
) together with (
9.10
) leads to the proof of (
9.9
).
∎

9.2.
A stability ODE lemma and analytic estimates on the approximate kernel

We begin this section by stating the following stability lemma whose proof is along the same lines as those of Lemma
8.1
and is therefore omitted:

Lemma 9.7

(Stability ODE lemma)
.

Assume either the setting of
1a
or that of
1b
of Lemma
8.1
. Let
\eta_{i}
,
i=1,2
be in
C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}
. For each
i=1,2
, denote by
\zeta_{i}
the corresponding solution to (
8.4
) with the same initial condition
\zeta_{0}
. Then there exists
C>0
such that for all
t\leq T\leq 0
:

\begin{split}\|\zeta_{2}-\zeta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\Lambda_{0},T}}+\|\dot{\zeta_{2}}-\dot{\zeta_{1}}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\Lambda_{0},T}}&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}.\end{split}

Notice that a similar stability result to Lemma
9.7
holds as well for forward solutions in the setting of Section
8.2
.

The following result gives pointwise estimates for the covariant and time derivatives of the difference of basis of two approximate kernels
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}_{i}(t):=\operatorname{vect}((\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{i}(t))_{j},\tilde{g}_{i}(t),\tilde{c}_{i}(t))
,
t\leq T
, (see Definition
3.12
) defined with respect to two parameters
\zeta_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
i=1,2
close to a fixed background
\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
. Its proof is a combination of Proposition
A.16
and Lemma
9.7
.

Proposition 9.8
.

Under the setting and notations of Theorem
9.1
, let
\tilde{g}_{0}(t)
be defined with respect to
\zeta_{0}(t):=\exp(2\mathbf{R}_{p}^{+}t)\zeta_{0}
for
t\leq T
,
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
m=0,1
,

\begin{split}&|\nabla^{\tilde{g}_{0}(t),\,l}\partial_{t}^{(m)}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t)-\tilde{g}_{1}(t))|_{\tilde{g}_{0}(t)}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}\frac{\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4^{-}}}{\rho(t)^{4^{-}+l}},\\
&|\nabla^{\tilde{g}_{0}(t),\,l}\partial_{t}^{(m)}(\tilde{c}_{2}(t)-\tilde{c}_{1}(t))|_{\tilde{g}_{0}(t)}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}\frac{\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4^{-}}}{\rho(t)^{4^{-}+l}},\\
&|\nabla^{\tilde{g}_{0}(t),\,l}\partial_{t}^{(m)}(\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{2}(t)-\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{1}(t))|_{\tilde{g}_{0}(t)}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}\frac{\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4^{-}}}{\rho(t)^{4^{-}+l}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}},\\
&|\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{2}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{2}(t)-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{1}(t))|_{\tilde{g}_{0}(t)}\leq\\
&C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4^{-}}}{\rho(t)^{4^{-}}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}+\frac{\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4^{-}}}{\delta(t)^{6^{-}}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\delta(t)/2\,\leq\,r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}}\right).\end{split}

9.3.
Proof of Theorem
9.1

We are now in a position to prove Theorem
9.1
through a series of claims.

Proof of Theorem
9.1
.

Let
(k_{i},\eta_{i},\beta_{i},\tau_{i})\in\mathcal{B}
and let
(h_{i},\xi_{i},\nu_{i},\mu_{i}):=\Phi(k_{i},\eta_{i},\beta_{i},\tau_{i})
for
i=1,2
. Observe that there is a conflict of notations with the map
\zeta\rightarrow h_{2,\zeta}
from Proposition
2.13
. However, since we do not use formally this map in the proof below, there is no source of confusion.

With the same notations as those in the proof of Proposition
8.10
, observe that
h_{2}-h_{1}
satisfies:

\begin{split}&\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}-2\Lambda\right)(h_{2}-h_{1})(t)=\\
&\Psi(t)+\sum_{j}\left(\lambda^{2}_{j}(t)-\lambda^{1}_{j}(t)\right)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{1}(t)+(\nu_{2}(t)-\nu_{1}(t))\tilde{g}_{1}(t)+(\mu_{2}(t)-\mu_{1}(t))\tilde{c}_{1}(t),\\
\Psi(t)&:=\psi_{2}(t)-\psi_{1}(t)+\left[\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{2}(t)}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\right]h_{2}(t)\\
&\quad+\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}^{2}(t)(\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{2}(t)-\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{1}(t))+\nu_{2}(t)(\tilde{g}_{2}(t)-\tilde{g}_{1}(t))+\mu_{2}(t)(\tilde{c}_{2}(t)-\tilde{c}_{1}(t)).\end{split}

If
\Psi
lied in
C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
then Proposition
7.20
would ensure that the norm of
h_{2}-h_{1}
is well-controlled in the
C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
norm
on the condition
that
h_{2}
is orthogonal to the approximate kernel defined with respect to
\tilde{g}_{1}(t)
. To remedy this issue, denote the
L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))
-orthogonal projection of
h_{2}
on the approximate kernel defined with respect to
\tilde{g}_{1}(t)
by
\pi_{1}(h_{2})
so that:

(9.11)

\begin{split}&\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}-2\Lambda\right)(h_{2}-\pi_{1}(h_{2})-h_{1})(t)=\\
&\psi(t)+\sum_{j}\left(\lambda^{2}_{j}(t)-\lambda^{1}_{j}(t)\right)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{1}(t)+(\nu_{2}(t)-\nu_{1}(t))\tilde{g}_{1}(t)+(\mu_{2}(t)-\mu_{1}(t))\tilde{c}_{1}(t),\\
\psi(t)&:=\Psi(t)-\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}-2\Lambda\right)\pi_{1}(h_{2}).\end{split}

Claim 3
.

The projection
\pi_{1}(h_{2})
lies in
C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
and,

\begin{split}\|\pi_{1}(h_{2})\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}},\\
\left\|\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}-2\Lambda\right)\pi_{1}(h_{2})\right\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}.\end{split}

In particular, if
K\in(0,1)
, there exist
T\leq 0
such that,

\left\|\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}-2\Lambda\right)\pi_{1}(h_{2})\right\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq K\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}.

Proof of Claim
3
.

Let us first start by estimating the scalar products of
h_{2}
against the approximate kernel defined with respect to
\tilde{g}_{1}(t)
by using that
h_{2}
is orthogonal to the approximate kernel defined with respect to
\tilde{g}_{2}(t)
.

(9.12)

\begin{split}\left|\langle h_{2}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{j}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\right|&\leq\left|\langle h_{2}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{j}(t)-\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{2}_{j}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\right|\\
&\quad+\left|\langle h_{2}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{2}_{j}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}-\langle h_{2}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{2}_{j}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4-\sigma},\end{split}

where we have used that
h_{2}
lies in
\mathcal{B}
thanks to Proposition
8.10
and where we have used Lemma
C.3
. Here, the terms
\tilde{g}_{2}(t)-\tilde{g}_{1}(t)
and
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{2}(t)-\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{1}(t)
have been estimated with the help of Proposition
9.8
. Similarly, invoking Proposition
9.8
leads to:

(9.13)

\left|\langle h_{2}(t),\tilde{g}_{1}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\right|+\left|\langle h_{2}(t),\tilde{c}_{1}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\right|\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4-\sigma}.

Thanks to estimates [(
9.4
), Lemma
9.4
], (
9.12
) and (
9.13
):

\|\pi_{1}(h_{2})\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}},

since
\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1>0
by choice of
\eta_{0}
in (

H
1

).
Moreover,

(9.14)

\|\pi_{1}(h_{2})\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}},

since again
2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)>0
by (

H
1

).

Since the coefficients are space-independent, the corresponding estimates will hold for covariant derivatives of
\pi_{1}(h_{2})
. Indeed, if
\pi_{1}(h_{2})=:L_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{1}(t)+M_{1}(t)\tilde{g}_{1}(t)+N_{1}(t)\tilde{c}_{1}(t)
, Lemma
3.10
, Lemma
3.11
and [(
9.6
), Lemma
9.4
] together with (
9.13
) give for
k=0,1,2
:

\begin{split}&\gamma(t)(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{k+\sigma}\left|\nabla^{\tilde{g}_{1}(t),\,k}\pi_{1}(h_{2})\right|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\\
&=\gamma(t)(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{k+\sigma}\left|\nabla^{\tilde{g}_{1}(t),\,k}\left(L_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)+N_{1}(t)\tilde{c}_{1}(t)\right)\right|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\left(1+\varepsilon(t)^{4-\sigma}\delta(t)^{-4+\sigma}\right)\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}.\end{split}

A similar reasoning leads to

(9.15)

\|\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{1}}\pi_{1}(h_{2})\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}.

We are therefore left with estimating time-derivatives of
\pi_{1}(h_{2})
. Let us take care of the term
L_{i}(t)\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{1}(t)+M_{1}(t)\partial_{t}\tilde{g}_{1}(t)+N_{1}(t)\partial_{t}\tilde{c}_{1}(t)
first. Invoking Lemma
3.8
, Lemma
3.10
and Lemma
3.11
together with (
9.12
) and (
9.13
) give:

(9.16)

\begin{split}\gamma(t)(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{2+\sigma}&\left|L_{i}(t)\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{1}(t)+M_{1}(t)\partial_{t}\tilde{g}_{1}(t)+N_{1}(t)\partial_{t}\tilde{c}_{1}(t)\right|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}},\quad t\leq T.\end{split}

Here in the second line, we have only kept the minimum exponent of
\varepsilon_{0}
.
Let us estimate
\dot{L_{i}}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{1}(t)+\dot{M_{1}}(t)\tilde{g}_{1}(t)+\dot{N_{1}}(t)\tilde{c}_{1}(t)
through the Gram matrix
G(t)
introduced in (
9.1
):

(9.17)

\begin{pmatrix}\dot{L_{1}}(t)\\
\dot{L_{2}}(t)\\
\vdots\\
\dot{M_{1}}(t)\\
\dot{N_{1}}(t)\end{pmatrix}=G(t)^{-1}\frac{d}{dt}\begin{pmatrix}\langle h_{2}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{1}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\\
\langle h_{2}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{2}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\\
\vdots\\
\langle h_{2}(t),\tilde{g}_{1}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\\
\langle h_{2}(t),\tilde{c}_{1}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\end{pmatrix}-G(t)^{-1}G^{\prime}(t)\begin{pmatrix}L_{1}(t)\\
L_{2}(t)\\
\vdots\\
M_{1}(t)\\
N_{1}(t)\end{pmatrix},\quad t\leq T.

Observe first that thanks to [(
9.3
), Lemma
9.4
] and [(
9.7
), Lemma
9.5
], the coefficients of the matrix
G(t)^{-1}G^{\prime}(t)
are at most uniformly bounded since
\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{j}(t))}
are in
\varepsilon(t)^{4}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
. Therefore, the second term of the righthand side of (
9.17
) can be estimated pointwise in time as follows:

(9.18)

\begin{split}\left|G(t)^{-1}G^{\prime}(t)\begin{pmatrix}L_{1}(t)\\
L_{2}(t)\\
\vdots\\
M_{1}(t)\\
N_{1}(t)\end{pmatrix}\right|&\leq C\sum_{j}|L_{j}(t)|+|N_{1}(t)|+|M_{1}(t)|\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-\sigma}.\end{split}

Here we have used (
9.12
) and (
9.13
) in the second inequality.

In order to estimate the time derivatives of the scalar products
\langle h_{2}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}
, we proceed as in (
9.12
). Schematically, by Lemma
C.4
,

\begin{split}&\left|\frac{d}{dt}\langle h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1})}\right|=\left|\frac{d}{dt}\left(\langle h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1})}-\langle h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{2}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2})}\right)\right|\leq\\
&C\int_{M}|h_{2}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{2}_{i}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\left(|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}_{1}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}+|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}_{2}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\right)|\tilde{g}_{2}(t)-\tilde{g}_{1}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\\
&+C\int_{M}|h_{2}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{2}_{i}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}|\partial_{t}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t)-\tilde{g}_{1}(t))|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\\
&+C\int_{M}|h_{2}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}|\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{2}_{i}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}|\tilde{g}_{2}(t)-\tilde{g}_{1}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\\
&+C\int_{M}|\partial_{t}h_{2}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\left(|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{2}_{i}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}|\tilde{g}_{2}(t)-\tilde{g}_{1}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}+|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{2}_{i}(t)-\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\right)\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\\
&+C\int_{M}|h_{2}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\left(|\partial_{t}(\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{2}_{i}(t)-\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t))|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}+|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}_{1}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{2}_{i}(t)-\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\right)\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}.\end{split}

The “worse” term above is the term in the fourth integral on the righthand side involving the time derivative
\partial_{t}h_{2}
.

Using that
h_{2}
lies in
\mathcal{B}
together with estimates from Lemma
3.8
and Lemma
3.10
combined with Proposition
9.8
:

(9.19)

\begin{split}&\left|\frac{d}{dt}\langle h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1})}\right|\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2-\sigma}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}.\end{split}

Similarly, by invoking Lemma
3.8
and Lemma
3.11
successively together with Proposition
9.8
,

(9.20)

\begin{split}\left|\frac{d}{dt}\langle h_{2},\tilde{g}_{1}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1})}\right|&+\left|\frac{d}{dt}\langle h_{2},\tilde{c}_{1}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1})}\right|\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{-6^{-}-\sigma}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}.\end{split}

The difference of estimate (
9.20
) with (
9.19
) lies in the fact that the (difference of) tensors are bot supported on the bubble region
\{r_{o}\,\leq\,2\delta(t)\}
anymore.

Then the expression of
G(t)^{-1}
given in [(
9.3
), Lemma
9.4
], in combination with (
9.18
) and (
9.19
) and (
9.20
) lead to:

(9.21)

\begin{split}\gamma(t)&(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{2+\sigma}\left|\dot{L_{i}}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{1}(t)+\dot{M_{1}}(t)\tilde{g}_{1}(t)+\dot{N_{1}}(t)\tilde{c}_{1}(t)\right|_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\\
&\leq C\left(\left(\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{\sigma-2}+1\right)\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2-\sigma}+\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{-6^{-}-\sigma}\right)\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}},\end{split}

thereby proving the desired intermediate estimate, i.e. if
K\in(0,1)
there exists
T_{0}\leq 0
such that for
T\leq T_{0}
,
\|\pi_{1}(h_{2})\|_{C^{2}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\leq K\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}
.

A similar reasoning leads to the existence of
T_{0}\leq 0
for a given
K\in(0,1)
such that for
T\leq T_{0}
,
\|\partial_{t}\pi_{1}(h_{2})\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq K\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}
thanks to the choice of parameters made in (

H
1

).

Moreover, the combination of (
9.14
), (
9.15
), (
9.16
) and (
9.21
) gives the quantitative decay on
\left\|\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}-2\Lambda\right)\pi_{1}(h_{2})\right\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}
by inspecting the choice of parameters (

H
1

). Corresponding Hölder estimates hold and are left to the reader.
∎

Claim 4
.

The data
\psi
lies in
C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
and

\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)-\iota\sigma}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}.

In particular, if
K\in(0,1)
, there exist
T\leq 0
such that,

\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq K\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}.

Proof of Claim
4
.

According to Claim
3
and the definition (
9.11
) of
\psi
, it suffices to estimate
\Psi
in the
C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
norm. We start with estimating the difference
\psi_{2}-\psi_{1}
appearing in the expression of
\Psi
. To do so, observe that:

\begin{split}\|\beta_{2}\cdot k_{2}&-\beta_{1}\cdot k_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\leq\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\left(\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{\beta_{0}}\right)\left(\|\beta_{2}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}}\|k_{2}-k_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+\|k_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\|\beta_{2}-\beta_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}}\right)\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{\beta_{0}}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}.\end{split}

Here we have used Lemma
6.6
and that
(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})
lies in
\mathcal{B}
.

Similarly,

\begin{split}\|\tau_{2}\cdot\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}_{2}}\tilde{v}_{2}}k_{2}-\tau_{1}\cdot\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}_{1}}\tilde{v}_{1}}k_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq\|(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})\cdot\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}_{2}}\tilde{v}_{2}}k_{2}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\quad+\|\tau_{1}\cdot\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}_{2}}\tilde{v}_{2}-\nabla^{\tilde{g}_{1}}\tilde{v}_{1}}k_{2}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\quad+\|\tau_{1}\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{\nabla^{\tilde{g}_{1}}\tilde{v}_{1}}(k_{2}-k_{1})\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{\tau_{0}}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}.\end{split}

Now, the difference
Q_{\tilde{g}_{2}(t)}(k_{2}(t))-Q_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}(k_{1}(t))
can be handled schematically as:

\begin{split}\|Q_{\tilde{g}_{2}(t)}(k_{2}(t))&-Q_{\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}(k_{1}(t))\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\leq C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\left(\gamma(t)\gamma(t)^{-2}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-\sigma}\right)\left(\|k_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+\|k_{2}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\right)\|k_{2}-k_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\left(\gamma(t)\gamma(t)^{-2}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-\sigma}\right)\left(\|k_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+\|k_{2}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\right)^{2}\|\tilde{g}_{2}-\tilde{g}_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{1,0,T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2+\iota\sigma}\|k_{2}-k_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2+\iota\sigma+2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2+\iota\sigma}\left(\|k_{2}-k_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}+\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}\right).\end{split}

Here we have used Lemma
C.2
in the first inequality together with Proposition
9.8
and (

H
1

) in the last line.

In the same vein, the difference
\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{2}(t)}h_{1}(t)-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}h_{1}(t)
can be estimated as:

\begin{split}\|\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{2}}h_{2}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{1}}h_{2}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\|h_{2}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\|\tilde{g}_{2}-\tilde{g}_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{1,0,T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\eta_{0},T}}.\end{split}

Here again we have used that
2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)>0
thanks to (

H
1

) together with Proposition
9.8
.

It remains to estimate the difference of the two approximate kernels weighted by the coefficients
(\lambda_{j}^{2})_{j}
,
\nu_{2}
and
\mu_{2}
. Thanks to [(
2
), Corollary
8.8
]
and Proposition
9.8
,

\begin{split}\left\|\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}^{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{2}-\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{1})\right\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\left(\gamma(t)\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2+\sigma+2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\right)\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{4\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)-\iota\sigma}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}},\end{split}

since
2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)>\iota\sigma
by (

H
1

).

Along the same lines, based on [(
4
), Corollary
8.8
] together with Proposition
9.8
,

\begin{split}\left\|\nu_{2}\cdot(\tilde{g}_{2}-\tilde{g}_{1})\right\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\left(\gamma(t)\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{8-10\delta+2+\sigma}\right)\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{8-10\delta-\iota\sigma+2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}.\end{split}

A similar estimate holds for the term
\mu_{2}(t)(\tilde{c}_{2}(t)-\tilde{c}_{1}(t))
.
Since
\iota\sigma<8-10\delta
by (

H
1

), the result follows.

Finally, invoking Proposition
A.16
together with the proofs of Lemmata
3.8
and
3.16
lead to:

\Big\|\partial_{t}(\tilde{g}_{2}-\tilde{g}_{1})+2(\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}_{2})-\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(\tilde{g}_{1}))-2\Lambda(\tilde{g}_{2}-\tilde{g}_{1})\Big\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)-\iota\sigma}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}.

Corresponding Hölder estimates hold and are left to the reader.
∎

The combination of Claims
3
and
4
together with Proposition
7.20
give us
T\leq 0
such that:

(9.22)

\begin{split}\|h_{2}-h_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}&\leq C\|\psi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\|\pi_{1}(h_{2})\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)-\iota\sigma}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}},\end{split}

thanks to the triangular inequality used in the first inequality.

We are left with proving that the remaining components
\xi
,
\nu
and
\mu
are
K
-Lipschitz for any
K\in(0,1)
if
T\leq 0
is small enough. Thanks to Corollary
7.11
, the choice of parameters made in (

H
1

) and estimates (
9.22
) and Claims
3
and
4
again, we have:

(9.23)

\begin{split}\|\nu_{2}-\nu_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{6+\sigma-(4+\sigma)\delta+\iota\sigma-\frac{2\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\|h_{2}-\pi_{1}(h_{2})-h_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2+(1+\iota)\sigma-\frac{2\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{(4+\sigma)(1-\delta)}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2+(1+\iota)\sigma-\frac{2\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{\sigma}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}},\\
\|\mu_{2}-\mu_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{6+\sigma-(4+\sigma)\delta+\iota\sigma-\frac{2\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\|h_{2}-\pi_{1}(h_{2})-h_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2+(1+\iota)\sigma-\frac{2\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{\sigma}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}.\end{split}

Recall that we expect that
\nu_{i}
,
\mu_{i}
,
i=1,2
belongs to
C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta_{0},T}
and
C^{0,\alpha}_{\tau_{0},T}
respectively. The previous estimate (
9.23
) shows in particular that the maps
(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)\in\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\nu\in C^{0}_{\beta_{0},T}
and
(k,\eta,\beta,\tau)\in\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\nu\in C^{0}_{\tau_{0},T}
are
K
-contracting maps provided
T\leq 0
is small enough:

(9.24)

\begin{split}\|\nu_{2}-\nu_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\beta_{0},T}}&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)+2\left(1-\frac{\beta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}\right)+\sigma}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)+\sigma}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}},\\
\|\mu_{2}-\mu_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\tau_{0},T}}&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)+2\left(1-\frac{\tau_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}\right)+\sigma}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)+\sigma}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}.\end{split}

Finally, for the difference
\xi_{2}(t)-\xi_{1}(t)
, recall that by definition of each
\xi_{i}
given in (
8.10
),

\begin{split}&d_{\zeta_{1}(t)}g(\xi_{2}(t)-\xi_{1}(t))=\\
&d_{\zeta_{1}(t)}g(\eta_{2}(t)-\eta_{1}(t))-\sum_{j}\left(\lambda_{j}^{2}(t)-\lambda_{j}^{1}(t)\right)\frac{\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{1}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}}{\|\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta_{1}(t))\|_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta_{1}(t)})}^{2}}\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta_{1}(t))\\
&-\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}^{2}(t)\left(\frac{\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{2}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}}{\|\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta_{2}(t))\|_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta_{2}(t)})}^{2}}\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta_{2}(t))-\frac{\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{1}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}}{\|\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta_{1}(t))\|_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta_{1}(t)})}^{2}}\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta_{1}(t))\right)\\
&+\left(d_{\zeta_{1}(t)}g-d_{\zeta_{2}(t)}g\right)(\xi_{2}(t)-\eta_{2}(t))\\
&=:d_{\zeta_{1}(t)}g(\eta_{2}(t)-\eta_{1}(t))-\sum_{j}\left(\lambda_{j}^{2}(t)-\lambda_{j}^{1}(t)\right)\frac{\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{1}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}}{\|\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta_{1}(t))\|_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta_{1}(t)})}^{2}}\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta_{1}(t))+\mathcal{E}(t),\end{split}

where the term
\mathcal{E}(t)
has to be interpreted as an error term. These heuristics are shown to be true in the following claim:

Claim 5
.

There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T\leq 0
,

\sum_{j}\left|\left\langle\mathcal{E}(t),\frac{\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta_{1}(t))}{\|\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta_{1}(t))\|_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta_{1}(t)})}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta_{1}(t)})}\right|\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{\frac{2\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}.

Proof of Claim
5
.

Let us start with the last term defining
\mathcal{E}(t)
: for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}&\left|\left\langle\left(d_{\zeta_{1}(t)}g-d_{\zeta_{2}(t)}g\right)(\xi_{2}(t)-\eta_{2}(t)),\frac{\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta_{1}(t))}{\|\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta_{1}(t))\|_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta_{1}(t)})}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta_{1}(t)})}\right|\\
&\leq C\frac{|\zeta_{2}(t)-\zeta_{1}(t)|}{\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2}}(|\xi_{2}(t)|+|\eta_{2}(t)|)\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{\frac{2\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}.\end{split}

Here we have been using Proposition
A.16
to estimate
d_{\zeta_{1}(t)}g-d_{\zeta_{2}(t)}g
, the term
|\zeta_{2}(t)-\zeta_{1}(t)|
has been estimated with the help of Lemma
9.7
and Corollary
8.8
is invoked to estimate
|\xi_{2}(t)|
.

Finally, the first term defining
\mathcal{E}(t)
can be handled as follows:

\begin{split}&\left|\left\langle\mathcal{E}(t)-\left(d_{\zeta_{1}(t)}g-d_{\zeta_{2}(t)}g\right)(\xi_{2}(t)-\eta_{2}(t)),\frac{\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta_{1}(t))}{\|\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta_{1}(t))\|_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta_{1}(t)})}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta_{1}(t)})}\right|\\
&\leq C\sum_{j}|\lambda_{j}^{2}(t)|\frac{|\zeta_{2}(t)-\zeta_{1}(t)|}{\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2}}\|\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta_{1}(t))\|_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta_{1}(t)})}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{\frac{2\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}},\end{split}

where we have used Corollary
8.8
to estimate the coefficients
\lambda_{j}^{2}(t)
together with the fact that
\|\mathbf{o}_{j}(\zeta_{1}(t))\|_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta_{1}(t)})}=O(\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2})
.
∎

Now, invoking (
7.3
) from Proposition
7.4
,

\begin{split}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}&\left|\langle\psi(t)+(\lambda_{i}^{2}(t)-\lambda_{i}^{1}(t))\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2+2(1-\delta)+(1-\delta+\iota)\sigma}\|h_{2}-\pi_{1}(h_{2})-h_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2+2\delta}|\nu_{2}(t)-\nu_{1}(t)|+C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2}|\mu_{2}(t)-\mu_{1}(t)|+C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4+\iota\sigma}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2-\iota\sigma}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2+2(1-\delta)+(1-\delta+\iota)\sigma}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{\sigma}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}+2}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-2-\iota\sigma}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4+\iota\sigma}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}.\end{split}

Here we have used (
9.22
) in the anti-penultimate line, (
9.24
) in the penultimate line and Claim
4
in the last line. By carefully inspecting each power with the help of the choice of parameters (

H
1

), observe that
2(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1)<2<2+2(1-\delta)+(1-\delta+\iota)\sigma
implies:

(9.25)

\begin{split}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}&\left|\langle\psi(t)+(\lambda_{i}^{2}(t)-\lambda_{i}^{1}(t))\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}.\end{split}

Here we have also used that
2+\sigma>2(\eta_{0}/\Lambda_{0}-1)
and
4+\iota\sigma>2\eta_{0}/\Lambda_{0}
from (

H
1

).

In particular, we get the following intermediate conclusion thanks to Claim
5
and (
9.25
):

(9.26)

\begin{split}&\|\xi_{2}-\xi_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)-\iota\sigma}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}\\
&+\left\|\varepsilon_{0}^{-2}\sum_{i}\left|\left\langle d_{\zeta_{1}}g(\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}),\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta_{1})\right\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta_{1}})}+\langle\psi,\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1})}\right|\right\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}.\end{split}

Estimating the last term on the righthand side of (

) is similar to the proof of
3
from Corollary
8.8
modulo the additional terms in the definition of
\psi
given in (
9.11
). This is the purpose of the following claims.

Claim 6
.

There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T\leq 0
,

(9.27)

\begin{split}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}\sum_{i}&\left|\left\langle\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}^{2}(t)(\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{2}(t)-\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{1}(t))+\nu_{2}(t)(\tilde{g}_{2}(t)-\tilde{g}_{1}(t))+\mu_{2}(t)(\tilde{c}_{2}(t)-\tilde{c}_{1}(t)),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{\frac{2\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}.\end{split}

Proof of Claim
6
.

Let us estimate term by term as follows:

\begin{split}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}\sum_{j}\left|\langle\lambda_{j}^{2}(t)(\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{2}(t)-\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{1}(t)),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\right|\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{\frac{2\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\left\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\right\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}},\\
\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}\left|\langle\nu_{2}(t)(\tilde{g}_{2}(t)-\tilde{g}_{1}(t)),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\right|\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{10-10\delta}\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\left\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\right\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}},\\
\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}\left|\langle\mu_{2}(t)(\tilde{c}_{2}(t)-\tilde{c}_{1}(t)),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\right|\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{10-10\delta}\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\left\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\right\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}.\end{split}

In the first line, we have used (
2
) from Corollary
8.8
together with Proposition
9.8
. In the second and third lines, [(
4
), Corollary
8.8
] and [(
5
), Corollary
8.8
] together with Proposition
9.8
have been used.
Since
2(\eta_{0}/\Lambda_{0}-1)<10(1-\delta)-2
by (

H
1

), the expected estimate (
9.27
) follows for the above terms.
∎

Up to the term
\psi_{2}(t)-\psi_{1}(t)
in the definition of
\psi(t)
given in (
9.11
), we are left with estimating:

\begin{split}\left\langle\left[\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{2}(t)}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\right]h_{2}(t)-\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}-2\Lambda\right)\pi_{1}(h_{2}),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}.\end{split}

It turns out that estimating each term as we did in the proof of Claim
3
and
4
is not enough to give the desired control: the main issue are the terms decaying like
(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o})^{-2-\sigma}
. Now, an integration by parts shows that

\begin{split}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}&\left|\left\langle\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\pi_{1}(h_{2}),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\right|=\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}\left|\left\langle\pi_{1}(h_{2}),\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\left(\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2-\sigma}+\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2-(2+\sigma)\delta}\right)\varepsilon(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4-2\delta+(1-\delta+\iota)\sigma}\varepsilon(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\varepsilon(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}},\end{split}

thanks to Claim
3
and Lemma
3.10
and the fact that
\sigma<(2+\sigma)\delta
together with
(2+\sigma)(1-\delta)>(5-\sigma)(1-\delta)-2>2(\eta_{0}\Lambda_{0}-1)
as imposed by (

H
1

).
So that we only need to estimate

\begin{split}\left\langle\left[\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{2}(t)}+2\Lambda-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{1}(t)}\right]h_{2}(t)-\partial_{t}(\pi_{1}(h_{2})-\pi_{2}(h_{2})),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))},\end{split}

since
\pi_{2}(h_{2})=0
.

Claim 7
.

There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T\leq 0
,

(9.28)

\begin{split}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}&\left|\left\langle(\pi_{1}-\pi_{2})(\partial_{t}h_{2})-\partial_{t}((\pi_{1}-\pi_{2})(h_{2})),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}.\end{split}

Proof of Claim
7
.

Define for
j=1,2
,
\pi_{j}(h_{2})(t)=:\sum_{i}L_{i}^{j}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{j}(t)+M_{j}(t)\tilde{g}_{j}(t)+N_{j}(t)\tilde{c}_{j}(t).

Then,

\begin{split}&\left|\left\langle\partial_{t}((\pi_{1}-\pi_{2})(h_{2}))-\sum_{j}\dot{L_{j}^{1}}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{1}(t)-\dot{M_{1}}(t)\tilde{g}_{1}(t)-\dot{N_{1}}(t)\tilde{c}_{1}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq\sum_{j}|L_{j}^{1}(t)||\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{1}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}|\\
&\quad+|M_{1}(t)||\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{g}_{1}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}|+|N_{1}(t)||\langle\partial_{t}\tilde{c}_{1}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}|\\
&\leq\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4}\left(\sum_{j}|L_{j}^{1}(t)|+|M_{1}(t)|+|N_{1}(t)|\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{i}|\langle h_{2}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}|+\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{4}\left(|\langle h_{2}(t),\tilde{g}_{1}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}|+|\langle h_{2}(t),\tilde{c}_{1}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}|\right).\end{split}

Here we have been using Proposition
4.2
in the second inequality and [(
9.4
), Lemma
9.4
] in the last inequality.
Since
\pi_{2}(h_{2})=0
, each scalar product of
h_{2}
and an element of the approximate kernel with respect to
\tilde{g}_{2}(t)
is
0
. By linearizing each term of the righthand side of the previous estimate leads to the following intermediate result:

(9.29)

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}\left|\left\langle\partial_{t}((\pi_{1}-\pi_{2})(h_{2}))-\sum_{j}\dot{L_{j}^{1}}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{1}(t)-\dot{M_{1}}(t)\tilde{g}_{1}(t)-\dot{N_{1}}(t)\tilde{c}_{1}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\left(\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma}+\varepsilon(t)^{6^{-}}\delta(t)^{-4^{-}-\sigma}\right)\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\varepsilon(t)^{4+\iota\sigma}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}.\end{split}

We have used (

H
1

) in the last two lines here.

Now, invoking [(
9.4
), Lemma
9.4
] again, the difference
\left\langle\pi_{2}(\partial_{t}h_{2}),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{1}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}-\left\langle\partial_{t}h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{2}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}
is dominated by a sum of the following terms:

\begin{split}&\left|\langle\partial_{t}h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{2}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}\right|\left|\frac{\left\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{2}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{1}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{2}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}}-1\right|,\\
&\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2}\sum_{j\neq i}\left|\langle\partial_{t}h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{2}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}\right|\left|\frac{\left\langle\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{2}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{1}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{2}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}}-1\right|,\\
&\left|\langle\partial_{t}h_{2},\tilde{g}_{2}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}\right|\left|\left\langle\tilde{g}_{2}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{1}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}-\left\langle\tilde{g}_{2}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{2}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}\right|,\\
&\left|\langle\partial_{t}h_{2},\tilde{c}_{2}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}\right|\left|\left\langle\tilde{c}_{2}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{1}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}-\left\langle\tilde{c}_{2}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{2}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}\right|.\end{split}

Each of these terms once multiplied by
\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}
can be estimated from above by

C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}},

at least thanks to Proposition
9.8
, Lemma
C.3
. Notice that this bound is not sharp by inspecting the decays of each of these terms but those turn out to be sufficient. Here we have used that
\pi_{2}(h_{2})=0
and Lemma
7.3
,
7.6
and
7.9
.
A second intermediate estimate is then:

(9.30)

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}\left|\left\langle(\pi_{2}-\pi_{1})(\partial_{t}h_{2}),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{1}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}-\left\langle\partial_{t}h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{2}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}+\left\langle\partial_{t}h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{1}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}.\end{split}

Similarly, invoking Lemma
C.4
, Proposition
9.8
and Lemma
C.3
,

(9.31)

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}\left|\frac{d}{dt}\left(\left\langle h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{1}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}-\left\langle h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{2}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}\right)-\left\langle\partial_{t}h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{2}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}+\left\langle\partial_{t}h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{1}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\right|\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\|\eta_{2}-\eta_{1}\|_{C^{0}_{\eta_{0},T}}.\end{split}

Finally, [(
9.9
), Lemma
9.8
] applied to
h_{2}
and the approximate kernel defined with respect to
\tilde{g}_{1}(t)
gives:

\begin{split}\Bigg|\frac{d}{dt}&\bigg(\left\langle h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{1}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}-\left\langle h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{2}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}\Bigg)-\\
&\left\langle\sum_{j}\dot{L_{j}^{1}}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{1}(t)+\dot{M_{1}}(t)\tilde{g}_{1}(t)+\dot{N_{1}}(t)\tilde{c}_{1}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\bigg|\\
&\leq C\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4}\left|\frac{d}{dt}\sum_{i}\langle h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right|+C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-2}\left|\frac{d}{dt}\langle h_{2},\tilde{g}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right|\\
&\quad+C\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4}\left|\frac{d}{dt}\langle h_{2},\tilde{c}\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right|+C\varepsilon(t)^{2}\|\pi_{1}(h_{2}(t))\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}.\end{split}

We now invoke (
9.12
), (
9.13
) and (
9.19
), (
9.20
) from the proof of Claim

to infer that the lefthand side of the previous estimate is bounded from above by the expected quantity. More precisely, one has

(9.32)

\begin{split}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}\Bigg|\frac{d}{dt}&\bigg(\left\langle h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{1}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}-\left\langle h_{2},\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{2}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}\bigg)-\\
&\left\langle\sum_{j}\dot{L_{j}^{1}}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{j}^{1}(t)+\dot{M_{1}}(t)\tilde{g}_{1}(t)+\dot{N_{1}}(t)\tilde{c}_{1}(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}^{1}_{i}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{1}(t))}\bigg|\\
&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}.\end{split}

Here we have invoked that
2(\eta_{0}/\Lambda_{0}-1)<2+\iota\sigma
and
2(\eta_{0}/\Lambda_{0}-1)<4(1-\delta)+\iota\sigma
thanks to (

H
1

).
The claim is proved by invoking the triangular inequality and estimates (

), (
9.30
), (

) and (

).
∎

&\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(T)^{2\left(\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}-1\right)}\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{2\frac{\eta_{0}}{\Lambda_{0}}}\left\|(k_{2},\eta_{2},\beta_{2},\tau_{2})-(k_{1},\eta_{1},\beta_{1},\tau_{1})\right\|_{\mathcal{X}}.\end{split}

On the other hand, an integration by parts and Lemma

shows:

(9.39)

\begin{split}\left|\left\langle\pi_{2}(\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}_{2}(t)}h_{2}(t)),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}^{2}(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}_{2}(t))}\right|&\leq C\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-2-\sigma},\\

&\quad+O(\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{5}\delta(t)^{-3-\sigma})+O(\gamma(t)^{-1}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4-\sigma})\\

Here we have used Lemma

by construction.

∎

10.
Topology and curvature estimates

10.1.
Topology of some examples

In this section, we prove the topological aspect of Theorem
1.3
.
As stated in Theorem
1.3
, we restrict ourselves to the homeotype of our ancient flows, we believe that they will also coincide with their diffeotype.

Proposition 10.1
.

The ancient flows provided by Theorem
1.3
with tangent soliton a spherical suspension over
\mathbb{S}^{3}/\Gamma
where
\Gamma
is a finite subgroup of
\operatorname{SU}(2)
acting freely, are oriented homeomorphic to:

(1)

(Case
\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
)
\mathbb{CP}^{2}\#\mathbb{CP}^{2}
,
\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}\#\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}
, or
\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
depending on the orientations in which the gluings are performed, and

(2)

(Case
\Gamma\subset\{A_{k},D_{k},E_{k}\}
,
k>2
)

\#k(\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})
.

Remark 10.2
.

In particular, the flows constructed in Theorem
1.3
show that the following are possible:

•

finding two homeomorphic ancient Ricci flows, one with smooth tangent Ricci soliton
\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
, and one with the singular suspension over
\mathbb{RP}^{3}
,

•

finding two smooth ancient Ricci flows with the same singular tangent Ricci soliton, the singular suspension over
\mathbb{RP}^{3}
, which are not homeomorphic, and

•

families of homeomorphic smooth ancient Ricci flows with different singular tangent Ricci soliton.

Proof.

When we start with the suspension over
\mathbb{RP}^{3}
, which is a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
quotient of
\mathbb{S}^{4}
, there are three possible (oriented) topologies for desingularizations by Eguchi-Hanson metrics. These depend on the orientation in which the gluing of each of the two Eguchi-Hanson metrics is performed: we obtain different oriented and simply connected
4
-manifolds,

(1)

gluing both in their anti-selfdual (or hyperkähler) orientation, leading to a
4
-manifold
M_{--}
, which we will see is homeomorphic to
\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}\#\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}
,

(2)

gluing both in their selfdual orientation, leading to a
4
-manifold
M_{++}
, which we will see is homeomorphic to
{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}\#{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}
, or

(3)

gluing one in each orientation, leading to a
4
-manifold
M_{+-}
, which we will see is homeomorphic to
\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

In order to reach these conclusions, we appeal to Freedman’s classification of
4
-manifolds up to homeomorphism by their intersection form. First, note that
b_{+}(M_{--})=0
and
b_{-}(M_{--})=2
,
b_{+}(M_{++})=2
and
b_{-}(M_{++})=0
, and
b_{+}(M_{+-})=1
and
b_{-}(M_{+-})=1
, which already confirms the homeotype of
M_{--}
and
M_{++}
. As for
M_{+-}
, this leaves two options:
\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
or
\mathbb{CP}^{2}\#\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}
. The homeomorphism of
M_{+-}\approx\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
can be obtained by different techniques, but we will treat the general case of subgroups of
\operatorname{SU(2)}
.

In the case of other quotients
\mathbb{S}^{3}/\Gamma
for
\Gamma\subset\operatorname{SU(2)}
, the manifolds we are considering are “doublings” as follows. Denoting
X_{\Gamma}
the manifold with boundary
\mathbb{S}^{3}/\Gamma
obtained from cutting the end
(\mathbb{R}^{4}/\penalty\Gamma)\backslash\overline{B_{\mathbf{e}}(0,\varepsilon_{0}^{-1})}
in the ALE coordinates of a hyperkähler ALE manifold, we define
M_{\Gamma}
as the gluing of
X_{\Gamma}
to
-X_{\Gamma}
along their common boundary
\mathbb{S}^{3}/\Gamma
. In the case of
\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, the obtained
4
-manifold is the above
M_{+-}
.

The manifolds with boundary
X_{\Gamma}
and
-X_{\Gamma}
are spin manifolds whose induced spin structures on the boundary
\mathbb{S}^{3}/\Gamma
coincide by construction. This is exactly the condition that ensures that the manifold
M_{\Gamma}
admits a spin structure. Being spin and having zero signature, and
b_{+}=b_{-}=k_{\Gamma}
for
k_{A_{k}}=k_{D_{k}}=k_{E_{k}}=k
, we conclude that
M_{\Gamma}
is homeomorphic to
\#k_{\Gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})
.

Through Mayer-Vietoris sequence, the intersection form of these manifolds can also be computed and compared to those of
\#k(\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})
. As an illustration, an interesting case is that of the group
E_{8}
. Indeed, the boundary
\mathbb{S}^{3}/E_{8}
is the Poincaré homology sphere, hence the intersection form of
M_{E_{8}}
is simply the direct sum of the intersection forms of
X_{E_{8}}
and
-X_{E_{8}}
which is
E_{8}\#(-E_{8})\approx\#8(\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})
.

∎

Remark 10.3
.

The examples obtained from desingularizing the “water drop” orbifold from Example
2.19
with one singularity are not orientable, and are
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
quotients of the above manifolds
M_{\Gamma}
. Other specific quotients of toric flows on
M_{A_{dn}}
for
d\geq 1
and
n\geq 2
, by a cyclic group of order
n
yield additional ancient flows with an orbifold tangent soliton with two singularities associated to cyclic subgroups of
\operatorname{U(2)}
.

10.2.
Curvature estimates

The main result of this section for ancient flows is the following theorem which rephrases Theorem
1.3
in terms of the scale
\varepsilon_{0}
and parameters introduced in (

H
1

).

Theorem 10.4
.

Under the setting of Proposition
8.10
, there exists
T<0
and an ancient solution
(g(t))_{t\,\leq\,T}
to the normalized Ricci flow
\partial_{t}g=-2\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g)+\Lambda g
on
M
satisfying:

(1)

(Curvature tensor)
For
t\leq T
,

C_{1}\,\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}\leq\sup_{M}|\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g(t))|_{g(t)}\leq C_{2}\,\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}\to+\infty\text{ as }t\to-\infty,

for some positive uniform constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
.

(2)

(Covariant derivatives of the curvature tensor)
For
k\geq 0
, there exists
C_{k}>0
such that for
t\leq T
:

\sup_{M}|\nabla^{g(t),\,k}\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g(t))|_{g(t)}\leq C_{k}\,\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{-2}.

(3)

(Ricci curvature)
There exists
C
such that for
t\leq T
,

\sup_{M}|\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g(t))|_{g(t)}\leq C.

(4)

(Scalar curvature) There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
,

\sup_{M}|\mathop{\rm R}\nolimits_{g(t)}-4\Lambda|\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{\iota\sigma}\to 0\text{ as }t\to-\infty.

(5)

(Local Ricci pinching) For
\theta>0
sufficiently small with respect to the supremum of the diameters of
(M,\tilde{g}(t))_{t\,\leq\,T}
, there exists
C(\theta)>0
such that for
t\leq T
:

\sup_{\{r_{o}\,\geq\,\theta\delta(t)\}}|\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g(t))-\Lambda g(t)|_{g(t)}\leq C(\theta)\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{\iota\sigma}\to 0\text{ as }t\to-\infty.

(6)

The metrics
g(t)
converge locally smoothly on
M_{0}
to
g_{o}
outside the singular set and for
t\leq T
,
d_{GH}(M,d_{g(t)}),(M_{o},d_{o}))\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{\delta}.

Moreover, if
\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and if we choose the orientations on the Eguchi-Hanson instantons
(N,\mathbf{eh})
such that they become anti-selfdual,

(1)

(
\mathbf{R}^{+}
-pinching) There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
,

\sup_{M}\left|\mathbf{R}^{+}(g(t))-\frac{\Lambda}{3}\mathop{\rm Id}\nolimits_{\Lambda^{+}}\right|_{g(t)}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{\iota\sigma}\to 0\text{ as }t\to-\infty.

In particular, the ancient solution
(g(t))_{t\,\leq\,T}
satisfies the
\operatorname{PIC}_{+}
curvature condition.

(2)

(Global Ricci pinching)
There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
,

\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g(t))\geq\left(\Lambda-1-C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{\iota\sigma}\right)g(t).

In particular, there exists
c>0
such that for
t\leq T
,

\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g(t))\geq c\mathop{\rm R}\nolimits_{g(t)}g(t).

(3)

(Almost selfduality of
g(t)
)
For
t\leq T
,

\sup_{M}|W^{+}(g(t))|_{g(t)}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}(t)^{\iota\sigma}\to 0\text{ as }t\to-\infty,

for some uniform positive constant
C
.

Remark 10.5
.

The above almost “selfdual metrics of constant scalar curvature” provided by the second part of Theorem
10.4
are reminiscent of the behavior of Poon-LeBrun anti-selfdual metrics
[
Poo86
,
LeB91
]
with constant scalar curvature on
\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}\#\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}
. The instability detected here is similar to that of
[
Via10
]
in the setting of the Yamabe problem: among the finite-dimensional space of symmetric Poon-LeBrun metrics on
\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}\#\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}
, the sine-cone over
\mathbb{RP}^{3}
is shown to be minimizing the Yamabe constant, making it an unstable critical point in the Yamabe setting.

Remark 10.6
.

In the main result of
[
BK17
]
is shown that the Ricci curvature converges to
0
as
t\rightarrow-\infty
on the whole of
M
. Theorem
10.4
only ensures the convergence of
\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g(t))-\Lambda g(t)
to
0
away from the singular set of
M_{o}
. This is explained by the absence of obstructions in
[
BK17
]
, which formally speaking amounts to having the righthand side of (
2.21
) to be
0
in Proposition
2.13
.

Remark 10.7
.

Theorem
10.4
applies to finitely many singular points provided the sizes of the Eguchi-Hanson or other hyperkähler ALE metrics glued near those points are comparable and provided the underlying Einstein orbifold
(M_{o}^{4},g_{o})
is stable.

The proof of Theorem
10.4
is given at the end of this section. We start with estimating each curvature conditions on the perturbed bubble then on the background metric
\tilde{g}(t)
and then proceed to analyzing the solution to the Ricci-DeTurck flow with background metric
