-dimensional space of
2
-forms.

Lemma A.8
.

For a hyperkähler metric
g_{b}
, the
2
-forms in
\mathcal{H}^{2}_{L^{2}}(g_{b})
are anti-selfdual.

Proof.

Consider
\Omega\in\mathcal{H}^{2}_{L^{2}}(g_{b})
and
\Omega^{\pm}:=\frac{1}{2}\left(\Omega\pm\ast\Omega\right)
its corresponding selfdual and anti-selfdual parts. The
2
-forms
\Omega^{\pm}
are still
L^{2}
since the selfdual and anti-selfdual
2
-forms are
pointwise
orthogonal, and they are still harmonic since the Hodge star commutes with the Hodge Laplacian.

Now, the classical Weitzenböck formula for the Hodge Laplacian on selfdual
2
-forms of
[
Bou81
]
applied to
g_{b}
with zero scalar curvature and zero selfdual Weyl curvature rewrites:

0=(dd^{*}+d^{*}d)_{g_{b}}\Omega^{+}=\nabla^{*}\nabla^{g_{b}}\Omega^{+},

and an integration by parts shows that
\Omega^{+}
is parallel and
L^{2}
, hence identically zero.
∎

The following proposition provides a noncompact Hodge theory in weighted Hölder spaces.

Proposition A.9
.

For any
\beta\in(0,4)
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
, the following operators are invertible:

(1)

d_{-,g_{b}}\oplus d^{*}_{g_{b}}:\Lambda^{1}\cap C^{3,\alpha}_{\beta-1}\to(\Lambda^{-}_{g_{b}}\cap(\mathcal{H}^{-}_{L^{2}}(g_{b}))^{\perp}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta})\oplus(\Lambda^{0}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta})
,

(2)

d_{+,g_{b}}\oplus d^{*}_{g_{b}}:\Lambda^{1}\cap C^{3,\alpha}_{\beta-1}\to(\Lambda^{+}_{g_{b}}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta})\oplus(\Lambda^{0}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta})
,

(3)

(d^{*}_{g_{b}},d)(\eta^{-},f):=d^{*}_{g_{b}}\eta^{-}+df
seen as an operator from
(\Lambda^{-}_{g_{b}}\cap(\mathcal{H}^{-}_{L^{2}}(g_{b}))^{\perp}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta})\oplus(\Lambda^{0}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta})
to
\Lambda^{1}\cap C^{1,\alpha}_{\beta+1}
, and

(4)

(d^{*}_{g_{b}},d)(\eta^{+},f):=d^{*}_{g_{b}}\eta^{+}+df
seen as an operator from
(\Lambda^{+}_{g_{b}}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta})\oplus(\Lambda^{0}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta})
to
\Lambda^{1}\cap C^{1,\alpha}_{\beta+1}
.

More precisely, there exists
C>0
depending on
g_{b}
so that: for any
\gamma\in\Lambda^{1}\cap C^{3,\alpha}_{\beta-1}
,

(1)

\|\gamma\|_{C^{3,\alpha}_{\beta-1}}<C(\|d_{-,g_{b}}\gamma\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}}+\|d^{*}_{g_{b}}\gamma\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}})
,

(2)

\|\gamma\|_{C^{3,\alpha}_{\beta-1}}<C(\|d_{+,g_{b}}\gamma\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}}+\|d^{*}_{g_{b}}\gamma\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}})
,

(3)

\|\eta^{-}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}}+\|f\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}}<C\|d_{g_{b}}^{*}\eta^{-}+df\|_{C^{1,\alpha}_{\beta+1}}
if
\eta^{-}\perp_{L^{2}(g_{b})}\mathcal{H}^{-}_{L^{2}}(g_{b})
, and

(4)

\|\eta^{+}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}}+\|f\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}}<C\|d_{g_{b}}^{*}\eta^{+}+df\|_{C^{1,\alpha}_{\beta+1}}
.

Remark A.10
.

Observe that any element in
\mathcal{H}^{2}_{L^{2}}(g_{b})
decays as fast as
r^{-4}
at infinity according to [(
11.5
), Section
11.3.4
]. This fact implies that the subspace
(\mathcal{H}^{-}_{L^{2}}(g_{b}))^{\perp}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
in
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
is closed for
\beta>0
, i.e.
(\mathcal{H}^{-}_{L^{2}}(g_{b}))^{\perp}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
is a Banach space with respect to the norm induced by that of
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
.

Proof.

We only prove the isomorphism property for the operator

d_{-,g_{b}}\oplus d^{*}_{g_{b}}:\Lambda^{1}\,\cap\,C^{3,\alpha}_{\beta-1}\to(\Lambda^{-}_{g_{b}}\,\cap\,(\mathcal{H}^{-}_{L^{2}}(g_{b}))^{\perp}\,\cap\,C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta})\oplus(\Lambda^{0}\,\cap\,C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}),

the case of
d_{+,g_{b}}\oplus d^{*}_{g_{b}}
is similar and simpler.

The operator
d_{-,g_{b}}\oplus d^{*}_{g_{b}}
is elliptic as part of the classical elliptic complex

(A.1)

0\xrightarrow{}\Lambda^{0}\xrightarrow{d}\Lambda^{1}\xrightarrow{d_{-}}\Lambda^{-}\xrightarrow{}0.

Between weighted Hölder spaces,
d_{-,g_{b}}\oplus d^{*}_{g_{b}}
is therefore Fredholm as long as a discrete set of exceptional values of
\beta
is avoided. Let us determine a range in which it is injective and surjective up to
\mathcal{H}^{-}_{L^{2}}(g_{b})
.

Let us start with the injectivity, and let us omit the references to the metric
g_{b}
for simplicity. Assume that
d_{-}\gamma=0
and
d^{*}\gamma=0
, then in particular, since

2d^{*}d_{-}\gamma=-*d*(d-*d)\gamma=-*d*d\gamma=d^{*}d\gamma,

we see that
(dd^{*}+d^{*}d)\gamma=0
, which implies that
\gamma=0
if it lies in
C^{3,\alpha}_{\beta-1}
with
\beta\in(-1,4)
. Indeed, for
\beta>3
, the only solution is
\gamma=0
since
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{L^{2}}(g_{b})=0
, and there are no exceptional values for
(dd^{*}+d^{*}d)
in the interval
(-1,4)
following an adaptation of the argument of
[
Ozu20
, Lemme 4.30]
.

Let us now prove the surjectivity. The adjoint of
d_{-,g_{b}}\oplus d^{*}_{g_{b}}:\Lambda^{1}\cap C^{3,\alpha}_{\beta-1}\to(\Lambda^{-}_{g_{b}}\cap(\mathcal{H}^{-}_{L^{2}}(g_{b}))^{\perp}\cap C^{3,\alpha}_{\beta})\oplus(\Lambda^{0}\cap C^{3,\alpha}_{\beta})
is the operator
(d^{*}_{g_{b}},d)(\eta,f):=d^{*}_{g_{b}}\eta+df
seen as an operator from
(\Lambda^{-}_{g_{b}}\cap(\mathcal{H}^{-}_{L^{2}}(g_{b}))^{\perp}\cap C^{4,\alpha}_{4-\beta})\oplus(\Lambda^{0}\cap C^{4,\alpha}_{4-\beta})
to
\Lambda^{1}\cap C^{3,\alpha}_{5-\beta}
.

Assume that
d^{*}\eta=0
and
df=0
with
(\eta,f)\in(\Lambda^{-}_{g_{b}}\cap(\mathcal{H}^{-}_{L^{2}}(g_{b}))^{\perp}\cap C^{4,\alpha}_{4-\beta})\oplus(\Lambda^{0}\cap C^{4,\alpha}_{4-\beta})
. If
4-\beta>0
, then
df=0
implies that
f=0
since it is constant and decaying at infinity. For
\eta
, we note that there are no exceptional weights in
(0,4)
for
2d_{-}d^{*}=dd^{*}+d^{*}d
on
\Lambda^{-}
. Since for
C^{4,\alpha}_{3}\subset L^{2}(g_{b})
, the kernel is
\mathcal{H}^{-}_{L^{2}}(g_{b})
, we obtain surjectivity for all
4-\beta\in(0,4)
, i.e.
\beta\in(0,4)
.
∎

In order to keep track of the dependence on the parameter
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{3k}
, we will construct geometric tensors on the manifold under consideration that vary analytically in
\zeta
. Recall the notation
\omega_{i}^{+}(\mathbf{e})
from (
2.1
) on Euclidean space, and denote by
(\Omega_{j}^{-})_{j}
the basis of anti-selfdual
2
-forms from Section
11.3
.

Proposition A.11
.

Let
g_{b}
be a hyperkähler ALE metric and let
\chi
be a fixed cut-off function supported in an ALE chart of
g_{b}
(in the sense of Definition
2.4
). Then, for any small enough symmetric
2
-tensor
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
with
\beta\in(0,4)
, there exist:

(1)

a basis
(\Omega_{1}^{-}(h),\dots,\Omega_{k}^{-}(h))
that is anti-selfdual with respect to
g_{b}+h
and which lies in
\mathcal{H}^{2}_{L^{2}}(g_{b}+h)
with
\Omega_{i}^{-}(h)-\Omega_{i}^{-}\perp_{g_{b}}\Omega_{j}^{-}
for all
i,j\in\{1,\dots,k\}
,

(2)

a unique family
(\omega^{+}_{1}(g),\omega^{+}_{2}(g),\omega^{+}_{3}(g))
of
g=(g_{b}+h)
-harmonic selfdual
2
-forms with
\omega^{+}_{i}(g)-\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b})\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
,

(3)

for
2<\beta<4
, a unique function
u
satisfying
\Delta_{g_{b}+h}u(h)=4
with
u(h)-\chi r^{2}/2\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta-2}
.

Moreover the following maps are real-analytic:

(1)

h\mapsto\Omega_{i}^{-}(h)
between
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
and
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
,

(2)

h\mapsto\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b}+h)-\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b})
between
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
and
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
, and

(3)

if
2<\beta<4
, then
h\mapsto u(h)-\chi r^{2}/2
between
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
and
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta-2}
.

As a consequence, the map
h\mapsto\mathbf{o}_{ij}(h):=\Omega_{j}^{-}(h)\circ_{g_{b}+h}\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b}+h)
is also real-analytic between
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
and
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
.

Proof.

We prove all of the above results by using the real-analytic implicit function Theorem
A.3
to appropriate maps.
First, let
\mathcal{B}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
be the ball of radius
1/2
centered at
0
in
\operatorname{Sym}^{2}(M)\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
so that from Lemma
A.7
, it is clear that the following maps are real-analytic:

•

\Phi_{1}:\mathcal{B}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}\times(\Lambda^{-}_{g_{b}}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta})\times(\Lambda^{0}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta})\rightarrow(\Lambda^{1}\cap C^{1,\alpha}_{\beta+1})\times\mathbb{R}^{k}
, where

\begin{split}\Phi_{1}&(h,\eta_{i}^{-},f_{i}):=\\
&\left(d^{*}_{g_{b}+h}\pi_{g_{b}+h}^{-}(\Omega_{i}^{-}+\eta_{i}^{-})+df_{i},\left(\int_{\Sigma_{j}}\pi_{g_{b}+h}^{-}(\Omega_{i}^{-}+\eta_{i}^{-})-\int_{\Sigma_{j}}\Omega_{i}^{-}\right)_{j}\right).\end{split}

•

\Phi_{2}:\mathcal{B}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}\times(\Lambda^{+}_{g_{b}}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta})\times(\Lambda^{0}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta})\rightarrow\Lambda^{1}\cap C^{1,\alpha}_{\beta+1}
where

\Phi_{2}(h,\eta_{i}^{+},\phi_{i}):=(d^{*}_{g_{b}+h}\pi_{g_{b}+h}^{+}(\chi\omega_{i}(\mathbf{e})^{+}+\eta_{i}^{+})+d\phi_{i}).

•

\Phi_{3}:\mathcal{B}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}\times(\Lambda^{0}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta})\rightarrow\Lambda^{0}\cap C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta+2}
where

\Phi_{3}(h,v):=\Delta_{g_{b}+h}(\chi r^{2}/2+v)-4.

Here
\chi
a cut-off function supported in a neighborhood of infinity.

Given solutions to
\Phi_{1}(h,\eta_{i}^{-},f_{i})=0
,
\Phi_{2}(h,\eta_{i}^{+},\phi_{i})=0
and
\Phi_{3}(h,v)=0
, we define:

•

\Omega_{i}^{-}(h):=\pi_{g_{b}+h}^{-}(\Omega_{i}^{-}+\eta_{i}^{-})
,

•

\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b}+h):=\pi_{g_{b}+h}^{+}(\chi\omega_{i}(\mathbf{e})^{+}+\eta_{i}^{+})
, and

•

u(h):=\chi r^{2}/2+v
.

They satisfy the desired properties.

Note that the functions
(f_{i})_{i}
and
(\phi_{i})_{i}
are simply auxiliary functions simplifying the proof of the invertibility of our operators.

Our goal is to parametrize zeros of the maps
\Phi_{1}
,
\Phi_{2}
and
\Phi_{3}
by the perturbation
h
by applying the implicit function Theorem
A.3
. For this we need the existence of solutions at
h=0
, and the invertibility of the linearization at
h=0
in the direction of the second variable of the maps
\Phi_{i}
for
i=1,2,3
.

See the discussion preceding this proposition for
\Omega_{i}^{-}=\Omega_{i}^{-}(0)
and for the existence of
u(0)
, see Section
2.3
. As for the existence of
\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b})
, it can be proved at the same time as the invertibility of the linearization of the affine map
\Phi_{2}
at
h=0
. Indeed,
\Phi_{2}(0,0,0)\in\Lambda^{1}\cap C^{1,\alpha}_{\beta}
, and the surjectivity of the linearization lets one obtain the existence of
\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b})
. We give a proof for completeness. Notice that
(dd^{*}+d^{*}d)_{g_{b}}(\chi\pi_{g_{b}}^{+}(\omega_{i}^{+}(\mathbf{e})))\in C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta+2}
and that it is selfdual. Now,
(dd^{*}+d^{*}d)_{g_{b}}:C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}\to C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta+2}
is Fredholm and invertible because of the Weitzenböck formula
(dd^{*}+d^{*}d)_{g_{b}}=\nabla^{*}\nabla^{g_{b}}
on selfdual
2
-forms as recalled in the proof of Lemma
A.8
. Consequently, there exists a unique selfdual
2
-form
\eta_{i}^{+}\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
such that
(dd^{*}+d^{*}d)_{g_{b}}\eta_{i}^{+}=-(dd^{*}+d^{*}d)_{g_{b}}(\pi_{g_{b}}^{+}(\chi\omega_{i}^{+}(\mathbf{e})))
, and we define

\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b}):=\pi_{g_{b}}^{+}(\chi\omega_{i}(\mathbf{e})^{+})+\eta_{i}^{+}.

Let us now investigate the invertibility of the linearizations of the maps
\Phi_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
at
h=0
.

The linearization of the affine map
(\eta^{-},f)\mapsto\Phi_{1}(0,\eta^{-},f)
is
(\eta^{-},f)\mapsto(d^{*}_{g_{b}}\eta^{-}+df,(\int_{\Sigma_{j}}\eta^{-})_{j})
, where one recognizes on the first factor the operator
d^{*}\oplus d
which is surjective between our spaces of interest by Proposition
A.9
. It is however not injective: its kernel being
\mathcal{H}^{-}_{L^{2}}(g_{b})
on which
\eta^{-}\mapsto(\int_{\Sigma_{j}}\eta^{-})_{j}
is invertible, this allows one to conclude about the invertibility of the differential of
\Phi_{1}
with respect to the last two variables.

Similarly, the linearization of
\Phi_{2}
is invertible by Proposition
A.9
.

Finally, regarding the invertibility of the linearization of the map
\Phi_{3}
at
g_{b}
with respect to the second variable, it is a well-known fact that
\Delta_{g_{b}}:C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta-2}\to C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta}
is invertible for
2<\beta<4
: see for instance
[
Bar86
]
.

Applying the implicit function Theorem
A.3
lets us conclude for each map
\Phi_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
.

∎

By applying the same technique as that of the proof of Proposition
A.11
applied to the map:

\Phi_{4}(h,k,(l_{ij})_{ij}):=\Delta_{L,g_{b}+h}(h_{2}(0)+k)-\sum_{i,j}l_{ij}\mathbf{o}_{ij}(h)-\Lambda(g_{b}+h),

seen as a map from
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}\times C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta-2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3k}
with values into
C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta}
, where
\mathbf{o}_{ij}(h)=\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b}+h)\circ_{g_{b}+h}\Omega_{j}^{-}(h)
we may prove the following result.
Notice that
\Phi_{4}
is well-defined provided there exists
(h_{2}(0),l_{ij}(0))\in C^{2,\alpha}_{-2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3k}
such that
\Phi_{4}(0,0,l_{ij}(0))=0
.

Corollary A.12
.

Let
(h_{2}(0),l_{ij}(0))\in C^{2,\alpha}_{-2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3k}
be solutions of
\Delta_{L,g_{b}}h_{2}(0)-\sum_{ij}l_{ij}(0)\mathbf{o}_{ij}(0)-\Lambda g_{b}=0
as in Proposition
11.6
. Then, for any
\beta\in(2,4)
and for every
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
small enough, there exists unique
(k(h),l_{ij}(h))\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta-2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3k}
such that:

•

\Delta_{L,g_{b}+h}(h_{2}(0)+k(h))-\sum_{ij}l_{ij}(h)\mathbf{o}_{ij}(h)=\Lambda(g_{b}+h)
, and

•

h_{2}(h)=\lambda(g_{b}+h)+\sum_{i}\phi_{i}^{-}\circ\omega^{+}_{i}(g_{b}+h)
, where
\phi_{i}^{-}
are
(g_{b}+h)
-anti-selfdual
2
-forms such that
\int_{\Sigma_{j}}\phi_{i}^{-}=0
.

Moreover, the map
h\mapsto(h_{2}(h)-h_{2}(0)):=k(h),l_{ij}(h))
is real-analytic as a map from
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
with values into
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta-2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3k}
.

Let us also compute the linearization of the map
h\mapsto\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b}+h)
which we will use shortly after.

Lemma A.13
.

The linearization of the map
h\mapsto\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b}+h)
for
h=\lambda g_{b}+\sum_{i}\phi_{i}^{-}\circ\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b})
at
h=0
is:

-\phi_{i}^{-}+S_{g_{b}}(\phi_{i}^{-}),

for some linear operator
S_{g_{b}}:C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}\cap\Lambda^{-}\to C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}\cap\Lambda^{+}
whose kernel is
\mathcal{H}^{2}_{L^{2}}(g_{b})
. In particular, if
h=\sum_{ij}h_{ij}\Omega_{j}^{-}\circ\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b})
for
h_{ij}\in\mathbb{R}
, then the linearization is exactly

-\sum_{ij}h_{ij}\Omega_{j}^{-}.

Proof.

This is the issue of the variation of the set of selfdual harmonic
2
-forms addressed in
[
Don86
]
, see also
[
NO24
]
. Denote for
t\in(-1,1)

g_{t}:=g_{b}+th
for
h=\lambda g+\sum_{i}\phi_{i}^{-}\circ\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b})
, and let
\phi:\Lambda^{+}\to\Lambda^{-}
be the linear map defined by
\phi(\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b}))=\phi_{i}^{-}
, and let
\alpha_{t}
be one of the
\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b}+th)
.

We will generally denote with a “dot” the derivatives at
t=0
. Our goal here is to understand
\dot{\alpha}
as a function of
\dot{g}
and
\alpha_{0}=\alpha
. For this, we decompose it into its selfdual and anti-selfdual parts.

Differentiating the equation
\pi^{t}_{-}\alpha_{t}:=\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{t}-*_{g_{t}}\alpha_{t})=0
, for at
t=0
thanks to the formulas for the variation of the projection
[
Biq16
,
NO24
]
gives:

(A.2)

\pi_{-}\dot{\alpha}=-\phi(\alpha)=-\phi_{i}^{-}.

We will then study the selfdual part
\dot{\alpha}^{+}
.

Following
[
Don86
]
or from the implicit function theorem, denote
G_{+}
, the inverse for
d_{+}^{*}d=\frac{1}{2}(d^{*}_{g_{b}}d+dd^{*}_{g_{b}}):C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}\to C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta+2}
from
\Lambda^{+}_{g_{b}}
to itself (only), where
d_{+}^{*}:=\pi_{g_{b}}^{+}d^{*}_{g_{b}}:\Lambda^{3}\to\Lambda^{+}_{g_{b}}
. Define
S_{g_{b}}:\Lambda^{-}_{g_{b}}\to\Lambda^{+}_{g_{b}}
as
G_{+}\circ d_{+}^{*}d
(note that
d_{+}^{*}d=d^{*}_{g_{b}}d-dd^{*}_{g_{b}}
on
\Lambda^{-}_{g_{b}}
). Maybe more concretely, if
\beta_{-}\in\Lambda^{-}
and
\beta_{+}\in\Lambda^{+}
satisfy
d\beta_{+}=d\beta_{-}
and are in
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
, then
S_{g}(\beta_{-})=\beta_{+}
.

This operator lets us understand the deformation
\dot{\alpha}^{+}
. Indeed, since
0=d\dot{\alpha}^{+}+d\dot{\alpha}^{-}=d\dot{\alpha}^{+}-d\phi(\alpha)
. This tells us that,
d\dot{\alpha}^{+}=d\phi(\alpha)
,
\dot{\alpha}^{+}\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
and
\phi(\alpha)\in\Lambda^{-}
, and consequently,
\dot{\alpha}^{+}=S(\phi(\alpha))
and finally:

\partial_{t|t=0}\alpha_{t}=-\phi_{i}^{-}+S_{g_{b}}(\phi_{i}^{-}).

∎

As explained in Section
11
, the elements
\Omega_{j}^{-}
may be chosen so that their Poincaré duals of the
\Sigma_{j}
, the
\Sigma_{j}
are smooth embedded
2
-spheres. Recall that we defined
\zeta=(\zeta_{i}^{j})_{ij}\in\mathbb{R}^{3k}
as:

\zeta_{i}^{j}:=\int_{\Sigma_{j}}{\omega}_{i}^{+}(g_{\zeta}).

This is Kronheimer’s period map described in Section
11
.
Let us now explain how to precise the above analytic dependence of the metrics, even after their first term of the expansion in
r^{-4}
. The key is to use the following norms in order to separate the explicit
r^{-4}
-term
H^{4}_{\zeta}
from the rest of the expansion of the nearby metrics. Consider
\chi:\mathbb{R}^{+}\to[0,1]
a cut-off function supported in
[1,+\infty)\subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

Definition A.14

(
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta,**}
-norm on a ALE orbifold)
.

Let
(N,g_{b})
be an ALE orbifold, and let
h
be a symmetric
2
-tensor on
N
, and assume that
h=H^{4}+O(r_{b}^{-4-\beta})
for
\beta>0
and
H^{4}
a homogeneous harmonic symmetric
2
-tensor with respect to
\mathbf{e}
with
|H^{4}|\sim r_{\mathbf{e}}^{-4}
. We define its
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta,**}
-norm by

\|h\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta,**}}:=\sup r_{\mathbf{e}}^{4}|H^{4}|_{\mathbf{e}}+\big\|(1+r_{b})^{4}(h-\chi(|\zeta|r_{b}^{2})H^{4})\big\|_{C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta,*}}.

Proposition A.15
.

Let
g_{b}=g_{\zeta_{0}}
be a hyperkähler ALE metric on
N
for
\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3k}\setminus\mathcal{D}
, where
\mathcal{D}
is the “forbidden set” introduced in Section
11
and let
(\Sigma_{j})_{j}
be the associated elements of
H^{2}_{c}(N)
.
Let
\beta\in(0,4)
.
Then, for every
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{3k}
close enough to
\zeta_{0}
, there exists a unique hyperkähler metric
g_{\zeta}=g_{b}+\tilde{h}^{5}_{\zeta}=g_{b}+\chi_{\zeta}H^{4}_{\zeta}+\tilde{h}^{5}_{\zeta}
such that
\tilde{h}^{5}_{\zeta}\in C^{2,\alpha}_{4+\beta}
for any
0<\beta<1
and:

(1)

B_{g_{b}}(g_{\zeta}-g_{b})=0
, and

(2)

\int_{\Sigma_{j}}\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{\zeta})=\zeta_{i}^{j}
.

Moreover, the map
\zeta\mapsto g_{\zeta}-g_{\zeta_{0}}-\chi_{\zeta}H^{4}_{\zeta}
is real-analytic as a map from a neighborhood of
\zeta_{0}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3k}
with values into
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}
.

Proof.

This is again an application of the implicit function Theorem
A.3
. We apply it to the map: for
(\zeta,h)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta,**}
,

\begin{split}\Phi_{5}(\zeta,h)&:=(\Phi_{5}^{1}(\zeta,h),\Phi_{5}^{2}(h))\in\mathbb{R}^{3k}\times(\ker_{L^{2}}\Delta_{L,g_{b}})^{\perp}\cap C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta+6},\\
\Phi_{5}^{1}(\zeta,h)_{ij}&:=\int_{\Sigma_{j}}\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b}+h)-\zeta_{i}^{j},\quad\Phi_{5}^{2}(h):=\pi_{(\ker_{L^{2}}\Delta_{L,g_{b}})^{\perp}}\left(\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g_{b}+h)-\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{b}}(h)}(g_{b}+h)\right),\end{split}

where
\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b}+h)
is defined in Proposition
A.11
, and where
\pi_{(\ker_{L^{2}}\Delta_{L,g_{b}})^{\perp}}
denotes the orthogonal projection on
(\ker_{L^{2}}\Delta_{L,g_{b}})^{\perp}
.

We already have a solution to
\Phi_{5}(\zeta,h)=(0,0)
if
h=0
by assumption since
\Phi_{5}(\zeta_{0},0)=(0,0)
. We need to show that the linearization of
\Phi_{5}
with respect to the second variable is invertible.

Indeed, it is well-known (and reproved in the present function spaces in
[
Ozu20
]
) that the infinitesimal Ricci-flat deformations of
g_{b}
are
integrable
, meaning that solving

\pi_{(\ker_{L^{2}}\Delta_{L,g_{b}})^{\perp}}\left(\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g_{b}+h)-\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{b}}(h)}(g_{b}+h)\right)=0,

is equivalent to solving
\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g_{b}+h)-\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{B_{g_{b}}(h)}(g_{b}+h)=0
, which is also equivalent to solving both
\mathop{\rm Ric}\nolimits(g_{b}+h)=0
and
B_{g_{b}}(h)=0
by Bianchi’s identity together with the invertibility of
B_{g_{b}}(\mathop{\rm\mathscr{L}}\nolimits_{V}(g_{b}))=\Delta_{g_{b}}V
on a Ricci flat metric
g_{b}
for any
C^{2}_{loc}
vector field
V
.

Therefore it suffices to show that the linearization of
\Phi_{5}
with respect to the second variable is invertible. From
[
Ozu22b
, Lemma 5.5]
, the linearization of
\Phi_{5}^{2}
at
0
is surjective between
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta,**}
and
(\ker_{L^{2}}\Delta_{L,g_{b}})^{\perp}\cap C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta+6}
, and its kernel is
\ker_{L^{2}}\Delta_{L,g_{b}}\subset C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta,**}
. Now, the
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta,**}
-closed space of
2
-tensors
h=\frac{\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{b}}h}{4}g_{b}+\sum_{i}\phi_{i}^{-}\circ\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b})
with
\int_{\Sigma_{j}}\phi_{i}^{-}=0
for all
i,j
is a complement of
\ker_{L^{2}}\Delta_{L,g_{b}}
, see for instance
[
Biq16
]
.

Any
2
-tensor
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta,**}
splits as
h=h_{\perp}+h_{T}=h_{\perp}+\sum_{ij}h_{ij}\Omega_{j}^{-}\circ\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b})\in S\oplus\ker_{L^{2}}\Delta_{L,g_{b}}
where
S
is the complement of
\ker_{L^{2}}\Delta_{L,g_{b}}
composed of symmetric
2
-tensors
h_{\perp}=\frac{\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g_{b}}h}{4}g_{b}+\sum_{i}\phi_{i}^{-}\circ\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b})
with
\int_{\Sigma_{j}}\phi_{i}^{-}=0
. The linearization of
\Phi_{5}^{2}
is invertible on
S
by the above discussion.

Consequently, we are left with proving that the linearization of
h\mapsto\Phi_{5}^{1}(\zeta,h)
computed in Lemma
A.13
is invertible at
(\zeta,h)=(0,0)
on
\ker_{L^{2}}\Delta_{L,g_{b}}
. By Lemma
A.13
, the infinitesimal variation of
h\rightarrow\int_{\Sigma_{k}}\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b}+h)
associated to the infinitesimal deformation
\sum_{ij}h_{ij}\Omega_{j}^{-}\circ\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b})
of the metric is
-\sum_{ij}h_{ij}\int_{\Sigma_{k}}\Omega_{j}^{-}=\sum_{ij}a_{jk}h_{ij}
, where the
a_{jk}
are the coefficients of the intersection form associated to the Poincaré duals
(\Sigma_{j})_{j}
. Recall
A=(a_{jk})_{jk}
defines an invertible matrix as explained in Section
11.3.1
. Consequently, the linearization of
h\mapsto\Phi^{1}_{5}(\zeta,h)
is invertible at
(\zeta,h)=(0,0)
since it is

\sum_{ij}h_{ij}\Omega_{j}^{-}\circ\omega_{i}^{+}(g_{b})\mapsto\Big(\sum_{j}a_{jk}h_{ij}\Big).

∎

We may therefore parametrize every tensor of interest by
\zeta
in a real-analytic way, and obtain controls on their variations at every order as follows.

Proposition A.16
.

The following maps are real-analytic for
\zeta
in a neighborhood of
\zeta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3k}\backslash\mathcal{D}
: for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\beta\in(2,4)
,

(1)

\zeta\mapsto g_{\zeta}=g_{\zeta_{0}}+h_{\zeta}=g_{\zeta_{0}}+\chi_{\zeta}H^{4}_{\zeta}-\chi_{\zeta_{0}}H^{4}_{\zeta_{0}}+\tilde{h}^{5}_{\zeta}
with the notation of Proposition
A.15
where
h_{\zeta}
has values into
C^{l,\alpha}_{5^{-}}
symmetric
2
-tensors,

(2)

\zeta\mapsto\mathbf{o}_{ij}(h_{\zeta})=:\mathbf{o}_{ij}(\zeta)
with values into
C^{l,\alpha}_{\beta}
symmetric traceless-2-tensors,

(3)

\zeta\mapsto h_{2}(h_{\zeta})-h_{2}(h_{\zeta_{0}})=:h_{2}(\zeta)-h_{2}(\zeta_{0})
with values into
C^{l,\alpha}_{\beta-2}
symmetric
2
-tensors,

(4)

\zeta\mapsto u(h_{\zeta})-u(h_{\zeta_{0}})=:u(\zeta)-u(\zeta_{0})
with values into
C^{l,\alpha}_{\beta}
functions.

As a consequence, we also have the following controls: for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, denote
d^{(m)}_{\zeta}
the
m
-th derivative of a tensor with respect to the
\zeta
variable, as
m
-linear operators from
(\mathbb{R}^{3k})^{m}
, we find the following pointwise controls. For any
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
\kappa>0

(1)

•

\left|\nabla^{g_{\zeta_{0}},\,l}(d^{(m)}_{\zeta}g(\zeta_{1})-d^{(m)}_{\zeta}g(\zeta_{2}))\right|\leq C\frac{|\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}|}{|\zeta_{0}|^{1+m}}|\zeta_{0}|^{2-\kappa}(\varepsilon_{0}+r_{o})^{-4+2\kappa-l},

•

\left|\nabla^{g_{\zeta_{0}},\,l}(d^{(m)}_{\zeta}\tilde{h}^{5}(\zeta_{1})-d^{(m)}_{\zeta}\tilde{h}^{5}(\zeta_{2}))\right|\leq C\frac{|\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}|}{|\zeta_{0}|^{1+m}}|\zeta_{0}|^{5-\kappa}(\varepsilon_{0}+r_{o})^{-5+2\kappa-l},

(2)

\left|\nabla^{g_{\zeta_{0}},\,l}(d^{(m)}_{\zeta}\mathbf{o}_{ij}(\zeta_{1})-d^{(m)}_{\zeta}\mathbf{o}_{ij}(\zeta_{2}))\right|\leq C\frac{|\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}|}{|\zeta_{0}|^{1+m}}|\zeta_{0}|^{2-\kappa}(\varepsilon_{0}+r_{o})^{-4+2\kappa-l},

(3)

\left|\nabla^{g_{\zeta_{0}},\,l}(d^{(m)}_{\zeta}h_{2}(\zeta_{1})-d^{(m)}_{\zeta}h_{2}(\zeta_{2}))\right|\leq C\frac{|\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}|}{|\zeta_{0}|^{1+m}}|\zeta_{0}|^{2-\kappa}(\varepsilon_{0}+r_{o})^{-2+2\kappa-l},

(4)

\left|\nabla^{g_{\zeta_{0}},\,l}(d^{(m)}_{\zeta}u(\zeta_{1})-d^{(m)}_{\zeta}u(\zeta_{2}))\right|\leq C\frac{|\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}|}{|\zeta_{0}|^{1+m}}|\zeta_{0}|^{2-\kappa}(\varepsilon_{0}+r_{o})^{-2+2\kappa-l}.

Proof.

The analyticity at
\zeta_{0}
with
|\zeta_{0}|=1
away from the codimension
3
forbidden set
\mathcal{D}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3k}
provides the estimates: for
r
depending on
\zeta_{0}
and some constant
C
and for
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}
with
|\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{0}|<r
and
|\zeta_{2}-\zeta_{0}|<r
, the estimates hold.
The estimates for others
\zeta=(\varepsilon,\phi)^{*}\zeta_{0}
are obtained by rescaling thanks to (
11.1
). For instance, one has

\displaystyle|g_{(\varepsilon,\phi)^{*}\zeta_{1}}-g_{(\varepsilon,\phi)^{*}\zeta_{2}}|_{g_{(\varepsilon,\phi)^{*}\zeta_{0}}}=

\displaystyle\,(s_{(\varepsilon,\phi)})_{*}|g_{\zeta_{1}}-g_{\zeta_{2}}|_{g_{\zeta_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C|\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}|\cdot(s_{(\varepsilon,\phi)})_{*}|\zeta_{0}|^{2-\kappa}(|\zeta_{0}|^{\frac{1}{2}}+r_{o})^{-4+2\kappa}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,C\frac{|\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}|}{\varepsilon^{2}}|\varepsilon^{2}\zeta_{0}|^{2-\kappa}(|\varepsilon^{2}|\zeta_{0}||^{\frac{1}{2}}+r_{o})^{-4+2\kappa}.

As a sanity check, one verifies that these estimates are true on the Eguchi-Hanson metric.
∎

Appendix B
Heat flows modulo obstructions

In this section, we state and give a proof of the existence of solutions to parabolic equations “modulo obstructions” as used in
[
BK17
, Corollary 6.6]
, and in Proposition
7.20
of the present paper. Let us mention some standard simplifying steps before stating and proving the core result.
We only discuss the ancient case, the immortal case can be adapted easily.
As discussed in the proof of Proposition
7.20
, we only need to show the existence of solutions on intervals
[T^{\prime},T]
.
Define:

•

I:=[T^{\prime},T]
,

•

(g_{t})_{t\in I}
to be the curve
(\tilde{g}(t))_{t}
restricted to
I
as defined in (
3.3
),

•

X:=C^{2,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}
and
Y:=C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
restricted to
(T^{\prime},T]
as defined in Section
6.1
,

•

(P_{t})_{t\in I}
to be the linearized operator
(\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}+2\Lambda)_{t\in I}
,

•

(V_{t})_{t\in I}
the spaces spanned by
((\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t))_{i},\tilde{g}(t),\tilde{c}(t))_{t\in I}
from
\tilde{\mathbf{O}}(t)
as defined in Definition
3.12
.

•

W_{t}
the
L^{2}_{g_{t}}
-orthogonal complement of
V_{t}
for each
t\in I
.

For a family of symmetric
2
-tensors
(h_{t})_{t\in I}
in
X
, we will denote the corresponding norm by
\|h_{t}\|_{X}
with a slight abuse of notation.

Proposition B.1
.

With the notations introduced above, assume that: there exist
C>0
and
\varepsilon>0
such that,

(1)

the operator
\partial_{t}-P_{t}:X\rightarrow Y
is continuous:
\|(\partial_{t}-P_{t})h_{t}\|_{Y}\leq C\|h_{t}\|_{X}
for all
(h_{t})_{t\in I}\in X
,

(2)

For any
(\psi_{t})_{t\in I}\in Y
, there exists a unique
(u_{t})_{t\in I}\in X
such that
(\partial_{t}-P_{t})u_{t}=\psi_{t}
and
u_{0}=0
,
with

\|u_{t}\|_{X}\leq C\|\psi_{t}\|_{Y},

(3)

the projections are continuous:
\|\pi_{V_{t}}(h_{t})\|_{X}\leq C\|h_{t}\|_{X}
, and
\|\pi_{W_{t}}(h_{t})\|_{X}\leq C\|h_{t}\|_{X}
,

(4)

if
h_{t}\in V_{t}
, then
\|\pi_{W_{t}}(\partial_{t}-P_{t})h_{t}\|_{Y}\leq\varepsilon\|h_{t}\|_{X}
.

Then, if
\varepsilon>0
is small enough compared to
C>0,
for any family of
2
-tensors on
M
,
(\psi_{t})_{t\in I}\in Y
and any initial
2
-tensor
h_{0}\in W_{0}
, then there exists a family of
2
-tensors
(h_{t})_{t\in I}\in X
so that for every
t\in I
, one has

(1)

(\partial_{t}-P_{t})h_{t}-\psi_{t}\in V_{t}
,

(2)

h_{0}=0
, and

(3)

h_{t}\in W_{t}
.

Remark B.2
.

The only constant that may depend on
T^{\prime}
in the assumptions of Proposition
B.1
is that of item (
2
): see the discussion after the proof of Proposition
B.1
below.

Proof.

Define the following “box” operator acting on family of symmetric
2
-tensors
(h_{t})_{t\in I}\in X
,

\Box_{t}h_{t}:=\left(\partial_{t}-P_{t}\right)h_{t}.

Along the proof, we will use
C>0
to denote various constants that may change from line to line, but which are independent of
\varepsilon>0
.

We prove the property by an iteration starting with
h^{0}_{t}\in X
the only solution of
\Box_{t}h^{0}_{t}=\psi_{t}
with initial condition
h^{0}_{0}=0
, which exists and is controlled as
\|h^{0}_{t}\|_{X}\leq C\|\psi_{t}\|_{Y}
from Assumption (
2
). Of course, it does not satisfy
h^{0}_{t}\in W_{t}
a priori.

We define a map
F:X\to X
as follows. Given
h_{t}
solving
\Box_{t}h_{t}-\psi_{t}\in V_{t}
with initial condition
h_{0}=0
, define

u
as the unique solution of

\Box_{t}u_{t}=\pi_{W_{t}}\Box_{t}\pi_{V_{t}}h_{t},\quad u_{0}=0,

which, by Assumption (
2
), (
3
) and (
4
) is controlled as

\displaystyle\|u_{t}\|_{X}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\pi_{W_{t}}\Box_{t}\pi_{V_{t}}h_{t}\|_{Y},

using (
2
),

(B.1)

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\|\pi_{V_{t}}h_{t}\|_{X},

using (
4
),

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\|h_{t}\|_{X},

using (
3
).

Define

(B.2)

F(h_{t}):=\pi_{W_{t}}h_{t}+u_{t},

which by construction satisfies
F(h_{0})=0
. Here
F(h_{t})
is a slight abuse of notation for
F((h_{s})_{s\in I})_{t}
. The family
(F(h_{t}))_{t\in I}
solves an equation of the desired type:

(B.3)

\Box_{t}F(h_{t})-\psi_{t}=\Box_{t}h_{t}-\Box_{t}\pi_{V_{t}}h_{t}+\pi_{W_{t}}\Box_{t}\pi_{V_{t}}h_{t}-\psi_{t}=\Box_{t}h_{t}-\psi_{t}-\pi_{V_{t}}\Box_{t}\pi_{V_{t}}h_{t}\in V_{t}.

Now, on the one hand let us show that
\pi_{V_{t}}F(h_{t})
is smaller than
\pi_{V_{t}}h_{t}
was in
X
:

\displaystyle\|\pi_{V_{t}}F(h_{t})\|_{X}

\displaystyle=\|\pi_{V_{t}}u_{t}\|_{X},

by (
B.2
),

\displaystyle\leq C\|u_{t}\|_{X},

using (
3
),

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\|\pi_{V_{t}}h_{t}\|_{X},

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:control u}}.

On the other hand, since
\pi_{W_{t}}F(h_{t})=\pi_{W_{t}}h_{t}+\pi_{W_{t}}u_{t}
,

\displaystyle\|\pi_{W_{t}}(F(h_{t})-h_{t})\|_{X}

\displaystyle=\|\pi_{W_{t}}u_{t}\|_{X},

by (
B.2
),

\displaystyle\leq C\|u_{t}\|_{X},

using (
3
),

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\|\pi_{V_{t}}h_{t}\|_{X},

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:control u}}.

Defining
h^{k+1}_{t}:=F(h^{k}_{t})
, we obtain a sequence of solutions to
\Box_{t}h_{t}^{k}-\psi_{t}\in V_{t}
satisfying
\|\pi_{V_{t}}h^{k+1}_{t}\|_{X}\leq C\varepsilon\|\pi_{V_{t}}h^{k}_{t}\|_{X}
and
\|\pi_{W_{t}}(h^{k+1}_{t}-h^{k}_{t})\|_{X}\leq C\varepsilon\|\pi_{V_{t}}h^{k}_{t}\|_{X}
. Choosing
\varepsilon>0
small enough depending on
C>0
implies that
(h_{t}^{k})_{k}
is a Cauchy sequence in the Banach space
X
. This fact alone not only gives the existence of a limit
h_{t}^{\infty}\in X
of the sequence
(h_{t}^{k})_{k}
in
X
but also guarantees that
h^{\infty}_{t}\in W_{t}
(since
\pi_{V_{t}}(h^{\infty}_{t})=0
) and
\Box_{t}h_{t}^{\infty}-\psi_{t}\in V_{t}
for each
t\in I
as desired.
∎

The next discussion justifies and checks the properties assumed in the previous Proposition
B.1
in the setting of the present paper.

(1)

Assumption (
1
) from Proposition
B.1
is straightforward by definition of the function spaces
X
and
Y
and that of the operator
P_{t}
.

(2)

Assumption (
2
) from Proposition
B.1
follows from standard existence and uniqueness results of solutions to inhomogeneous heat equations with initial condition in the setting of Hölder spaces which can be proved along the same lines as those of the proof of Proposition
6.1
. Notice that the constant
C
from the corresponding Schauder estimate may depend on
T^{\prime}
here as highlighted in Remark
B.2
.

(3)

Assumption (
3
) from Proposition
B.1
is slightly trickier to check as it is essentially equivalent to showing that the norms
\|\cdot\|_{X}
(respectively
\|\cdot\|_{Y}
) and
\|h_{t}\|^{*}_{X}:=\|\pi_{W_{t}}h_{t}\|_{X}+\|\pi_{V_{t}}h_{t}\|_{X}
(respectively
\|h_{t}\|^{*}_{Y}:=\|\pi_{W_{t}}h_{t}\|_{Y}+\|\pi_{V_{t}}h_{t}\|_{Y}
) are equivalent:

Lemma B.3
.

There exists
C>0
such that
\|h_{t}\|^{*}_{X}\leq C\|h_{t}\|_{X}
for all
(h_{t})_{t\in I}\in X
and
\|h_{t}\|^{*}_{Y}\leq C\|h_{t}\|_{Y}
for all
(h_{t})_{t\in I}\in Y
. Moreover, if
\pi_{V_{t}}h_{t}=:\sum_{i}L_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+M(t)\tilde{g}(t)+N(t)\tilde{c}(t)
then

\sum_{i}\|L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{X}+\|M\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{X}+\|N\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{X}\leq C\|h_{t}\|_{X},\quad(h_{t})_{t\in I}\in X,

for some uniform positive constant
C
. The same holds if
(h_{t})_{t\in I}\in Y
:

\sum_{i}\|L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{Y}+\|M\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{Y}+\|N\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{Y}\leq C\|h_{t}\|_{Y},\quad(h_{t})_{t\in I}\in Y.

Proof.

We give a proof for the space
Y=C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
only. The corresponding proof for the space
X
is analogous.

Thanks to [(
9.4
), Lemma
9.4
],

\begin{split}\sum_{i}&\|L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\|M\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\|N\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq\\
&C\Big(\sum_{i}\|\overline{L_{i}}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\|\overline{M}\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\|\overline{N}\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\Big),\end{split}

where the coefficients
\overline{L_{i}}
,
\overline{M}
and
\overline{N}
are defined by:

\overline{L_{i}}(t):=\frac{\langle h(t),\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}},\quad\overline{M}(t):=\frac{\langle h(t),\tilde{g}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}{\|\tilde{g}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}},\quad\overline{N}(t):=\frac{\langle h(t),\tilde{c}(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}{\|\tilde{c}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}}.

The following intermediate conclusion holds by invoking Lemmata
7.2
,
7.5
and
7.8
:

(B.4)

\begin{split}\sum_{i}&\|L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\|M\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}+\|N\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq C\|h\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.\end{split}

As for the estimate involving the semi-norm
[\,\cdot\,]_{\alpha,t,x}
, we apply Proposition
6.7
to
f:=L_{i}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}
and observe by the above estimate (
B.4
) that it suffices to estimate
[L_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}
from above. To do so, we consider difference quotients of [(
9.1
), Section
9.1
] to get:

(B.5)

\begin{pmatrix}[L_{1}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
[L_{2}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
\vdots\\
[M]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
[N]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\end{pmatrix}\leq G(t)^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\left[\overline{L_{1}}\cdot\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
\left[\overline{L_{2}}\cdot\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
\vdots\\
\left[\overline{M}\cdot\|\tilde{g}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
\left[\overline{N}\cdot\|\tilde{c}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\end{pmatrix}+G(t)^{-1}[G]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\begin{pmatrix}\sup_{t-\rho^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\,\leq\,t}|L_{1}(t^{\prime})|\\
\sup_{t-\rho^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\,\leq\,t}|L_{2}(t^{\prime})|\\
\vdots\\
\sup_{t-\rho^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\,\leq\,t}|M(t^{\prime})|\\
\sup_{t-\rho^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\,\leq\,t}|N(t^{\prime})|\end{pmatrix},

where the previous inequality is meant line by line and where the
[G]_{\alpha,t,\rho}
denotes the matrix whose coefficients are given by the semi-norm
[\,\cdot\,]_{\alpha,t,x}
of the coefficients of the Gram matrix
G
.

On the one hand, Lemmata
7.12
and
7.15
ensure with
\sigma+2
in lieu of
\sigma
that:

(B.6)

\begin{pmatrix}\left[\overline{L_{1}}\cdot\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
\left[\overline{L_{2}}\cdot\|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
\vdots\\
\left[\overline{M}\cdot\|\tilde{g}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
\left[\overline{N}\cdot\|\tilde{c}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{g})}\right]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\end{pmatrix}\leq\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\begin{pmatrix}\rho^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma-2\alpha}\\
\rho^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma-2\alpha}\\
\vdots\\
\vdots\\
1\\
1\end{pmatrix}.

On the other hand, Proposition
4.6
gives:

(B.7)

\begin{split}&[G]_{\alpha,t,\rho}=\\
&\rho^{2}\begin{pmatrix}O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})&O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4})&\dots&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}})&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})\\
O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-4})&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})&\dots&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}})&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}})&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{2^{+}})&\dots&O(\varepsilon(t)^{8}\delta(t)^{-8})&0\\
O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4})&\dots&0&O(\varepsilon(t)^{4-}\delta(t)^{-4^{-}})\end{pmatrix}.\end{split}

In particular, one can estimate as follows:

(B.8)

\begin{split}&G(t)^{-1}[G]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\begin{pmatrix}\sup_{t-\rho^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\,\leq\,t}|L_{1}(t^{\prime})|\\
\sup_{t-\rho^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\,\leq\,t}|L_{2}(t^{\prime})|\\
\vdots\\
\sup_{t-\rho^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\,\leq\,t}|M(t^{\prime})|\\
\sup_{t-\rho^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\,\leq\,t}|N(t^{\prime})|\end{pmatrix}=\rho^{2}\gamma(t)^{-1}\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\begin{pmatrix}O(\varepsilon(t)^{-2-\sigma})\\
O(\varepsilon(t)^{-2-\sigma})\\
\vdots\\
O(\varepsilon(t)^{2^{-}-\sigma}\delta(t)^{2^{+}})\\
O(\varepsilon(t)^{2-\sigma})\end{pmatrix}.\end{split}

Combining (
B.5
), (
B.6
) and (
B.8
) gives:

\begin{split}&\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}\rho^{-2+\sigma}[L_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\leq\\
&C\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}\left((\rho/\varepsilon(t))^{2\alpha}\varepsilon(t)^{-2-\sigma}+1\right)\rho^{-2+\sigma}\\
&+C\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\varepsilon(t)^{4}\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}\left(\rho^{2}\varepsilon(t)^{-2-\sigma}\right)\rho^{-2+\sigma}\\
&\leq C\|h\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}.\end{split}

The corresponding estimates for
\|M\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}
and
\|N\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}
can be proved along the same lines.
∎

(4)

Assumption (
4
) from Proposition
B.1
essentially follows from the estimates from Section
3
and the previous Lemma
B.3
:

Lemma B.4
.

There exists
\varepsilon(T)>0
independent of
T^{\prime}
such that for all
h\in X
,

\left\|\pi_{W_{t}}\left(\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda\right)\pi_{V_{t}}h_{t}\right)\right\|_{Y}\leq o_{T}(1)\|\pi_{V_{t}}h_{t}\|_{X},

where
\lim_{T\rightarrow-\infty}\varepsilon(T)=0.

Proof.

We first check that the following holds.

If
\pi_{V_{t}}h_{t}=:L_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)+M(t)\tilde{g}(t)+N(t)\tilde{c}(t)
then for each index
i
:

(B.9)

\begin{split}\left\|\pi_{W_{t}}\left(\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda\right)L_{i}(t)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\right)\right\|_{Y}&=\left\|L_{i}(t)\pi_{W_{t}}\left(\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}\right)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)\right)\right\|_{Y}\\
&\leq o_{T}(1)\|L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{X},\\
\left\|\pi_{W_{t}}\left(M(t)\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda\right)\tilde{g}(t)\right)\right\|_{Y}&=\left\|M(t)\pi_{W_{t}}\left(\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t)\right)\right\|_{Y}\\
&\leq o_{T}(1)\|M\cdot\tilde{g}\|_{X},\\
\left\|\pi_{W_{t}}\left(N(t)\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-2\Lambda\right)\tilde{c}(t)\right)\right\|_{Y}&=\left\|N(t)\pi_{W_{t}}\left(\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}(t)}-4\right)\tilde{c}(t)\right)\right\|_{Y}\\
&\leq o_{T}(1)\|N\cdot\tilde{c}\|_{X}.\end{split}

Let us prove the first estimate involving the tensor
\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)
.
By definition of
Y:=C^{0,\alpha}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}
, Lemma
3.10
and since
\delta<2/3
by (

H
0

),

\begin{split}\left\|L_{i}\cdot\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}}\right)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\right\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}&\leq C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)|L_{i}(t)|\left(\delta(t)^{2+\sigma}+\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{\sigma-4}\right)\\
&\leq C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\delta(t)^{2+\sigma}|L_{i}(t)|,\end{split}

for some uniform positive constant
C
. Moreover, according to Lemmata
D.4
,
D.6
, Lemma
3.10
and Propositions
3.14
and
3.15
,

(B.10)

\begin{split}&\sup_{M\times(-\infty,T]}\gamma(t)\rho(x,t)^{2+\sigma}[L_{i}\cdot\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}}\right)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}]_{\alpha,x,t}\\
&\leq C\sup_{M\times(-\infty,T]}\gamma(t)\rho(x,t)^{2+\sigma}\left(\sup_{P_{\rho}(x,t)}[L_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}|\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}}\right)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\right)\\
&+\sup_{M\times(-\infty,T]}\gamma(t)\rho(x,t)^{2+\sigma}\left(\sup_{t-\rho(x,t)^{2}\,\leq\,s\,\leq\,t}|L_{i}(s)|[\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}}\right)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}]_{\alpha,x,t}\right)\\
&\leq C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}\left(\varepsilon(t)^{4}\rho^{-2+\sigma}+\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4+\sigma}\right)[L_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
&\quad+C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}\left(\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}-2\alpha}\rho^{-2^{-}+\sigma+2\alpha}+\varepsilon(t)^{4^{-}}\delta(t)^{-2^{-}+\sigma}\right)\sup_{t-\rho(x,t)^{2}\,\leq\,s\,\leq\,t}|L_{i}(s)|\\
&\leq C\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{4}\delta(t)^{-4+\sigma}\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}[L_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
&\quad+C\left(\varepsilon(T)^{2}+\varepsilon(T)^{4^{-}-\sigma}\delta(T)^{-2^{-}+\sigma}\right)\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{\sigma}|L_{i}(t)|,\end{split}

where we have used that
\delta<2/3<1
in the penultimate line together with
(4-\sigma)\delta>2-\sigma
since
\delta>1/2
thanks to (

H
0

) in the last line.

Now,

(B.11)

\|L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{X}\geq\|L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma,T}}\geq C^{-1}\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{\sigma}|L_{i}(t)|,

where we have chosen a point in
(N,g_{\zeta(t)})
such that
|\mathbf{o}_{i}(\zeta(t))|_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\geq C^{-1}
uniformly in time at that point. In particular, we get as an intermediate result:

(B.12)

\left\|L_{i}\cdot\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}}\right)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\right\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma,\sigma+2,T}}\leq C\varepsilon(T)^{\delta(2+\sigma)-\sigma}\|L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{X}\leq o_{T}(1)\|L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{X},

where we have used that
\delta(2+\sigma)-\sigma>0
thanks to (

H
0

).

Now,

\begin{split}\|L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{X}&\geq\sup_{M\times(-\infty,T]}\gamma(t)\rho(x,t)^{\sigma}[L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}]_{\alpha,x,t}\geq\sup_{M\times(-\infty,T]}\gamma(t)\rho(x,t)^{\sigma}[L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\\
&\geq\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\sup_{M}\rho^{\sigma}[L_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}|\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}(t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}-\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\sup_{M}\rho^{\sigma}[\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\sup_{t-\rho^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|L_{i}(t^{\prime})|\\
&\geq C^{-1}\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{\sigma}\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}[L_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}-\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\sup_{M}\rho^{\sigma}[\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\sup_{t-\rho^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|L_{i}(t^{\prime})|\\
&\geq C^{-1}\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{\sigma}\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}[L_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}-C\varepsilon(T)^{2+\sigma}\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{\sigma}|L_{i}(t)|\\
&\geq C^{-1}\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{\sigma}\sup_{\rho\,\leq\,2\delta(t)}[L_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}-C\varepsilon(T)^{2+\sigma}\|L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{X}.\end{split}

Here we have invoked [(
3.11
), proof of Proposition
3.13
] in the penultimate line and (
B.11
) in the last line.

In particular, we get:

\begin{split}\|L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{X}&\geq C^{-1}\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{\sigma}\sup_{M}[L_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}.\end{split}

Thanks to (
B.10
), we therefore obtain in combination with the previous lower bound on
\|L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{X}
:

(B.13)

\begin{split}\sup_{M\times(-\infty,T]}&\gamma(t)\rho(x,t)^{2+\sigma}[L_{i}\cdot\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L,\tilde{g}}\right)\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}]_{\alpha,x,t}\leq\\
&C\varepsilon(T)^{4-\sigma}\delta(T)^{-4+\sigma}\sup_{t\,\leq\,T}\gamma(t)\varepsilon(t)^{\sigma}\sup_{M}[L_{i}]_{\alpha,t,\rho}+o_{T}(1)\|L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{X}\\
&\leq o_{T}(1)\|L_{i}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{o}}_{i}\|_{X}.\end{split}

Estimates (
B.12
) and (
B.13
) give the first desired estimate of (
B.9
).

The proof of the remaining estimates follow the same lines.

On the basis of (
B.9
), one concludes thanks to Lemma
B.3
and the triangular inequality for the norm
\|\cdot\|_{Y}
.

∎

Appendix C
Some nonlinear estimates

Lemma C.1
.

Let
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
be two smooth Riemannian metrics on a smooth manifold
M
. Then:

\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{2})-\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{1})=g_{2}^{-1}\ast\nabla^{g_{1},2}(g_{2}-g_{1})+g_{2}^{-1}\ast g_{2}^{-1}\ast\nabla^{g_{1}}(g_{2}-g_{1})\ast\nabla^{g_{1}}(g_{2}-g_{1}),

if
\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{i})
,
i=1,2
, is interpreted as a
(3,1)
-tensor.

A proof of this lemma can be found in [Chapter 2,
[
CLN06
]
].

Lemma C.2
.

For
g
a Riemannian metric and
k
a symmetric
2
-tensor such that
|k|_{g}\leq 1/2
, defined as in (
8.8
):

\begin{split}Q_{g}(k)&=g^{-1}\ast k\ast\nabla^{g,\,2}k+g^{-1}\ast g^{-1}\ast\nabla^{g}k\ast\nabla^{g}k+g^{-1}\ast\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g)\ast k\ast k.\end{split}

Then one has:

(C.1)

|Q_{g}(k)|_{g}\leq C\left(|k|_{g}|\nabla^{g,2}k|_{g}+|\nabla^{g}k|^{2}_{g}+|\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g)|_{g}|k|^{2}_{g}\right).

Moreover, for a couple
(g_{i},k_{i})
,
i=1,2
as above, then:

(C.2)

\begin{split}Q_{g_{2}}(k_{2})-Q_{g_{1}}(k_{1})&=g_{2}^{-1}\ast(g_{2}-g_{1})\ast g_{1}^{-1}\ast k_{2}\ast\nabla^{g_{2},2}k_{2}+g_{1}^{-1}\ast(k_{2}-k_{1})\ast\nabla^{g_{2},2}k_{2}\\
&\quad+g_{1}^{-1}\ast k_{1}\ast\nabla^{g_{1},2}(k_{2}-k_{1})\\
&\quad+g_{1}^{-1}\ast k_{1}^{*2}\ast\left[g_{2}^{-1}\ast\nabla^{g_{1},2}(g_{2}-g_{1})+g_{2}^{-1}\ast g_{2}^{-1}\ast\nabla^{g_{1}}(g_{2}-g_{1})^{*2}\right]\\
&\quad+g_{1}^{-1}\ast g_{2}^{-1}\ast\nabla^{g_{1}}(g_{2}-g_{1})\ast\nabla^{g_{1}}k_{1}\ast k_{1}\\
&\quad+g_{2}^{-1}\ast(g_{2}-g_{1})\ast g_{1}^{-1}\ast\left(g_{2}^{-1}+g_{1}^{-1}\right)\ast\nabla^{g_{2}}k_{2}\ast\nabla^{g_{2}}k_{2}\\
&\quad+g_{1}^{-1}\ast g_{1}^{-1}\ast g_{2}^{-1}\ast\nabla^{g_{1}}(g_{2}-g_{1})\ast k_{2}\ast(\nabla^{g_{2}}k_{2}+\nabla^{g_{1}}k_{2})\\
&\quad+g_{1}^{-1}\ast g_{1}^{-1}\ast\nabla^{g_{1}}(k_{2}-k_{1})\ast\nabla^{g_{1}}(k_{2}+k_{1})\\
&\quad+\left(g_{2}^{-1}\ast(g_{2}-g_{1})\ast g_{1}^{-1}\ast\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{2})+g_{1}^{-1}\ast g_{2}^{-1}\ast\nabla^{g_{1},2}(g_{2}-g_{1})\right)\ast k_{2}\ast k_{2}\\
&\quad+g_{1}^{-1}\ast g_{2}^{-1}\ast g_{2}^{-1}\ast\nabla^{g_{1}}(g_{2}-g_{1})\ast\nabla^{g_{1}}(g_{2}-g_{1})\ast k_{2}\ast k_{2}\\
&\quad+g_{1}^{-1}\ast\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{1})\ast(k_{2}-k_{1})\ast(k_{2}+k_{1}).\end{split}

In particular, if
|g_{2}-g_{1}|_{g_{1}}\leq 1/2
then there exists
C>0
such that:

\begin{split}&|Q_{g_{2}}(k_{2})-Q_{g_{1}}(k_{1})|_{g_{1}}\leq|k_{2}-k_{1}|_{g_{1}}\left(|\nabla^{g_{2},2}k_{2}|_{g_{1}}+|\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{1})|_{g_{1}}(|k_{2}|_{g_{1}}+|k_{1}|_{g_{1}})\right)\\
&+|\nabla^{g_{1}}(k_{2}-k_{1})|_{g_{1}}|\nabla^{g_{1}}(k_{2}+k_{1})|_{g_{1}}+|k_{1}|_{g_{1}}|\nabla^{g_{1},2}(k_{2}-k_{1})|_{g_{1}}\\
&+\left(|\nabla^{g_{1},2}(g_{2}-g_{1})|_{g_{1}}+|\nabla^{g_{1}}(g_{2}-g_{1})|_{g_{1}}^{2}+\left(|\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{1})|_{g_{1}}+|\mathop{\rm Rm}\nolimits(g_{2})|_{g_{2}}\right)|g_{2}-g_{1}|_{g_{1}}\right)\left(|k_{1}|^{2}_{g_{1}}+|k_{2}|^{2}_{g_{1}}\right)\\
&+|\nabla^{g_{1}}(g_{2}-g_{1})|_{g_{1}}\left(|\nabla^{g_{1}}k_{1}|_{g_{1}}+|\nabla^{g_{1}}k_{2}|_{g_{1}}\right)(|k_{1}|_{g_{1}}+|k_{2}|_{g_{1}})\\
&+|g_{2}-g_{1}|_{g_{1}}\left(|\nabla^{g_{2}}k_{2}|_{g_{1}}^{2}+|k_{2}|_{g_{1}}|\nabla^{g_{2},2}k_{2}|_{g_{1}}\right).\end{split}

We end this section by recalling how to compare scalar products of two tensors defined with respect to two equivalent Riemannian metrics:

Lemma C.3
.

If
S
and
T
are two tensors of the same type on
M^{n}
and if
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
are two Riemannian metrics such that
|g_{2}-g_{1}|_{g_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{2}
then there exists a constant
c(n)>0
such that:

\left|\langle S,T\rangle_{g_{2}}-\langle S,T\rangle_{g_{1}}\right|\leq c(n)|g_{2}-g_{1}|_{g_{1}}|S|_{g_{1}}|T|_{g_{1}},

and,

\left|\langle S,T\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{2})}-\langle S,T\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{1})}\right|\leq c(n)\int_{M}|g_{2}-g_{1}|_{g_{1}}|S|_{g_{1}}|T|_{g_{1}}\,d\mu_{g_{1}}.

We end with the following straightforward but crucial lemma:

Lemma C.4
.

Let
(M,g(t))_{t}
be a one-parameter
C^{1}
family of
C^{2}
Riemannian metrics on a closed manifold
M
and let
(S(t))_{t}
and
(T(t))_{t}
be two families of
C^{2}
tensors on
M
. Then:

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}&\langle S(t),T(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(g(t))}=\int_{M}\left\langle\partial_{t}S(t),T(t)\right\rangle_{g(t)}\,d\mu_{g(t)}\\
&+\int_{M}\left\langle S(t),\left(\partial_{t}+\frac{\mathop{\rm tr}\nolimits_{g(t)}\partial_{t}g(t)}{2}\operatorname{Id}-\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits(\partial_{t}g(t)\circ\cdot)\right)T(t)\right\rangle_{g(t)}\,d\mu_{g(t)},\end{split}

where in coordinates, one has
\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits(a\circ b)_{ij}=g^{pq}a_{ip}b_{qj}+g^{pq}b_{ip}a_{qj}
.

Appendix D
Hölder estimates

Definition D.1
.

Let
f\in(-\infty,T)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a function and let
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
. The Hölder semi-norm
[f]_{\alpha,t,r}
is defined as:

[f]_{\alpha,t,r}:=r^{2\alpha}\sup_{t^{\prime}\neq t\,,\,t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}\frac{|f(t^{\prime})-f(t)|}{|t^{\prime}-t|^{\alpha}},\quad r\in\left(\inf_{x\in M}\rho_{0}(x,t),\sup_{x\in M}\rho_{0}(x,t)\right),

where
\rho_{0}(x,t):=\iota_{0}(\varepsilon(t)+r_{o}(x))
as defined in Section
6.1
.

A straightforward application of the mean value theorem gives:

Lemma D.2
.

Let
f\in(-\infty,T)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a
C^{1}
function and let
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
. Then for
r\in(\inf_{M}\rho_{0}(t),\sup_{M}\rho_{0}(t))
,

[f]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq r^{2}\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,\tau\,\leq\,t}|f^{\prime}(\tau)|.

Observe also that we have the weighted interpolation:

[f]_{\alpha,t,r}\leq\left(2\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|f(t^{\prime})|\right)^{1-\alpha}\left(r^{2}\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,\tau\,\leq\,t}|f^{\prime}(\tau)|\right)^{\alpha}.

Definition D.3
.

We define similarly a Hölder semi-norm at time
t\leq T
for tensors at a point
x\in M
:

[h]_{\alpha,t,r}(x):=r^{2\alpha}\sup_{t^{\prime}\neq t\,,\,t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}\frac{|h(x,t^{\prime})-h(x,t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}}{|t^{\prime}-t|^{\alpha}}.

For ease of reading, we will omit the dependency on
x
from now on.
Observe that if
h
is time-differentiable then:

(D.1)

[h]_{\alpha,t,r}(x)\leq r^{2}\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,\tau\,\leq\,t}|\partial_{t}h(x,\tau)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}.

We also compare the semi-norm
[\,\cdot\,]_{\alpha,t,r}
for different scales:

Lemma D.4
.

For
r>0
and
\rho>0
,
t\leq T
and a tensor
S
on
M\times(-\infty,T]
,

[S]_{\alpha,t,r}(x)\leq\max\left\{1\,;\,\left(\frac{r}{\rho}\right)^{2\alpha}\right\}\left(2\sup_{t-r^{2}\leq\,s\,\leq\,t}|S(x,s)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}+[S]_{\alpha,t,\rho}(x)\right).

Proof.

If
r\leq\rho
then
[S]_{\alpha,t,r}(x)\leq[S]_{\alpha,t,\rho}(x)
by definition of the semi-norm
[\,\cdot\,]_{\alpha,t,r}
. If
r>\rho
, let us split the supremum over
t^{\prime}\in(t-r^{2},t-\rho^{2}]
and
(t-\rho^{2},t]
and write:

\begin{split}[S]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq r^{2\alpha}\sup_{t^{\prime}\in(t-\rho^{2},\,t]}\frac{|S(x,t^{\prime})-S(x,t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}}{|t^{\prime}-t|^{\alpha}}+r^{2\alpha}\sup_{t^{\prime}\in(t-r^{2},\,t-\rho^{2}]}\frac{|S(x,t^{\prime})-S(x,t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}}{|t^{\prime}-t|^{\alpha}}\\
&\leq\left(\frac{r}{\rho}\right)^{2\alpha}[S]_{\alpha,t,\rho}(x)+r^{2\alpha}\sup_{t^{\prime}\in(t-r^{2},\,t-\rho^{2}]}\frac{|S(x,t^{\prime})-S(x,t)|_{\tilde{g}(t)}}{\rho^{2\alpha}},\end{split}

which leads to the desired estimate by invoking the triangular inequality in the last line.

∎

We gather some results on the use of the previously defined Hölder semi-norms
[\,\cdot\,]_{\alpha,t,r}
whose proofs are omitted.

Lemma D.5
.

There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
,

\begin{split}[S\,\ast\,T]_{\alpha,t,r}(x)&\leq C[S]_{\alpha,t,r}(x)\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|T(x,t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}+C\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|S(x,t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}[T]_{\alpha,t,r}(x)\\
&\quad+Cr^{2}\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(x,t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}|S(x,t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}|T(x,t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}.\end{split}

The last term on the righthand side of Lemma
D.5
will turn out to be negligible in our estimates.

Let us estimate the
[\,\cdot\,]_{\alpha,x,t}
-semi-norm (introduced in Section
6.1
) of a product
\lambda\cdot T
in terms of the
[\,\cdot\,]_{\alpha,t,r}
-semi-norm of time-dependent function
\lambda
and the
[\,\cdot\,]_{\alpha,x,t}
-semi-norm of a tensor
T
:

Lemma D.6
.

There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
,
\lambda:(-\infty,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
S
a tensor on
M\times(-\infty,T]
:

\begin{split}[\lambda\cdot S]_{\alpha,x,t}\leq C[\lambda]_{\alpha,t,\rho}\sup_{P_{\rho}(x,t)}|S|_{\tilde{g}(t)}+C\sup_{t-\rho_{0}(x,t)^{2}\,\leq\,s\,\leq\,t}|\lambda(s)|[S]_{\alpha,x,t}.\end{split}

Observe the following straightforward interpolation lemma:

Lemma D.7
.

There exists
C>0
such that for
t\leq T
and any
C^{1}_{loc}
-tensor
S
on
M\times(-\infty,T]
,

[S]_{\alpha,x,t}\leq C\rho(x,t)\sup_{P_{\rho}(x,t)}|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}S|_{\tilde{g}(t)}+C\rho(x,t)^{2}\sup_{P_{\rho}(x,t)}|\partial_{\tau}S|_{\tilde{g}(t)}.

The semi-norm
[h]_{\alpha,t,r}
also shows up naturally when one estimates a Hölder semi-norm of
L^{2}
-scalar products:

Lemma D.8
.

If
S(t)
and
T(t)
are two tensors of the same type for each
t\leq T
then there exists
C>0
such that for each
t\leq T
and
r>0
:

\begin{split}\left[\langle S(\cdot),T(\cdot)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(\cdot))}\right]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq C\int_{M}[S]_{\alpha,t,r}\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|T(t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}+\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|S(t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}[T]_{\alpha,t,r}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&\quad+C\int_{M}r^{2}\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}|S(t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}|T(t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}.\end{split}

The next lemma estimates the Hölder semi-norm
[\cdot]_{\alpha,t,r}
of the first and second covariant derivatives of time-dependent tensor along a one-parameter family of metrics
\tilde{g}(t)
.

Lemma D.9
.

Let
(T(t))_{t\in I}
be a one-parameter family of tensors on
M
. There exists
C
such that for
[t-r^{2},t]\subset I
,

\begin{split}[\nabla^{\tilde{g}}T]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq Cr^{2}\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}|T(t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&\quad+r^{2\alpha}\sup_{t^{\prime}\neq t\,,\,t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}\frac{|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}(T(t^{\prime})-T(t))|_{\tilde{g}(t)}}{|t^{\prime}-t|^{\alpha}}.\end{split}

Moreover,

\begin{split}[\nabla^{\tilde{g},2}T]_{\alpha,t,r}&\leq Cr^{2}\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),2}\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}|T(t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&\quad+Cr^{4}\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t^{\prime})|^{2}_{\tilde{g}(t)}|T(t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&\quad+Cr^{2}\sup_{t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}\partial_{t}\tilde{g}(t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t)}T(t^{\prime})|_{\tilde{g}(t)}\\
&\quad+r^{2\alpha}\sup_{t^{\prime}\neq t\,,\,t-r^{2}\,\leq\,t^{\prime}\,\leq\,t}\frac{|\nabla^{\tilde{g}(t),2}(T(t^{\prime})-T(t))|_{\tilde{g}(t)}}{|t^{\prime}-t|^{\alpha}}.\end{split}

Finally, the following lemma estimates the difference of the scalar products of localized and unlocalized tensors on the bubble:

Lemma D.10
.

Let
S(t)
and
T(t)
two tensors on
M
of the same type for each
t\leq T\leq 0
. Then,

\begin{split}&\langle\chi_{\delta(t)}S(t),\chi_{\delta(t)}T(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\tilde{g}(t))}-\langle S(t),T(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}=\left\langle(1-\chi_{\delta(t)}^{2})S(t),T(t)\right\rangle_{L^{2}(g_{\zeta(t)})}\\
&+\int_{M}\left(\left\langle\chi_{\delta(t)}S(t),\chi_{\delta(t)}T(t)\right\rangle_{\tilde{g}(t)}\frac{d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}}{d\mu_{g_{\zeta(t)}}}-\left\langle\chi_{\delta(t)}S(t),\chi_{\delta(t)}T(t)\right\rangle_{g_{\zeta(t)}}\right)\,d\mu_{\tilde{g}(t)}.\end{split}

Meaning
